# Teams and Workflows, Plan B (Core 2: Delegate, Send-backs, Branches, Deploy, Schedule and Webhook) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** A workflow can branch by an agent's outcome or by the project, let an agent start other members, send work back to an earlier step within a cap, deploy the branches the person approved (push, pull request, auto-merge, never a secret), and start on a schedule or from a webhook; and plan A's findings parked for plan B are fixed.
**Architecture:** The definition (`workflow.rs`) becomes a tree: a branch step holds paths of steps, and `workflows.rs` moves by step id (`first`, `next_after`, `before`), taking the path its branch chose. Slow work (Dots' commit for a hand-off, a deploy) runs as jobs outside `Workflows::lock` (`workflows/jobs.rs`) and writes only while its step is still open. An agent step's runs get `report_outcome`, `send_back` and `start_member` by what the step declares (`team_tools.rs`, both engines, the one policy name `Workflow`). A delegate step's members are dot runs kept in `workflow_step_members`; the lead is continued with their results (`workflows/delegate.rs`). A send-back gives the target a new attempt and continues its last session, within `max_send_backs` (`workflows/send_back.rs`). A deploy acts on the tips the check approved: it refuses secrets and moved branches, pushes the approved commit and runs `gh` (`deploy.rs`, `workflows/deploy_step.rs`). The `Scheduler` pass also fires scheduled workflows (`scheduler/workflow_slots.rs`), and `POST /workflows/{id}/trigger` starts one from a webhook (`server/workflow_webhook.rs`). One migration, `0027_teams_b.sql`.
**Tech Stack:** Rust (sqlx SQLite, tokio, axum for the webhook route), git and the GitHub CLI `gh` run as child processes, a `fake-gh` test binary. No new dependency. No app, contract or UI change.
**Spec:** docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-teams-workflows-design.md (sections 5.1, 5.3 to 5.6, 6.2, 7 and 9 for the parts listed under "Scope"; Plan A did the rest of the core, Plan C does the UI)

## Scope

This is the second of three plans for the spec:

- **Plan A (merged):** teams, members, projects, workflows, workflow runs and step runs; `manual` and `chat` triggers; agent steps; checks (approve, stop); budget and clock; hand-offs, start branch and Dots' commit; `start_workflow`; the runner's workspace, exemption, restart and cancellation.
- **Plan B (this one):** 5.3 delegate steps and `start_member`; the send-back (5.4, the person's choice: resume where it left off) with its cap (9), `send_back` and `report_outcome` (7); 5.5 deploy; 5.6 branch on outcome or project; 6.2; `schedule` and `webhook` triggers (5.1). Also plan A's findings parked for plan B, and the Stop race found in Task 1.
- **Plan C:** section 10 and the app commands of 11: Teams in the rail, the builder, the run page, the chat card, Needs you, notifications, the Inbox. It also adds `Workflow`, `WorkflowRun`, `StepRun` and `Handoff` to the UI's types and the contract, the workflow webhook URL and a command for `Teams::regenerate_workflow_token`.

- The person's decisions (2026-11-02):
  - One plan B, not split.
  - Deploy `pr_merge` runs `gh pr merge <url> --auto --<method>`. The step succeeds once auto-merge is set. A repo with auto-merge turned off fails the step with gh's message.
  - A `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` deploy refuses secrets. Before anything is pushed, the files each approved branch changed since its base are checked. A name `.env`, `.env.*` (but not `.env.example`, `.env.sample`, `.env.template`), `*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, `id_rsa*`, `id_dsa*`, `id_ecdsa*`, `id_ed25519*`, `.netrc`, `.npmrc`, `.pypirc` or `credentials.json`, or a file over 10 MiB (10 × 1024 × 1024 bytes), fails the deploy, naming the files.

Task 11 also fixes the Stop race found in Task 1.

  - The new refusals of `workflow::Definition::validate` (Task 1); `the trigger's schedule: <message>` also on save (Task 9).
  - The tools' answers: `Outcome recorded.`; `"<x>" is not an outcome of this step; use one of: "a", "b".`; `Sent back to "<step>". End your turn now: the work goes back when you finish.`; `You can send work back only to: "<a>", "<b>".`; `Give a note of 1 to 8,000 characters that says what to change.`; `Started "<member>".`; `"<m>" is not a member you may start; you may start: "<a>".`; `Give a task of 1 to 8,000 characters that says what the member should do.`; `Not started: you have started <total> members, the most this step allows.`; `Not started: <n> members you started are still running, and at most <at_once> may run at once. End your turn; you will be told when they finish.`; `Not started: Budget reached: <limit>.`; `Not started: <dot> is disabled.`; `This step of the workflow has ended, so nothing was recorded.` (`STEP_CLOSED`).

  - Send-backs: `The person sent this back: <note>`; `The step "<sender>" sent this back: <note>`; `Work was sent back to "<target>" and has run again. Its new hand-off:`; `Your branch still has your earlier commits: merge "<b>" into it before you go on.`; `Update your work to match, then end with a new summary.`; `Send-back limit reached: <n> of <max>`; the notices `Started again from "<branch>": the earlier session could not be resumed.` and `Started again from the start: the earlier session could not be resumed.`; `workflow run <id> is not waiting at its send-back limit`; `the note must be 1 to 8,000 characters`; `the check "<check>" has no agent step before it to send the work back to`.

Remove them from the branch, then retry the step.`; `<tool> failed: <stderr>`; `<tool> did not finish within 5 minutes.`; `gh pr create printed no pull request URL.`; `the check before it recorded no approved branches`; `the project's default branch could not be found; set it on the project`; `interrupted`.

- Names: tools `start_member`, `report_outcome`, `send_back` (Claude: `mcp__dots__<name>`), all under the policy name `Workflow`; step kinds `agent`, `check`, `deploy` (modes `branch`, `push`, `pr`, `pr_merge`, `member`), `branch` (`on`: `outcome`, `project`); trigger kinds `schedule`, `webhook`; route `POST /workflows/{id}/trigger`; the bin `fake-gh` (`FAKE_GH_LOG`, `FAKE_GH_MODE`).
- Shared files get the smallest edits: in the core `model.rs` (appended), `lib.rs` and `store/mod.rs` (modules), `runner.rs`, `runtime.rs` (two lines), `checkins.rs`, `policy.rs` (a doc comment), `run_tools.rs`, `scheduler.rs`, `server/mod.rs` (the route), `server/mcp.rs`, `store/teams.rs`, `store/workflow_runs.rs`, `engine/{claude/args.rs,local/mcp_tools.rs,local/mod.rs,local/tools/mod.rs,local/tools/team.rs}`. New code goes in new files: the child modules `workflows/{jobs,tools,delegate,send_back,deploy_step}.rs`, `scheduler/workflow_slots.rs`, and `deploy.rs`, `store/{step_members,workflow_schedules}.rs`, `server/workflow_webhook.rs`, `bin/fake-gh.rs`.
- How the code blocks are written. A step changes files in two ways, applied in the order given:
  - **Create `path`:** the block is the whole new file.
  - **In `path`, replace:** … **with:** … The first block occurs in the file exactly once, as it stands at that moment; the second takes its place.
- A Replace whose passage is not found once (someone changed it): stop, read the file, and apply the same change to the passage as it is now; never paste over their change. The working copy has CRLF line ends (`core.autocrlf=true`): a passage is matched with line ends ignored, and a file written here is LF with a final newline (git normalises it).
- Before each commit: the task's own tests; then `cargo test -p dots-core --no-fail-fast -j 2 -- --test-threads 4` (the whole core) for Tasks 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9 and 11 (they change shared paths), the touched binaries for the others; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -j 2` (no new warning) and `cargo fmt --check`.
- Known failures, not caused here: under load `browser_host_test` and `browser_call_test` can fail (rerun alone); until Task 11, `workflows_test::stopping_cancels_its_runs_and_a_cancelled_run_fails_its_step` can fail under load (rerun alone; Task 11 fixes it); until Task 11, `runner_workflow_test` can time out when git is slow (rerun alone).

## Review Focus

The inputs and failure modes the spec implies that are most likely to bite a person, each pinned to the test that covers it:

1. **A secret or a huge file on a branch being deployed.** An agent leaves `.env`, `config/id_rsa`, `x.PEM` or a file over 10 MiB on the approved branch of a `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` deploy. Expected: nothing is pushed and no gh runs; the step fails naming the files (ten, then "and <n> more"); `.env.example`, `docs/.ENV.sample` and exactly 10 MiB pass; a secret added and then deleted on the branch passes. Tests: `deploy_test::files_that_look_like_secrets_or_are_too_big_refuse_the_deploy`, `a_refusal_names_ten_files_and_counts_the_rest`, `a_secret_added_and_then_deleted_on_the_branch_is_not_refused` (Task 7); `workflows_test::a_deploy_refuses_a_branch_that_holds_a_secret` (Task 8).
2. **A reviewer and a builder that keep sending work back.** Expected: past `max_send_backs` nothing is performed; the run waits on `send_backs` with `Send-back limit reached: 3 of 3` and the held note; Approve is refused; "allow one more" raises the limit by one and performs it, once; a send-back past the runs limit waits on budget instead. Tests: `workflows_test::past_the_cap_a_send_back_waits_until_the_person_allows_one_more`, `a_send_back_past_the_runs_limit_waits_on_budget_then_goes_on` (Task 6).
3. **Dots closed in the middle of a deploy, a send-back, a delegate step or a hand-off.** Expected: a deploy cut off fails `interrupted` and pushes nothing until the person retries; a send-back cut off is finished once by the next pass; a member cut off is reported as failed and the lead is continued once; a lost hand-off is built again. Tests: `workflows_test::a_deploy_cut_off_by_a_restart_fails_interrupted_and_is_not_pushed_until_retried` (Task 8), `a_send_back_cut_off_by_a_restart_is_finished_by_the_next_pass_once` (Task 6), `a_member_interrupted_by_a_restart_is_reported_as_failed_and_the_lead_goes_on` (Task 5), `a_lost_hand_off_is_built_again_by_a_later_pass` (Task 3).
4. **A scheduled workflow while Dots was closed, or while the last run still waits at its check.** Expected: slots missed while closed never start late (a slot under 60 s late still starts); a slot that comes due while any run of the workflow is in flight, a check included, is skipped and not counted; the next slot after that run ended starts. Tests: `workflow_schedule_test::slots_missed_while_dots_was_closed_never_start_late`, `a_slot_while_a_run_is_in_flight_is_skipped_and_the_next_one_starts_after_it_ended` (Task 9).
5. **The branch moves after the person approved it.** A commit lands on the approved branch, or the branch is deleted, before the deploy runs. Expected: `Ship: The branch changed after it was approved.`, nothing pushed; with the branch put back, Retry ships the approved commit. Tests: `deploy_test::a_branch_moved_after_approval_is_refused_and_nothing_is_pushed` (Task 7), `workflows_test::a_branch_changed_after_the_approval_fails_the_deploy_until_it_is_put_back` (Task 8).

---

## Context

Plan A left a workflow as a flat list of agent steps and checks (`workflow.rs`), stored per team (`store/teams.rs`), run by the loop `workflows::Workflows`: each pass reads the workflow runs in flight and moves each one under `Workflows::lock` (queue the member's run with a `## Workflow` section, watch it, build the hand-off with Dots' commit, wait at a check or a limit). `start_workflow` is served by `CheckIns` on both engines under the policy name `Workflow` (`team_tools.rs`, `run_tools.rs`, `server/mcp.rs`, `engine/local/tools/team.rs`). The `Scheduler` fires the dots' schedules from `schedule_state`, and `server/webhook.rs` starts a dot from `POST /dots/{id}/trigger`. Plan A's "Deviations from the spec" table still holds.

Task 1 is the definition's new kinds, navigation by id and their rules; Task 2 movement by id and branch steps; Task 3 hand-offs built as jobs outside the loop's lock; Task 4 `report_outcome` and `send_back` on both engines; Task 5 delegate steps and `start_member`; Task 6 send-backs and their cap; Task 7 `deploy.rs` and `fake-gh`; Task 8 deploy steps; Task 9 the schedule trigger; Task 10 the webhook trigger; Task 11 late questions and follow-ups, branch names, and the Stop race.

**How this plan's code was written:** every block below was generated from a prototype, one commit per task, on branch `proto/teams-b` (`.claude/worktrees/proto-teams-b`, based on `d69a5a55`): Task 1 `81599485`, Task 2 `e5b50395`, Task 3 `d40a1ac3`, Task 4 `0634155b`, Task 5 `4864db67`, Task 6 `cb839a80`, Task 7 `58cc9d78`, Task 8 `aa71669c`, Task 9 `08ca0b4b`, Task 10 `6b987c53`, Task 11 `806efdf9`. Each commit is `cargo fmt` clean, and at each one the prototype's `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` gave no warning. In the prototype, `cargo test -p dots-core --no-fail-fast` ran 1992 tests before this plan and 2094 at Task 11, all passing. The blocks were then applied in order, by a script that follows the headings above, to a fresh checkout of `d69a5a55`, and each task's tree matched its commit.
The replay (`python replay.py <plan> <checkout> <proto> T1=… T11=…`, 2026-11-03) reported "matches" for all 11 tasks: after each task the staged tree equalled its prototype commit.
Step 2 of each task fails to compile (a new module, type, field or method the test uses does not exist yet) unless it says otherwise.

## Deviations from the spec

| Spec | This plan | Why |
|---|---|---|
| §6, §6.2 the hand-offs "of every earlier step", the branches "on the path taken" | `ran_before(wr, id)`: the steps before a step on its path and, after each earlier branch, the steps of the path it took (recursively). A step's hand-offs, its start branch, what a check shows and the default send-back target follow it. A path not taken is never read | `before()` alone leaves out the taken path's steps, so the step after a branch would lose that path's work |
| §5.4 a check's `send_back_to` default "the step before" | The nearest agent step in `ran_before(check)` | It is the step whose hand-off the check showed, so the note belongs there |
| §5.8 "a send-back target that is not earlier in the list" | Only an agent step in `before(step)`; one inside an earlier branch's path is refused. A check with no agent step before it is saved, and its send-back is refused at run time | A target must have run on every path to the sender |
| §5.8 "a deploy not preceded by a check on every path" | Refused unless a check, or an earlier branch whose every path and `otherwise` passes one at any depth, runs before it on its path. An empty `otherwise` passes no check | A check after the deploy, on the deploy's own path, does not count |
| §5.8 refusals | Also refused: a delegate with an empty `may_start`; a branch with no paths; a path with a blank or repeated label (labels compared without case); an outcome listed twice (compared exactly). `start_member` also refuses a blank task and a disabled member: `Not started: <dot> is disabled.` | Each would fail later with no clearer message |
| §5.5 deploy fields | `mode` and its fields are flattened into the step: `{"kind": "deploy", "mode": "pr_merge", "merge_method": "squash"}` | One level, as the builder shows it |
| §5.6 "the outcome label the previous agent step reported" | The nearest agent step in `before(branch)`; its newest succeeded run's `outcome`. Labels are trimmed and matched exactly; `report_outcome` takes the exact label, else the one label equal without case, and stores the declared label. No outcome reported takes `otherwise`. A step run again after a send-back starts with no outcome | Validation and the run use one source; an old outcome could take a path the agent no longer means |
| §5.6 a branch's choice | Its step run is created `Running`, then succeeded with `decision: {"path": "<label>" \| null}` in one write; a pass that finds it `Running` decides it on the same step run | A step run that read succeeded without its choice would send the next pass down `otherwise` |
| §6.1 Dots' commit before the hand-off | Built by a job outside `Workflows::lock`; under the lock it writes only if the run is `Running` at that step, the step run is `Running` and the step's runs are unchanged. A Stop meanwhile wins; a job lost to a restart is built again by a later pass | Plan A's parked finding: the commit held the loop lock for up to 120 s |
| §5.3 "When every member it started has ended, Dots resumes the delegating run" | The lead is continued (same session, worktree and branch) once its own thread has ended `Succeeded`, no member thread runs and some are unreported; past a limit it waits on budget. A lead that fails or is cancelled fails the step and cancels its running members | A member's run must not outlive the step that started it |
| §5.3 `total` | Counts the members the step started over every attempt (send-backs included), read from the store (`step_members_started`); `at_once` counts this attempt's running members, queued runs included | Spec 5.4: new runs after a send-back are "counted against `total`" |
| §6 a delegate hand-off's `members` `{dot, sub_task, status, summary, branch}` | `MemberHandoff { dot_id, task, status, summary, branch }`; the members are rows of `workflow_step_members`, a thread is a member's when its root has a row. A member gets its own `## Workflow` section and no workflow tools | Plan A's field names; the step's own thread and the members' must be told apart after a restart |
| §7 the tools "given only to the runs that may use it" | Only to runs of the step's own thread while the step is open: `report_outcome` with `outcomes`, `send_back` with `can_send_back_to`, `start_member` with `delegate`. A dot whose policy denies `Workflow` gets none, and its prompt lists none | A member's thread or an ended step must not change the step |
| §7 `report_outcome` and `send_back` (and `start_member`) under the policy name `Workflow` | Kept under the one name: a Sandboxed step asks before each call | **Open: the person decides at review.** Plan A parked "split or exempt in plan B"; the prototype kept asking |
| §7 `send_back` "the send-back happens when it ends" | Recorded on the step run (the last call wins) and performed when the step succeeds | A failed step has nothing to send back |
| §5.4 the steps after the target "resumed from its last run with a message" | Each is continued with the rerun message; when its own branch still exists the message asks it to merge the new work; when its branch is gone, its worktree is restored on the new start tip | The step builds on the new work either way |
| §5.4 "starts fresh from the target's branch" when the session is gone | The step starts fresh from its own old branch while that exists, else from its usual start, and its step run's `notice` says which | The old branch holds its own earlier work; the target's branch is not always its start |
| §4 `workflow_runs` | Also `send_back` (JSON `{from, to, note, by, held}`): the held send-back at the cap, else the last one performed. Step runs also get `outcome`, `notice`, `send_back` | The run page shows what waits; a pass after a restart finishes a send-back cut off |
| §5.5 `push`: `git push <remote> <branch>` | Pushes `<commit>:refs/heads/<branch>`, the commit the check approved, after checking each tip is unchanged; the tip check runs in every mode, `branch` and `member` included. A branch gone at approval is recorded with `"commit": null` and fails the deploy with the same message | The push is exactly what the person approved, even if a commit lands between the check and the push |
| §5.5 `pr`: `gh pr create` | An open PR for the branch is reused (`gh pr view <b> --json url,state`, open only); a missing gh is found (`gh --version`) before anything is pushed; a blank title line falls back to the branch name | A Retry does not fail on an existing PR; a merged or closed PR is never reused |
| §5.5 `pr_merge`: `gh pr merge` with `merge_method` | `gh pr merge <url> --auto --<method>`; the step succeeds once auto-merge is set | The person's decision (2026-11-02) |
| §5.5 (silent) what may be pushed | `push`, `pr` and `pr_merge` refuse the secret names and files over 10 MiB of Global Constraints, changed since the branch's base, before any push | The person's decision (2026-11-02) |
| §5.5 `pr` "against the default branch" | `--base` is the project's `default_branch`, else the remote's HEAD (`refs/remotes/<remote>/HEAD`), else the repo's current branch (which can be a feature branch, as plan A's worktrees fall back to `HEAD`), else the step fails `the project's default branch could not be found; set it on the project` | gh needs a branch name |
| §5.5 zero branches | A deploy after a check that showed no branch succeeds with `{"branches": [], "ready": …}` | "For each branch in the hand-off" of none is nothing |
| §5.5 `member` | Runs as an agent step with that member and job, from the approved branch; its hand-off is the result (`deploy_result` stays null). Later steps get its hand-off | It is "an agent step with a deploy job" |
| §5.1 a Test run's deploys act as `branch` | A test run deploys as `branch`, a `member` deploy included (no one is started) | A member deploy could push |
| §8 restart | A deploy step found `Running` with no job (a restart) fails `interrupted`; the person retries it | Nothing is pushed twice without the person |
| §8 cancellation during a deploy | A Stop while the deploy waits wins and nothing is pushed; a Stop during a push does not stop git or gh, and nothing is recorded | A half-killed push is worse than a finished one |
| §5.1 `schedule` "no catch-up and no retries" | A slot found 60 s or more late is skipped (so the slots missed while Dots was closed never run); a slot while any run of the workflow is in flight (any trigger) is skipped; a refused start uses up its slot; turning the workflow off, a problem or another trigger deletes its row, so turning it on starts afresh; `Until::Count` counts started runs; a resume from "Pause schedules" moves the slots on too | The person's "no catch-up" with the dots' 60 s tolerance; at most one run per slot |
| §5.1 `schedule` trigger fields | `{schedule, task, project}` (project by name); `catch_up` is ignored; the schedule is prepared on save as a dot's (an `Every` gets its anchor; a schedule that would never run is refused) | A workflow schedule never catches up |
| §5.1 `webhook` | Each call starts a run, even while another run of the workflow is in flight | Unlike a schedule, each call is a request |
| §5.1 `webhook` answers | 404 `unknown workflow` before 401 `invalid token` (as the dots' webhook); 409 for a workflow without a webhook trigger, turned off or with a problem, before the body is read; then 400 for the body; the role check (403) comes after the body, so a Viewer with a bad body gets 400. Blank or null body values fall back to the trigger's; extra keys are ignored | Turned off and problem are `Workflows::start`'s words; the role check is `start`'s; a sender's own payload can start the workflow |
| §4 `workflows.webhook_token` | Nullable; a new workflow has none until `Teams::regenerate_workflow_token`; kept when the trigger changes away from a webhook | The token is handed out once, on request |
| (silent) a project named in a schedule trigger or a webhook body | `teams::project_named`: trimmed and without case | As `start_workflow` and a branch on project match |
| §8 a run whose step was stopped | A run whose own step run was cancelled before its engine started ends `Cancelled`, not `Failed` with the ended-step message | The Stop race found in Task 1 |
| Plan A §4, §8 a run continuing a step's thread | `ask` and `follow_up` from a run whose step has ended are refused (`STEP_CLOSED`) and store nothing, checked under the loop's lock; `notify` is unchanged | Plan A's parked finding: they were accepted after the step closed |
| Plan A §4 a project's `default_branch` | Saving refuses one with a remote prefix (`origin/main`: the repo's remotes and the project's own `remote`) and names git refuses (`..`, a trailing `/`, a part ending `.lock`). A `default_branch` like `origin/main` saved before this plan is not re-checked until the project is saved again | Plan A's parked findings; plan A shipped only to the person's own machine |

### Task 1: Workflow definitions gain delegate, deploy and branch steps, send-backs, outcomes, schedule and webhook triggers

`workflow.rs` gains the `schedule` and `webhook` triggers, an agent step's `can_send_back_to`, `outcomes` and `delegate`, a check's `send_back_to`, and the `deploy` and `branch` kinds. A branch holds paths of steps, so the definition is a tree: steps are found by id (`all`, `step`, `position`, `first`, `next_after`, `before`), and `validate` checks the rules of section 5.8 across the whole tree. `workflows.rs` and the runner adapt to the new `step()`; a branch or deploy step fails as not built yet until Tasks 2 and 8.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/{workflow.rs,workflows.rs,runner.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/{workflow_def_test,workflows_test}.rs`; brought up to date (the builders only): `crates/dots-core/tests/{runner_workflow_test,runtime_roles_test,store_workflow_runs_test,teams_test,workflow_tools_test}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: plan A's `workflow::{Trigger, Step, StepKind, Budget, Definition}`, `schedule::Schedule` (`validate`).
- Produces: `workflow::{Trigger::Schedule { schedule: Schedule, task: String, project: String }, Trigger::Webhook { task: Option<String>, project: Option<String> }, StepKind::Agent { member, job, can_send_back_to: Vec<String>, outcomes: Vec<String>, delegate: Option<Delegate> }, Delegate { may_start: Vec<String>, at_once: u32 (default 3), total: u32 (default 6) }, StepKind::Check { send_back_to: Option<String> }, StepKind::Deploy { mode: DeployMode } (flattened), DeployMode { Branch, Push, Pr, PrMerge { merge_method: MergeMethod }, Member { member: String, job: String } } (tag "mode"), MergeMethod { Merge, Squash (default), Rebase }, StepKind::Branch { on: BranchOn, paths: Vec<BranchPath>, otherwise: Vec<Step> }, BranchOn { Outcome, Project }, BranchPath { label: String, steps: Vec<Step> }}` (new fields `#[serde(default)]`, so plan A's JSON still reads); `MAX_OUTCOME = 40`; `MAX_STEPS` counts the whole tree; `Definition::{all(&self) -> Vec<&Step>, step(&self, id: &str) -> Option<&Step> (was Option<(usize, &Step)>), position(&self, id: &str) -> Option<usize>, first(&self) -> Option<&Step>, next_after(&self, id: &str) -> Option<&Step>, before(&self, id: &str) -> Vec<&Step>}`; `workflows::NOT_BUILT = "this kind of step is not built yet"` (Task 8 removes it).

```rust
fn with_outcomes(mut step: Step, labels: &[&str]) -> Step {
    if let StepKind::Agent { outcomes, .. } = &mut step.kind {
        *outcomes = labels.iter().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();
    }
    step
}

fn sending_back_to(mut step: Step, ids: &[&str]) -> Step {
    if let StepKind::Agent {
        can_send_back_to, ..
    } = &mut step.kind
    {
        *can_send_back_to = ids.iter().map(|i| i.to_string()).collect();
    }
    step
}

fn delegating(mut step: Step, may_start: &[&str], at_once: u32, total: u32) -> Step {
    if let StepKind::Agent { delegate, .. } = &mut step.kind {
        *delegate = Some(Delegate {
            may_start: may_start.iter().map(|m| m.to_string()).collect(),
            at_once,
            total,
        });
    }
    step
}

fn check_to(id: &str, to: &str) -> Step {
    Step {
        id: id.into(),
        name: "Your check".into(),
        kind: StepKind::Check {
            send_back_to: Some(to.into()),
        },
    }
}

fn deploy(id: &str, mode: DeployMode) -> Step {
    Step {
        id: id.into(),
        name: format!("Deploy {id}"),
        kind: StepKind::Deploy { mode },
    }
}

fn branch(id: &str, on: BranchOn, paths: Vec<(&str, Vec<Step>)>, otherwise: Vec<Step>) -> Step {
    Step {
        id: id.into(),
        name: format!("Branch {id}"),
        kind: StepKind::Branch {
            on,
            paths: paths
                .into_iter()
                .map(|(label, steps)| BranchPath {
                    label: label.into(),
                    steps,
                })
                .collect(),
            otherwise,
        },
    }
}

fn daily() -> Schedule {
    serde_json::from_value(json!({"rule": {"kind": "daily", "time": "09:00"}})).unwrap()
}

#[test]
fn plan_a_definitions_still_read() {
    let d: Definition = serde_json::from_str(
        r#"{"trigger":{"kind":"manual"},"steps":[
            {"id":"a","name":"Fix it","kind":"agent","member":"dev","job":"Fix the bug."},
            {"id":"c","name":"Your check","kind":"check"}
        ],"budget":{"max_runs":10,"max_tokens":1500000,"max_minutes":120,"max_send_backs":3}}"#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        d.steps[0].kind,
        StepKind::Agent {
            member: "dev".into(),
            job: "Fix the bug.".into(),
            can_send_back_to: vec![],
            outcomes: vec![],
            delegate: None,
        }
    );
    assert_eq!(d.steps[1].kind, StepKind::Check { send_back_to: None });
    d.validate(&members()).unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn the_new_shapes_round_trip() {
    let value = json!({
        "trigger": {"kind": "webhook", "task": "Fix what the report says."},
        "steps": [
            {"id": "tm", "name": "Plan", "kind": "agent", "member": "helper", "job": "Split it.",
             "delegate": {"may_start": ["dev"]}},
            {"id": "dev", "name": "Fix", "kind": "agent", "member": "dev", "job": "Fix it.",
             "can_send_back_to": ["tm"], "outcomes": ["fixed", "needs_info"]},
            {"id": "br", "name": "Which", "kind": "branch", "on": "outcome", "paths": [
                {"label": "fixed", "steps": [
                    {"id": "c", "name": "Your check", "kind": "check", "send_back_to": "dev"},
                    {"id": "ship", "name": "Ship", "kind": "deploy", "mode": "pr_merge"}
                ]}
            ], "otherwise": [
                {"id": "ask", "name": "Ask", "kind": "agent", "member": "helper", "job": "Ask."}
            ]},
            {"id": "c2", "name": "Release check", "kind": "check"},
            {"id": "rel", "name": "Release", "kind": "deploy", "mode": "member", "member": "dev",
             "job": "Tag a release."}
        ]
    });
    let d: Definition = serde_json::from_value(value).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        d.trigger,
        Trigger::Webhook {
            task: Some("Fix what the report says.".into()),
            project: None
        }
    );
    let StepKind::Agent { delegate, .. } = &d.steps[0].kind else {
        panic!("not an agent step: {:?}", d.steps[0]);
    };
    assert_eq!(
        delegate,
        &Some(Delegate {
            may_start: vec!["dev".into()],
            at_once: 3,
            total: 6
        })
    );
    assert_eq!(
        d.step("ship").unwrap().kind,
        StepKind::Deploy {
            mode: DeployMode::PrMerge {
                merge_method: MergeMethod::Squash
            }
        }
    );
    assert_eq!(
        d.step("rel").unwrap().kind,
        StepKind::Deploy {
            mode: DeployMode::Member {
                member: "dev".into(),
                job: "Tag a release.".into()
            }
        }
    );
    d.validate(&members()).unwrap();
    let out = serde_json::to_value(&d).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(out["steps"][2]["paths"][0]["steps"][1]["mode"], "pr_merge");
    assert_eq!(
        out["steps"][2]["paths"][0]["steps"][1]["merge_method"],
        "squash"
    );
    let again: Definition = serde_json::from_value(out).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(again, d);
}

#[test]
fn a_schedule_trigger_round_trips_and_is_checked() {
    let mut d = def(vec![check("c")]);
    d.trigger = Trigger::Schedule {
        schedule: daily(),
        task: "Look for new issues.".into(),
        project: "Dots".into(),
    };
    d.validate(&members()).unwrap();
    let value = serde_json::to_value(&d).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(value["trigger"]["kind"], "schedule");
    assert_eq!(value["trigger"]["schedule"]["rule"]["kind"], "daily");
    assert_eq!(value["trigger"]["project"], "Dots");
    let again: Definition = serde_json::from_value(value).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(again, d);

    let with = |schedule: Schedule, task: &str, project: &str| {
        let mut d = def(vec![check("c")]);
        d.trigger = Trigger::Schedule {
            schedule,
            task: task.into(),
            project: project.into(),
        };
        d
    };
    let mut bad = daily();
    bad.rule = Rule::Weekly {
        days: vec![],
        time: chrono::NaiveTime::from_hms_opt(9, 0, 0).unwrap(),
    };
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&with(bad, "Look.", "Dots")),
        "the trigger's schedule: weekly: pick at least one day"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&with(daily(), " ", "Dots")),
        "the trigger's task must be 1 to 8,000 characters"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&with(daily(), &"x".repeat(8_001), "Dots")),
        "the trigger's task must be 1 to 8,000 characters"
    );
    with(daily(), &"x".repeat(8_000), "Dots")
        .validate(&members())
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&with(daily(), "Look.", "  ")),
        "the trigger needs a project"
    );
}

fn a_webhook_trigger_s_fallback_task_is_at_most_8000_characters() {

}

#[test]
fn a_delegate_step_may_start_only_members() {
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![delegating(
            agent("a", "helper"),
            &["ghost"],
            3,
            6
        )])),
        "step \"Step a\": it may start ghost, who is not a member of the team"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![delegating(agent("a", "helper"), &[], 3, 6)])),
        "step \"Step a\": a delegate step needs at least one member it may start"
    );
    def(vec![delegating(agent("a", "helper"), &["dev"], 3, 6)])
        .validate(&members())
        .unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn a_delegate_step_s_limits() {
    let limits = |at_once, total| {
        def(vec![delegating(
            agent("a", "helper"),
            &["dev"],
            at_once,
            total,
        )])
    };
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&limits(0, 0)),
        "step \"Step a\": the members it may start in all must be at least 1"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&limits(0, 6)),
        "step \"Step a\": the members it may start at once must be 1 to 6"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&limits(7, 6)),
        "step \"Step a\": the members it may start at once must be 1 to 6"
    );
    limits(6, 6).validate(&members()).unwrap();
    limits(1, 1).validate(&members()).unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn outcomes_are_1_to_40_characters_and_listed_once() {
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![with_outcomes(
            agent("a", "dev"),
            &["fixed", " "]
        )])),
        "step \"Step a\": an outcome must be 1 to 40 characters"
    );
    let long = "x".repeat(41);
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![with_outcomes(agent("a", "dev"), &[&long])])),
        "step \"Step a\": an outcome must be 1 to 40 characters"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![with_outcomes(
            agent("a", "dev"),
            &["fixed", " fixed"]
        )])),
        "step \"Step a\": the outcome \"fixed\" is listed twice"
    );
    def(vec![with_outcomes(
        agent("a", "dev"),
        &["fixed", "Fixed", &"x".repeat(40)],
    )])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn an_agent_sends_back_only_to_an_agent_step_before_it() {
    let refused = |to: &str| {
        format!(
            "step \"Step b\": work can be sent back only to an agent step before it, not to \
             \"{to}\""
        )
    };
    // A later step, a check, an unknown id, itself.
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            sending_back_to(agent("b", "dev"), &["c"]),
            agent("c", "dev")
        ])),
        refused("c")
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            check("k"),
            sending_back_to(agent("b", "dev"), &["k"])
        ])),
        refused("k")
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![sending_back_to(agent("b", "dev"), &["zz"])])),
        refused("zz")
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![sending_back_to(agent("b", "dev"), &["b"])])),
        refused("b")
    );
    // A step inside an earlier branch's path is on another path.
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            branch(
                "br",
                BranchOn::Project,
                vec![("Dots", vec![agent("in", "dev")])],
                vec![]
            ),
            sending_back_to(agent("b", "dev"), &["in"]),
        ])),
        refused("in")
    );
    def(vec![
        agent("a", "helper"),
        sending_back_to(agent("b", "dev"), &["a"]),
    ])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
    // From inside a path, back to a step before the branch.
    def(vec![
        agent("a", "helper"),
        branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Project,
            vec![("Dots", vec![sending_back_to(agent("b", "dev"), &["a"])])],
            vec![],
        ),
    ])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn a_check_sends_back_only_to_an_agent_step_before_it() {
    let refused = |to: &str| {
        format!(
            "step \"Your check\": work can be sent back only to an agent step before it, not \
             to \"{to}\""
        )
    };
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![check_to("k", "a"), agent("a", "dev")])),
        refused("a")
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![check("k1"), check_to("k2", "k1")])),
        refused("k1")
    );
    def(vec![agent("a", "dev"), check("k1"), check_to("k2", "a")])
        .validate(&members())
        .unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn a_deploy_needs_a_check_before_it_on_every_path() {
    let refused = "step \"Deploy d\": a deploy needs a check before it on every path";
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![agent("a", "dev"), deploy("d", DeployMode::Push)])),
        refused
    );
    // A check after it does not count.
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![deploy("d", DeployMode::Pr), check("k")])),
        refused
    );
    // A branch whose `otherwise` has no check leaves a path without one.
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            branch(
                "br",
                BranchOn::Project,
                vec![("Dots", vec![check("k")])],
                vec![]
            ),
            deploy("d", DeployMode::Push),
        ])),
        refused
    );
    // A nested branch with a path without a check.
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            branch(
                "br",
                BranchOn::Project,
                vec![(
                    "Dots",
                    vec![branch(
                        "inner",
                        BranchOn::Project,
                        vec![("Dots", vec![check("k1")])],
                        vec![agent("a", "dev")],
                    )]
                )],
                vec![check("k2")],
            ),
            deploy("d", DeployMode::Push),
        ])),
        refused
    );
    // A check after it in its own path does not count, even when the branch's other lists
    // hold one.
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            agent("a", "dev"),
            branch(
                "br",
                BranchOn::Project,
                vec![("Dots", vec![deploy("d", DeployMode::Push), check("k1")])],
                vec![check("k2")],
            ),
        ])),
        refused
    );
    def(vec![check("k"), deploy("d", DeployMode::Push)])
        .validate(&members())
        .unwrap();
    // Every path and `otherwise` holds a check, one of them in a nested branch.
    def(vec![
        branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Project,
            vec![(
                "Dots",
                vec![branch(
                    "inner",
                    BranchOn::Project,
                    vec![("Dots", vec![agent("a", "dev"), check("k1")])],
                    vec![check("k2")],
                )],
            )],
            vec![check("k3")],
        ),
        deploy("d", DeployMode::Push),
    ])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
    // Inside a path, after a check of that path or before the branch.
    def(vec![branch(
        "br",
        BranchOn::Project,
        vec![("Dots", vec![check("k"), deploy("d", DeployMode::Branch)])],
        vec![],
    )])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
    def(vec![
        check("k"),
        branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Project,
            vec![("Dots", vec![deploy("d", DeployMode::Branch)])],
            vec![],
        ),
    ])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn a_deploy_member_is_a_member_with_a_job() {
    let member = |member: &str, job: &str| {
        def(vec![
            check("k"),
            deploy(
                "d",
                DeployMode::Member {
                    member: member.into(),
                    job: job.into(),
                },
            ),
        ])
    };
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&member("ghost", "Tag it.")),
        "step \"Deploy d\": ghost is not a member of the team"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&member("dev", " ")),
        "step \"Deploy d\": the job must be 1 to 8,000 characters"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&member("dev", &"x".repeat(8_001))),
        "step \"Deploy d\": the job must be 1 to 8,000 characters"
    );
    member("dev", "Tag it.").validate(&members()).unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn a_branch_on_outcome_uses_the_labels_of_the_nearest_agent_step() {
    let on_outcome = |labels: &[&str]| {
        branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Outcome,
            labels.iter().map(|l| (*l, vec![])).collect(),
            vec![],
        )
    };
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![check("c"), on_outcome(&["fixed"])])),
        "step \"Branch br\": a branch on outcome needs an agent step before it"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            with_outcomes(agent("a", "dev"), &["fixed"]),
            on_outcome(&["fixed", "needs_info"]),
        ])),
        "step \"Branch br\": \"needs_info\" is not an outcome of the step \"Step a\""
    );
    // The nearest agent step's labels, not an earlier one's.
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            with_outcomes(agent("a", "dev"), &["fixed"]),
            agent("b", "helper"),
            on_outcome(&["fixed"]),
        ])),
        "step \"Branch br\": \"fixed\" is not an outcome of the step \"Step b\""
    );
    def(vec![
        with_outcomes(agent("a", "dev"), &["fixed", "needs_info"]),
        check("c"),
        on_outcome(&["fixed", "needs_info"]),
    ])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
    // A branch on project needs no outcomes.
    def(vec![branch(
        "br",
        BranchOn::Project,
        vec![("Dots", vec![check("k")])],
        vec![],
    )])
    .validate(&members())
    .unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn a_branch_s_labels_are_given_once() {
    let labels = |labels: &[&str]| {
        def(vec![branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Project,
            labels.iter().map(|l| (*l, vec![])).collect(),
            vec![],
        )])
    };
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&labels(&["Dots", " "])),
        "step \"Branch br\": every path needs a label"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&labels(&["Dots", "dots "])),
        "step \"Branch br\": two paths have the label \"dots\""
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&labels(&[])),
        "step \"Branch br\": a branch needs at least one path"
    );
    labels(&["Dots", "Other app"]).validate(&members()).unwrap();
}

#[test]
fn ids_are_unique_and_names_checked_across_the_tree() {
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![
            agent("a", "dev"),
            branch(
                "br",
                BranchOn::Project,
                vec![("Dots", vec![agent("a", "dev")])],
                vec![]
            ),
        ])),
        "step \"Step a\": another step has the id \"a\""
    );
    assert_eq!(
        refusal(&def(vec![branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Project,
            vec![("Dots", vec![])],
            vec![agent("a", "ghost")]
        )])),
        "step \"Step a\": ghost is not a member of the team"
    );
}

#[test]
fn the_step_limit_counts_the_steps_inside_branches() {
    let nested = |n: usize| {
        def(vec![branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Project,
            vec![("Dots", (0..n).map(|i| check(&format!("c{i}"))).collect())],
            vec![],
        )])
    };
    assert_eq!(refusal(&nested(50)), "a workflow has at most 50 steps");
    nested(49).validate(&members()).unwrap();
}

/// `a`, then a branch on its outcome: `fixed` runs a check, a nested branch on project
/// (`Dots`: a deploy; otherwise `x`) and `after`; `needs_info` runs `h`; otherwise `o`. Then
/// `z`.
fn tree() -> Definition {
    def(vec![
        with_outcomes(agent("a", "helper"), &["fixed", "needs_info"]),
        branch(
            "br",
            BranchOn::Outcome,
            vec![
                (
                    "fixed",
                    vec![
                        check("c1"),
                        branch(
                            "nb",
                            BranchOn::Project,
                            vec![("Dots", vec![deploy("d1", DeployMode::Push)])],
                            vec![agent("x", "dev")],
                        ),
                        agent("after", "dev"),
                    ],
                ),
                ("needs_info", vec![agent("h", "helper")]),
            ],
            vec![agent("o", "dev")],
        ),
        check("z"),
    ])
}

fn ids(steps: Vec<&Step>) -> Vec<&str> {
    steps.into_iter().map(|s| s.id.as_str()).collect()
}

#[test]
fn all_lists_the_tree_in_run_order() {
    let d = tree();
    d.validate(&members()).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        ids(d.all()),
        ["a", "br", "c1", "nb", "d1", "x", "after", "h", "o", "z"]
    );
    assert_eq!(d.position("a"), Some(0));
    assert_eq!(d.position("d1"), Some(4));
    assert_eq!(d.position("z"), Some(9));
    assert_eq!(d.position("nope"), None);
    assert_eq!(d.step("d1").map(|s| s.name.as_str()), Some("Deploy d1"));
    assert_eq!(d.first().map(|s| s.id.as_str()), Some("a"));
    assert!(def(vec![]).first().is_none());
}

#[test]
fn next_after_climbs_out_of_ended_paths() {
    let d = tree();
    let next = |id: &str| d.next_after(id).map(|s| s.id.as_str());
    assert_eq!(next("a"), Some("br"));
    // A branch's next is the step after the whole branch.
    assert_eq!(next("br"), Some("z"));
    assert_eq!(next("c1"), Some("nb"));
    // The end of a nested path goes on in the path around it.
    assert_eq!(next("d1"), Some("after"));
    assert_eq!(next("x"), Some("after"));
    // The end of a path goes on after its branch.
    assert_eq!(next("after"), Some("z"));
    assert_eq!(next("h"), Some("z"));
    assert_eq!(next("o"), Some("z"));
    assert_eq!(next("z"), None);
    assert_eq!(next("nope"), None);
}

#[test]
fn before_lists_the_steps_on_the_way_to_a_step() {
    let d = tree();
    assert!(d.before("a").is_empty());
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("br")), ["a"]);
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("c1")), ["a", "br"]);
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("d1")), ["a", "br", "c1", "nb"]);
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("x")), ["a", "br", "c1", "nb"]);
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("after")), ["a", "br", "c1", "nb"]);
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("h")), ["a", "br"]);
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("o")), ["a", "br"]);
    // Steps inside the branch's paths are not on the way to the step after it.
    assert_eq!(ids(d.before("z")), ["a", "br"]);
    assert!(d.before("nope").is_empty());
}
```

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_branch_or_deploy_step_fails_as_not_built_yet() {
    let w = world().await;
    let branch = Step {
        id: "pick".into(),
        name: "Pick".into(),
        kind: StepKind::Branch {
            on: BranchOn::Project,
            paths: vec![BranchPath {
                label: "Dots".into(),
                steps: vec![check("inner")],
            }],
            otherwise: vec![],
        },
    };
    let flow = w.workflow("Branch", vec![branch]).await;
    let wr = w.start(&flow).await;
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::StepFailed)).await;
    assert_eq!(
        wr.error.as_deref(),
        Some("Pick: this kind of step is not built yet")
    );
    assert_eq!(wr.current_step.as_deref(), Some("pick"));
    let deploy = Step {
        id: "ship".into(),
        name: "Ship".into(),
        kind: StepKind::Deploy {
            mode: DeployMode::Push,
        },
    };
    let flow = w.workflow("Deploy", vec![check("check"), deploy]).await;
    let wr = w.start(&flow).await;
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::Check)).await;
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::StepFailed)).await;
    assert_eq!(
        wr.error.as_deref(),
        Some("Ship: this kind of step is not built yet")
    );
    // A retry tries it again, and it fails again.
    w.workflows.retry_step(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::StepFailed)).await;
    let attempts: Vec<_> = wr
        .steps
        .iter()
        .filter(|s| s.step_id == "ship")
        .map(|s| (s.attempt, s.status))
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(
        attempts,
        [(1, StepRunStatus::Failed), (2, StepRunStatus::Failed)]
    );
}
```

```rust
pub enum StepKind {
    /// A member (a dot id) does one job, added to its own instructions. With `delegate`, it
    /// may start other members (section 5.3).
    Agent {
        member: String,
        job: String,
        /// The ids of earlier agent steps it may send work back to.
        #[serde(default)]
        can_send_back_to: Vec<String>,
        /// The labels it may report, for a branch on outcome after it.
        #[serde(default)]
        outcomes: Vec<String>,
        #[serde(default)]
        delegate: Option<Delegate>,
    },
    /// The workflow waits for the person to approve or send back.
    Check {
        /// The id of the agent step a send-back goes to; `None`: the nearest agent step before
        /// it on its path.
        #[serde(default)]
        send_back_to: Option<String>,
    },
    /// Dots ships the branches the check before it approved (section 5.5).
    Deploy {
        #[serde(flatten)]
        mode: DeployMode,
    },
    /// The workflow takes the first path whose label matches, else `otherwise`, then goes on
    /// after the branch (section 5.6).
    Branch {
        on: BranchOn,
        paths: Vec<BranchPath>,
        #[serde(default)]
        otherwise: Vec<Step>,
    },
}

/// What a delegate step's member may start (section 5.3).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Delegate {
    /// The members (dot ids) it may start.
    pub may_start: Vec<String>,
    /// The most of them running at once.
    #[serde(default = "default_at_once")]
    pub at_once: u32,
    /// The most it starts in all.
    #[serde(default = "default_total")]
    pub total: u32,
}

/// `Delegate::at_once` when the JSON leaves it out.
fn default_at_once() -> u32 {
    3
}

/// `Delegate::total` when the JSON leaves it out.
fn default_total() -> u32 {
    6
}

/// How a deploy step ships (section 5.5). JSON: `"mode"` beside the step's other fields.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(tag = "mode", rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum DeployMode {
    /// The branches are kept and listed as ready.
    Branch,
    /// `git push` of each branch.
    Push,
    /// `push`, then a pull request against the default branch.
    Pr,
    /// `pr`, then auto-merge with `merge_method`.
    PrMerge {
        #[serde(default)]
        merge_method: MergeMethod,
    },
    /// A member does the deploy as an agent step with this job.
    Member { member: String, job: String },
}

/// How `gh pr merge` merges.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum MergeMethod {
    Merge,
    #[default]
    Squash,
    Rebase,
}

/// What a branch step compares its labels with.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum BranchOn {
    /// The outcome the nearest agent step before it reported.
    Outcome,
    /// The name of the workflow run's project.
    Project,
}

/// One path of a branch step: an outcome label or a project name, and its steps.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct BranchPath {
    pub label: String,
    pub steps: Vec<Step>,
}
```

```rust
impl Definition {
    /// Checks the definition against the team's `members` (dot ids): section 5.8's rules that
    /// apply to the kinds above. The message names the step it is about.
    pub fn validate(&self, members: &[String]) -> Result<()> {
```

**with:**

```rust

/// Whether `text`, trimmed, is longer than `max` characters.
fn too_long(text: &str, max: usize) -> bool {
    text.trim().chars().count() > max
}

impl StepKind {
    /// The lists of steps inside a branch step, in run order: its paths, then `otherwise`.
    /// Empty for the other kinds.
    fn lists(&self) -> Vec<&[Step]> {
        match self {
            StepKind::Branch {
                paths, otherwise, ..
            } => paths
                .iter()
                .map(|p| p.steps.as_slice())
                .chain(std::iter::once(otherwise.as_slice()))
                .collect(),
            _ => Vec::new(),
        }
    }
}

/// Every step of `steps`, depth first: a branch, then its paths in order, then `otherwise`.
fn walk<'a>(steps: &'a [Step], out: &mut Vec<&'a Step>) {
    for step in steps {
        out.push(step);
        for list in step.kind.lists() {
            walk(list, out);
        }
    }
}

/// The way down to the step `id`: for each level from the top, the list it is in and its place
/// in that list. The last entry is the step itself; the ones before it are its enclosing
/// branches. False when no step has the id.
fn trail<'a>(steps: &'a [Step], id: &str, out: &mut Vec<(&'a [Step], usize)>) -> bool {
    for (i, step) in steps.iter().enumerate() {
        out.push((steps, i));
        if step.id == id {
            return true;
        }
        for list in step.kind.lists() {
            if trail(list, id, out) {
                return true;
            }
        }
        out.pop();
    }
    false
}

/// Whether running `steps` always passes a check: a check in the list, or a branch whose every
/// path and `otherwise` always passes one, at any depth.
fn always_checks(steps: &[Step]) -> bool {
    steps.iter().any(passes_check)
}

/// Whether running `step` always passes a check: it is one, or it is a branch every one of
/// whose lists always passes one.
fn passes_check(step: &Step) -> bool {
    match &step.kind {
        StepKind::Check { .. } => true,
        StepKind::Branch { .. } => step.kind.lists().into_iter().all(always_checks),
        _ => false,
    }
}
```

```rust
    /// The trigger's rules; `member` tells whether a dot id is a member of the team.
    fn validate_trigger(&self, member: &dyn Fn(&str) -> bool) -> Result<()> {
        match &self.trigger {
            Trigger::Manual => {}
            Trigger::Chat { starters } => {
                if starters.is_empty() {
                    return invalid(
                        "a chat trigger needs at least one member who starts it".into(),
                    );
                }
                if let Some(s) = starters.iter().find(|s| !member(s)) {
                    return invalid(format!(
                        "the trigger names {s}, who is not a member of the team"
                    ));
                }
            }
            Trigger::Schedule {
                schedule,
                task,
                project,
            } => {
                match schedule.validate() {
                    Err(Error::Invalid(m)) => {
                        return invalid(format!("the trigger's schedule: {m}"));
                    }
                    other => other?,
                }
                if task.trim().is_empty() || too_long(task, MAX_TASK) {
                    return invalid("the trigger's task must be 1 to 8,000 characters".into());
                }
                if project.trim().is_empty() {
                    return invalid("the trigger needs a project".into());
                }
            }
            Trigger::Webhook { task, .. } => {
                // A blank fallback is no fallback: the body must then carry the task.
                if task.as_deref().is_some_and(|t| too_long(t, MAX_TASK)) {
                    return invalid("the trigger's task must be 1 to 8,000 characters".into());
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// The rules of one step's own fields and of what must come before it on its path.
    fn validate_step(&self, step: &Step, member: &dyn Fn(&str) -> bool) -> Result<()> {
        let name = step.name.trim();
        // An agent step `id` before `step` on its path, as a send-back target must be.
        let earlier_agent = |id: &str| {
            self.before(&step.id)
                .iter()
                .any(|s| s.id == id && matches!(s.kind, StepKind::Agent { .. }))
        };
        let refused_target = |id: &str| {
            invalid(format!(
                "step \"{name}\": work can be sent back only to an agent step before it, not to \
                 {id:?}"
            ))
        };
        let member_and_job = |m: &str, job: &str| {
            if !member(m) {
                return invalid(format!("step \"{name}\": {m} is not a member of the team"));
            }
            let job = job.trim();
            if job.is_empty() || job.chars().count() > MAX_JOB {
                return invalid(format!(
                    "step \"{name}\": the job must be 1 to 8,000 characters"
                ));
            }
            Ok(())
        };
        match &step.kind {
            StepKind::Agent {
                member: m,
                job,
                can_send_back_to,
                outcomes,
                delegate,
            } => {
                member_and_job(m, job)?;
                let mut seen = HashSet::new();
                for outcome in outcomes {
                    let outcome = outcome.trim();
                    if outcome.is_empty() || outcome.chars().count() > MAX_OUTCOME {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": an outcome must be 1 to {MAX_OUTCOME} characters"
                        ));
                    }
                    if !seen.insert(outcome) {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": the outcome \"{outcome}\" is listed twice"
                        ));
                    }
                }
                if let Some(id) = can_send_back_to.iter().find(|id| !earlier_agent(id)) {
                    return refused_target(id);
                }
                if let Some(d) = delegate {
                    if d.may_start.is_empty() {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": a delegate step needs at least one member it may \
                             start"
                        ));
                    }
                    if let Some(s) = d.may_start.iter().find(|s| !member(s)) {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": it may start {s}, who is not a member of the team"
                        ));
                    }
                    if d.total == 0 {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": the members it may start in all must be at least 1"
                        ));
                    }
                    if d.at_once == 0 || d.at_once > d.total {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": the members it may start at once must be 1 to {}",
                            d.total
                        ));
                    }
                }
            }
            StepKind::Check { send_back_to } => {
                if let Some(id) = send_back_to.as_deref().filter(|id| !earlier_agent(id)) {
                    return refused_target(id);
                }
            }
            StepKind::Deploy { mode } => {
                if let DeployMode::Member { member: m, job } = mode {
                    member_and_job(m, job)?;
                }
                if !self.checked_before(&step.id) {
                    return invalid(format!(
                        "step \"{name}\": a deploy needs a check before it on every path"
                    ));
                }
            }
            StepKind::Branch { on, paths, .. } => {
                if paths.is_empty() {
                    return invalid(format!("step \"{name}\": a branch needs at least one path"));
                }
                let mut seen = HashSet::new();
                for path in paths {
                    let label = path.label.trim();
                    if label.is_empty() {
                        return invalid(format!("step \"{name}\": every path needs a label"));
                    }
                    // Projects are matched without case, so two labels that differ only in
                    // case could never both be taken.
                    if !seen.insert(label.to_lowercase()) {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": two paths have the label \"{label}\""
                        ));
                    }
                }
                if *on == BranchOn::Outcome {
                    // The outcome a run branches on is the nearest agent step's before it.
                    let source =
                        self.before(&step.id)
                            .into_iter()
                            .rev()
                            .find_map(|s| match &s.kind {
                                StepKind::Agent { outcomes, .. } => Some((s, outcomes)),
                                _ => None,
                            });
                    let Some((source, outcomes)) = source else {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": a branch on outcome needs an agent step before it"
                        ));
                    };
                    let declared = |label: &str| outcomes.iter().any(|o| o.trim() == label);
                    if let Some(path) = paths.iter().find(|p| !declared(p.label.trim())) {
                        return invalid(format!(
                            "step \"{name}\": \"{}\" is not an outcome of the step \"{}\"",
                            path.label.trim(),
                            source.name.trim()
                        ));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Every step, depth first in run order: a branch, then its paths in order, then
    /// `otherwise`.
    pub fn all(&self) -> Vec<&Step> {
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        walk(&self.steps, &mut out);
        out
    }

    /// The step with `id`, at any depth.
    pub fn step(&self, id: &str) -> Option<&Step> {
        self.all().into_iter().find(|s| s.id == id)
    }

    /// The place of the step `id` in `all()`, from 0: a prompt's "<n> of <total>" is this
    /// plus one, of `all().len()`.
    pub fn position(&self, id: &str) -> Option<usize> {
        self.all().iter().position(|s| s.id == id)
    }

    /// The step a workflow run begins with.
    pub fn first(&self) -> Option<&Step> {
        self.steps.first()
    }

    /// The step after `id` once it has run: its next sibling, else the step after its
    /// enclosing branch, and so on up the tree. `None` past the end, or for an unknown id. For
    /// a branch step this is the step after the whole branch: the path it takes is the run's
    /// to choose.
    pub fn next_after(&self, id: &str) -> Option<&Step> {
        let mut way = Vec::new();
        if !trail(&self.steps, id, &mut way) {
            return None;
        }
        way.into_iter().rev().find_map(|(list, i)| list.get(i + 1))
    }

    /// The steps that run before `id` on its path, oldest first: at each level from the top,
    /// the siblings before it and then the branch it is in. Steps inside an earlier branch's
    /// paths are not in it: which path ran is not known here. Empty for an unknown id.
    pub fn before(&self, id: &str) -> Vec<&Step> {
        let mut way = Vec::new();
        if !trail(&self.steps, id, &mut way) {
            return Vec::new();
        }
        let last = way.len() - 1;
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (level, (list, i)) in way.into_iter().enumerate() {
            out.extend(&list[..i]);
            if level < last {
                out.push(&list[i]);
            }
        }
        out
    }

    /// Whether every path to the step `id` passes a check before it: at some level, an
    /// earlier sibling is a check or a branch that always passes one. The enclosing branches
    /// do not count themselves: one of their lists is the step's own path, where a check may
    /// come after the step.
    fn checked_before(&self, id: &str) -> bool {
        let mut way = Vec::new();
        trail(&self.steps, id, &mut way)
            && way
                .into_iter()
                .any(|(list, i)| list[..i].iter().any(passes_check))
    }
```

### Task 2: A workflow run moves through branches by outcome or project

The loop moves by step id: it begins `first()`, and after a step succeeds begins the next one on the path taken (`next_of`). A branch step chooses at once, by the outcome the nearest agent step before it reported or by the project's name, and records `{"path": …}`. Hand-offs, the start branch and the prompt's step number follow the path taken (`ran_before`, `position`). Migration 0027 is created: step runs gain `outcome` and `notice`.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/dots-core/migrations/0027_teams_b.sql`
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/{model.rs,store/workflow_runs.rs,workflows.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/{workflows_test,store_workflow_runs_test}.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `Definition::{all, step, position, first, next_after, before}` and `StepKind::Branch`.
- Produces: `StepRun.{outcome: Option<String>, notice: Option<String>}`; `StepRunChange.notice: Option<Option<String>>` (`None` leaves it, `Some(None)` clears it); `Store::set_step_outcome(&self, step_run_id: &str, outcome: Option<&str>) -> Result<StepRun>` (unchecked; `Error::NotFound("step run <id>")`). Crate-private in `workflows.rs`: `latest(wr, step_id)`, `succeeded(wr, step_id) -> Option<&StepRun>`, `next_attempt(wr, step_id) -> u32`, `holds(step, id) -> bool`, `taken(wr, branch: &Step) -> &[Step]`, `ran_before(wr, id) -> Vec<&Step>`, `next_of(wr, id) -> Option<&Step>`, `choose(wr, id, on, paths) -> Option<&BranchPath>`, `handoffs_before(wr, id: &str) -> Vec<(&Step, &Handoff)>`, `start_branch(wr, id: &str) -> Option<String>`; `Workflows::{begin_step(wr, step: &Step), begin_or_finish(wr, next: Option<&Step>), finish(wr), take_branch(wr, step, step_run: Option<&StepRun>) -> Result<Option<&Step>>, queue_agent_run(wr, step: &Step, step_run), section(wr, step: &Step), watch_agent(wr, step: &Step, step_run)}`. `index_of` is gone.

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn step_runs_record_their_outcome_and_notice() {
    let f = fixture().await;
    let wr = f.store.create_workflow_run(&f.new).await.unwrap();
    let step = f
        .store
        .create_step_run(&wr.id, "a", 1, StepRunStatus::Running)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        (step.outcome.as_deref(), step.notice.as_deref()),
        (None, None)
    );
    let reported = f
        .store
        .set_step_outcome(&step.id, Some("fixed"))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(reported.outcome.as_deref(), Some("fixed"));
    assert_eq!(reported.status, StepRunStatus::Running);
    let noticed = f
        .store
        .set_step_run(
            &step.id,
            &StepRunChange {
                notice: Some(Some("Started again from the start.".into())),
                ..Default::default()
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        noticed.notice.as_deref(),
        Some("Started again from the start.")
    );
    // A change that does not name them leaves them as they are.
    let done = f
        .store
        .set_step_run(
            &step.id,
            &StepRunChange {
                status: Some(StepRunStatus::Succeeded),
                ..Default::default()
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(done.outcome.as_deref(), Some("fixed"));
    assert_eq!(
        done.notice.as_deref(),
        Some("Started again from the start.")
    );
    // Both clear.
    let cleared = f.store.set_step_outcome(&step.id, None).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(cleared.outcome, None);
    let cleared = f
        .store
        .set_step_run(
            &step.id,
            &StepRunChange {
                notice: Some(None),
                ..Default::default()
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(cleared.notice, None);
    assert!(matches!(
        f.store.set_step_outcome("nope", Some("fixed")).await,
        Err(Error::NotFound(_))
    ));
}
```
