```rust
    /// How many members the delegate step of the step run `step_run_id` has started in its
    /// workflow run, over every step run of that step (teams spec section 5.4: a send-back to
    /// the step goes on counting against its `total`).
    pub async fn step_members_started(&self, step_run_id: &str) -> Result<u32> {
        let n: i64 = sqlx::query_scalar(
            "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM workflow_step_members m \
             JOIN workflow_step_runs s ON s.id = m.step_run_id \
             JOIN workflow_step_runs o ON o.workflow_run_id = s.workflow_run_id \
             AND o.step_id = s.step_id \
             WHERE o.id = ?",
        )
        .bind(step_run_id)
        .fetch_one(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        Ok(n as u32)
    }
```

**Create `crates/dots-core/src/workflows/send_back.rs`:**

```rust
//! Send-backs (teams spec sections 5.4 and 9): work goes back to an earlier agent step, from
//! the person at a check (`send_back_check`) or from an agent step that asked for it with
//! `send_back` (performed once that step has succeeded). The target gets a new step run whose
//! first run continues the last run of its earlier one, as an answer to `ask` does (the same
//! session, worktree and branch), with the note. The steps after it then run again in order:
//! an agent step is continued in the same way with the newest hand-off before it, a check waits
//! again and a branch chooses again. Past `max_send_backs` nothing is performed: the workflow
//! run waits until the person allows one more, or stops it.
//!
//! The writes are ordered so a pass finishes a send-back cut off half way (Dots stopped):
//! a sender that is still the current step with its step run succeeded has not performed the
//! send-back it asks for (`move_on` performs it); the target's new step run is made before the
//! workflow run moves to it, and a pass that performs again reuses it; and the first run of a
//! step run that has none is the one `queue_first` makes, whoever starts it.

use std::path::Path;

use serde_json::json;

use super::delegate::own_runs;
use super::{
    branch_exists, handoffs_before, latest, next_attempt, next_of, ran_before, start_branch,
    Workflows, MAX_TASK,
};
use crate::accounts::Permission;
use crate::model::{
    SendBack, SentBy, StepRun, StepRunChange, StepRunStatus, WaitingOn, WorkflowRun,
    WorkflowRunChange, WorkflowRunStatus,
};
use crate::team_tools::quoted;
use crate::workflow::{Step, StepKind};
use crate::workspace::git;
use crate::{Error, Result};

/// Why a step that went back started afresh, after where it started from.
const NOT_RESUMED: &str = "the earlier session could not be resumed.";

/// How a fresh run of a step that went back starts, when its earlier session cannot be
/// resumed: from `start` (a full ref; `None` as the step starts the first time), with `message`
/// in its `## Workflow` section.
pub(super) struct Again {
    pub(super) start: Option<String>,
    pub(super) message: String,
}

fn is_agent(step: &&Step) -> bool {
    matches!(step.kind, StepKind::Agent { .. })
}

/// The agent step a send-back from the check `check` goes to: its `send_back_to`, else the
/// nearest agent step that ran before it on its path, whose hand-off the check showed.
pub(super) fn check_target<'a>(wr: &'a WorkflowRun, check: &Step) -> Option<&'a Step> {
    let StepKind::Check { send_back_to } = &check.kind else {
        return None;
    };
    match send_back_to {
        Some(id) => wr.definition.step(id).filter(is_agent),
        None => ran_before(wr, &check.id).into_iter().rev().find(is_agent),
    }
}

/// The send-back the succeeded step run `step_run` of `step` asks for, if any: a check's
/// decision to send the work back, or what its agent asked for with `send_back`
/// (`{"to", "note"}`). While `step` is still the current step it has not been performed.
fn asked(wr: &WorkflowRun, step: &Step, step_run: &StepRun) -> Option<SendBack> {
    let (to, note, by) = match &step.kind {
        StepKind::Check { .. } => {
            let decision = step_run.decision.as_ref()?;
            if decision.get("decision")?.as_str()? != "sent_back" {
                return None;
            }
            let note = decision.get("note")?.as_str()?;
            (check_target(wr, step)?.id.clone(), note, SentBy::Person)
        }
        StepKind::Agent { .. } => {
            let asked = step_run.send_back.as_ref()?;
            let to = asked.get("to")?.as_str()?.to_string();
            (to, asked.get("note")?.as_str()?, SentBy::Agent)
        }
        _ => return None,
    };
    Some(SendBack {
        from: step.id.clone(),
        to,
        note: note.to_string(),
        by,
        held: false,
    })
}

/// What the target of `send` is told (section 5.4): who sent the work back, and the note.
fn sent_message(wr: &WorkflowRun, send: &SendBack) -> String {
    match send.by {
        SentBy::Person => format!("The person sent this back: {}", send.note),
        SentBy::Agent => {
            let from = wr
                .definition
                .step(&send.from)
                .map_or(send.from.as_str(), |s| s.name.as_str());
            format!("The step \"{from}\" sent this back: {}", send.note)
        }
    }
}

/// What a step after the target of the workflow run's last send-back is told when it runs
/// again: the newest hand-off before it, with its branches. `kept`: the step's own branch
/// still has its earlier commits, so it is told to merge the new work into it.
fn rerun_message(wr: &WorkflowRun, step: &Step, kept: bool) -> String {
    let target = wr
        .send_back
        .as_ref()
        .and_then(|s| wr.definition.step(&s.to))
        .map_or("an earlier step", |s| s.name.as_str());
    let newest = handoffs_before(wr, &step.id).last().map(|(_, h)| *h);
    let summary = newest
        .map(|h| h.summary.trim())
        .filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
        .unwrap_or("(no summary)");
    let branches: Vec<&str> = newest
        .map(|h| h.branches.iter().map(|b| b.branch.as_str()).collect())
        .unwrap_or_default();
    let mut out =
        format!("Work was sent back to \"{target}\" and has run again. Its new hand-off:\n\n");
    out.push_str(summary);
    out.push_str("\n\n");
    if !branches.is_empty() {
        out.push_str(&format!("Branches: {}\n\n", branches.join(", ")));
        if kept {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "Your branch still has your earlier commits: merge {} into it before you go \
                 on.\n\n",
                quoted(branches.iter().copied())
            ));
        }
    }
    out.push_str("Update your work to match, then end with a new summary.");
    out
}

impl Workflows {
    /// Sends the work the check the workflow run waits on was shown back (section 5.4), with
    /// the person's note of 1 to 8,000 characters: the check's step run succeeds with the
    /// decision, and the work goes to the check's `send_back_to`, else to the nearest agent
    /// step before it, within the cap of send-backs (section 9). Refused for a run not waiting
    /// on a check, and for a check with no agent step before it.
    pub async fn send_back_check(&self, id: &str, note: &str) -> Result<WorkflowRun> {
        let _one = self.lock.lock().await;
        let wr = self.store.get_workflow_run(id).await?;
        self.require(&wr.team_id, Permission::AnswerApprovals)
            .await?;
        let wr = self.tick(&wr).await?;
        let step_run = match (&wr.status, &wr.waiting_on, &wr.current_step) {
            (WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting, Some(WaitingOn::Check), Some(current)) => {
                latest(&wr, current).cloned()
            }
            _ => None,
        };
        let check = step_run
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|s| wr.definition.step(&s.step_id));
        let (Some(step_run), Some(check)) = (step_run.as_ref(), check) else {
            return Err(Error::Conflict(format!(
                "workflow run {id} is not waiting for a check"
            )));
        };
        let note = note.trim();
        if note.is_empty() || note.chars().count() > MAX_TASK {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(
                "the note must be 1 to 8,000 characters".into(),
            ));
        }
        if check_target(&wr, check).is_none() {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "the check \"{}\" has no agent step before it to send the work back to",
                check.name
            )));
        }
        let shown = handoffs_before(&wr, &check.id)
            .last()
            .map(|(_, h)| (*h).clone())
            .unwrap_or_default();
        self.store
            .set_step_run(
                &step_run.id,
                &StepRunChange {
                    status: Some(StepRunStatus::Succeeded),
                    handoff: Some(Some(shown)),
                    decision: Some(Some(json!({"decision": "sent_back", "note": note}))),
                    ..Default::default()
                },
            )
            .await?;
        let wr = self
            .store
            .set_workflow_run(
                id,
                &WorkflowRunChange {
                    status: Some(WorkflowRunStatus::Running),
                    waiting_on: Some(None),
                    ..Default::default()
                },
            )
            .await?;
        self.move_on(&wr, check).await?;
        let wr = self.store.get_workflow_run(id).await?;
        self.updated(&wr);
        Ok(wr)
    }

    /// Performs the send-back the workflow run holds at its cap (section 9): raises its
    /// `max_send_backs` by one, once, and sends the work back.
    pub async fn allow_send_back(&self, id: &str) -> Result<WorkflowRun> {
        let _one = self.lock.lock().await;
        let wr = self.store.get_workflow_run(id).await?;
        self.require(&wr.team_id, Permission::RunDots).await?;
        let wr = self.tick(&wr).await?;
        if !(wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting && wr.waiting_on == Some(WaitingOn::SendBacks))
        {
            return Err(Error::Conflict(format!(
                "workflow run {id} is not waiting at its send-back limit"
            )));
        }
        let mut budget = wr.budget;
        budget.max_send_backs = budget.max_send_backs.saturating_add(1);
        self.store
            .set_workflow_run(
                id,
                &WorkflowRunChange {
                    status: Some(WorkflowRunStatus::Running),
                    waiting_on: Some(None),
                    budget: Some(budget),
                    error: Some(None),
                    ..Default::default()
                },
            )
            .await?;
        // The sender is still the current step with its send-back not performed: the move on
        // from it performs it, now within the cap.
        self.advance(id).await?;
        let wr = self.store.get_workflow_run(id).await?;
        self.updated(&wr);
        Ok(wr)
    }

    /// Moves the workflow run on from `step`, its current step, whose newest step run has
    /// succeeded: performs the send-back that step run asks for, else begins the step after
    /// it (past the last, the workflow run has succeeded).
    pub(super) async fn move_on(&self, wr: &WorkflowRun, step: &Step) -> Result<()> {
        let send = latest(wr, &step.id)
            .filter(|s| s.status == StepRunStatus::Succeeded)
            .and_then(|s| asked(wr, step, s));
        match send {
            Some(send) => self.perform_send_back(wr, send).await,
            None => self.begin_or_finish(wr, next_of(wr, &step.id)).await,
        }
    }

    /// Performs `send` (section 5.4) within the cap (section 9). At the cap the workflow run
    /// waits on `send_backs` with `send` held. Otherwise the target gets a new step run and
    /// becomes the current step as the send-back is counted, then its first run starts
    /// (`start_agent`: past a limit of the budget, it waits on budget instead).
    async fn perform_send_back(&self, wr: &WorkflowRun, send: SendBack) -> Result<()> {
        let max = wr.budget.max_send_backs;
        if wr.send_backs >= max {
            let waiting = self
                .store
                .set_workflow_run(
                    &wr.id,
                    &WorkflowRunChange {
                        status: Some(WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting),
                        waiting_on: Some(Some(WaitingOn::SendBacks)),
                        error: Some(Some(format!(
                            "Send-back limit reached: {} of {max}",
                            wr.send_backs
                        ))),
                        send_back: Some(Some(SendBack { held: true, ..send })),
                        ..Default::default()
                    },
                )
                .await?;
            self.updated(&waiting);
            return Ok(());
        }
        let Some(target) = wr.definition.step(&send.to).filter(is_agent) else {
            return Err(Error::Other(format!(
                "step {} is not an agent step of the workflow",
                send.to
            )));
        };
        // A target running while a later step is current is the step run of this send-back,
        // made by a move cut off before the workflow run moved to it: every step before the
        // current one has ended otherwise.
        let step_run = match latest(wr, &target.id) {
            Some(s) if s.status == StepRunStatus::Running => s.clone(),
            _ => {
                self.store
                    .create_step_run(
                        &wr.id,
                        &target.id,
                        next_attempt(wr, &target.id),
                        StepRunStatus::Running,
                    )
                    .await?
            }
        };
        // One write: the target becomes the current step as the send-back is counted, so a
        // pass never performs it twice.
        let wr = self
            .store
            .set_workflow_run(
                &wr.id,
                &WorkflowRunChange {
                    status: Some(WorkflowRunStatus::Running),
                    waiting_on: Some(None),
                    error: Some(None),
                    current_step: Some(Some(target.id.clone())),
                    send_backs: Some(wr.send_backs + 1),
                    send_back: Some(Some(send)),
                    ..Default::default()
                },
            )
            .await?;
        self.start_agent(&wr, target, &step_run).await?;
        self.updated(&self.store.get_workflow_run(&wr.id).await?);
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Queues the first run of the agent step run `step_run`. A step that succeeded earlier
    /// in this workflow run runs again only after a send-back: the last run of its newest
    /// succeeded step run's own thread is continued, with the same session, worktree and
    /// branch (for a delegate step, the delegating run). The target of the workflow run's last
    /// send-back is told the note; a step after it gets the newest hand-off before it, and a
    /// worktree it no longer has is made again from that hand-off's tip. A last run without a
    /// session starts afresh, from its old branch while that exists, and the step run's notice
    /// says so. Any other step starts afresh as usual (`queue_agent_run`).
    pub(super) async fn queue_first(
        &self,
        wr: &WorkflowRun,
        step: &Step,
        step_run: &StepRun,
    ) -> Result<()> {
        let earlier = wr.steps.iter().rev().find(|s| {
            s.step_id == step.id && s.id != step_run.id && s.status == StepRunStatus::Succeeded
        });
        let Some(earlier) = earlier else {
            self.queue_agent_run(wr, step, step_run, None).await?;
            return Ok(());
        };
        let runs = self.store.runs_of_step_run(&earlier.id).await?;
        let members = self.store.step_members(&earlier.id).await?;
        let last = own_runs(&runs, &members).last().copied();
        let repo = Path::new(&wr.project.repo_path);
        let branch = last.and_then(|r| r.branch.clone());
        let kept = match &branch {
            Some(b) => branch_exists(repo, b).await,
            None => false,
        };
        let target = wr.send_back.as_ref().filter(|s| !s.held && s.to == step.id);
        let message = match target {
            Some(send) => sent_message(wr, send),
            None => rerun_message(wr, step, kept),
        };
        match last.filter(|r| r.session_id.is_some()) {
            Some(last) => {
                let mut new = crate::checkins::continuation(last, last, message, None);
                new.workflow_step_run_id = Some(step_run.id.clone());
                // A step after the target whose branch is gone (it had no changes): the
                // runner makes its worktree again on its branch from the base commit, so the
                // base is the tip it starts from now, which holds the new work.
                if target.is_none() && branch.is_some() && !kept {
                    if let Some(start) = start_branch(wr, &step.id) {
                        let commit = format!("{start}^{{commit}}");
                        let args = ["rev-parse", "--verify", "--end-of-options", &commit];
                        if let Ok(tip) = git(repo, &args).await {
                            new.base_commit = Some(tip);
                        }
                    }
                }
                self.runner.enqueue(new).await?;
            }
            None => {
                let old = branch.filter(|_| kept);
                let (start, notice) = match &old {
                    Some(b) => (
                        Some(format!("refs/heads/{b}")),
                        format!("Started again from \"{b}\": {NOT_RESUMED}"),
                    ),
                    None => (
                        start_branch(wr, &step.id),
                        format!("Started again from the start: {NOT_RESUMED}"),
                    ),
                };
                self.store
                    .set_step_run(
                        &step_run.id,
                        &StepRunChange {
                            notice: Some(Some(notice)),
                            ..Default::default()
                        },
                    )
                    .await?;
                let again = Again { start, message };
                self.queue_agent_run(wr, step, step_run, Some(&again))
                    .await?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
```

### Task 7: Deploys push, open and auto-merge pull requests, refusing secrets

`deploy.rs` does a deploy for a list of approved branches: it checks each tip is the approved commit, refuses secrets and files over 10 MiB before any push, pushes the approved commit, then opens a pull request (or reuses an open one) and sets it to auto-merge, by mode. `fake-gh` stands in for `gh` in the tests: it logs its arguments and answers by `FAKE_GH_MODE`. Nothing calls the deployer yet.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/dots-core/src/deploy.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/bin/fake-gh.rs`
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/lib.rs` (the module)
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/deploy_test.rs` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `workflow::{DeployMode, MergeMethod}`; `workspace::default_branch_ref`; `vault::redact::global()`.
- Produces: `deploy::{TOOL_TIMEOUT: Duration (5 min), MAX_FILE_BYTES: u64 (10 × 1024 × 1024), SECRET_NAMES: &[&str] ("!" marks an exception), BRANCH_CHANGED, GH_MISSING, Deployer { gh: PathBuf } (Default: "gh"), ApprovedBranch { branch, commit, base: Option<String> }, DeployedBranch { branch, commit, pushed: bool, pr_url: Option<String>, auto_merge: bool }, DeployResult { branches: Vec<DeployedBranch>, ready: bool }}`; `Deployer::deploy(&self, repo: &Path, remote: &str, base: &str, mode: &DeployMode, branches: &[ApprovedBranch], title: &str, body: &str) -> Result<DeployResult, String>`; the bin `fake-gh` (`FAKE_GH_LOG`; `FAKE_GH_MODE` `ok` (default), `existing`, `fail-create`, `fail-merge`), found by tests as `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_fake-gh")`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

**Create `crates/dots-core/tests/deploy_test.rs`:**

```rust
//! Deploys against a local bare repository as the remote and the fake `gh`: no network.

mod common;

use std::path::PathBuf;

use common::{git_out, init_repo};
use dots_core::deploy::{ApprovedBranch, Deployer, BRANCH_CHANGED, GH_MISSING};
use dots_core::workflow::{DeployMode, MergeMethod};
use tempfile::TempDir;

/// `FAKE_GH_LOG` and `FAKE_GH_MODE` are process-wide: a test that runs the fake `gh` holds this
/// for as long as it sets them and deploys.
static GH: tokio::sync::Mutex<()> = tokio::sync::Mutex::const_new(());

const MIB: usize = 1024 * 1024;

/// A repository on `main` with a bare repository as its `origin`.
struct Scratch {
    dir: TempDir,
    repo: PathBuf,
    remote: PathBuf,
}

fn scratch() -> Scratch {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let repo = dir.path().join("repo");
    let remote = dir.path().join("remote.git");
    init_repo(&repo);
    git_out(dir.path(), &["init", "-q", "--bare", "remote.git"]);
    git_out(
        &repo,
        &["remote", "add", "origin", remote.to_str().unwrap()],
    );
    git_out(&repo, &["push", "-q", "origin", "main"]);
    Scratch { dir, repo, remote }
}

impl Scratch {
    /// Commits `files` (path, content; `None` deletes) on `branch`, made from `main` when it does
    /// not exist yet, and returns the new tip.
    fn commit(&self, branch: &str, files: &[(&str, Option<&[u8]>)]) -> String {
        let exists = git_out(&self.repo, &["branch", "--list", branch]);
        if exists.is_empty() {
            git_out(&self.repo, &["checkout", "-q", "-b", branch, "main"]);
        } else {
            git_out(&self.repo, &["checkout", "-q", branch]);
        }
        for (path, content) in files {
            let file = self.repo.join(path);
            match content {
                Some(bytes) => {
                    std::fs::create_dir_all(file.parent().unwrap()).unwrap();
                    std::fs::write(&file, bytes).unwrap();
                }
                None => std::fs::remove_file(&file).unwrap(),
            }
        }
        git_out(&self.repo, &["add", "-A"]);
        git_out(&self.repo, &["commit", "-q", "-m", "work"]);
        let tip = git_out(&self.repo, &["rev-parse", "HEAD"]);
        git_out(&self.repo, &["checkout", "-q", "main"]);
        tip
    }

    /// The commit `branch` points at on the remote, if it is there.
    fn remote_tip(&self, branch: &str) -> Option<String> {
        let out = std::process::Command::new("git")
            .arg("-C")
            .arg(&self.remote)
            .args(["rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet"])
            .arg(format!("refs/heads/{branch}"))
            .output()
            .unwrap();
        out.status
            .success()
            .then(|| String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string())
    }

    fn log(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.dir.path().join("gh.log")
    }

    /// The fake `gh`'s calls, one argument list each.
    fn gh_calls(&self) -> Vec<Vec<String>> {
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(self.log()).unwrap_or_default();
        text.lines()
            .map(|l| serde_json::from_str(l).unwrap())
            .collect()
    }

    /// Deploys `branches` with the fake `gh` in `mode`.
    async fn deploy(
        &self,
        gh_mode: &str,
        mode: &DeployMode,
        branches: &[ApprovedBranch],
    ) -> Result<dots_core::deploy::DeployResult, String> {
        let _held = GH.lock().await;
        std::env::set_var("FAKE_GH_LOG", self.log());
        std::env::set_var("FAKE_GH_MODE", gh_mode);
        fake_gh()
            .deploy(
                &self.repo,
                "origin",
                "main",
                mode,
                branches,
                "Fix the bug\nThe rest of the task.",
                "The summary.",
            )
            .await
    }
}

fn fake_gh() -> Deployer {
    Deployer {
        gh: PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_fake-gh")),
    }
}

fn approved(branch: &str, commit: &str) -> ApprovedBranch {
    ApprovedBranch {
        branch: branch.to_string(),
        commit: commit.to_string(),
        base: None,
    }
}

fn strings(words: &[&str]) -> Vec<String> {
    words.iter().map(|w| w.to_string()).collect()
}

/// The fake's calls of `gh pr <sub>`.
fn calls_of(calls: &[Vec<String>], sub: &str) -> Vec<Vec<String>> {
    calls
        .iter()
        .filter(|c| c.len() > 1 && c[0] == "pr" && c[1] == sub)
        .cloned()
        .collect()
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn push_lands_the_approved_commit_on_the_remote() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let result = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), Some(tip.clone()));
    assert_eq!(
        serde_json::to_value(&result).unwrap(),
        serde_json::json!({
            "branches": [{"branch": "dots/fix", "commit": tip, "pushed": true, "pr_url": null,
                "auto_merge": false}],
            "ready": false,
        })
    );
    assert!(s.gh_calls().is_empty(), "push runs no gh");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn branch_mode_pushes_nothing_and_lists_the_branches_as_ready() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let result = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Branch, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert!(result.ready);
    assert_eq!(result.branches.len(), 1);
    assert!(!result.branches[0].pushed);
    assert_eq!(result.branches[0].commit, tip);
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), None);
    assert!(s.gh_calls().is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn pr_pushes_then_opens_a_pull_request_against_the_base() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let result = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Pr, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), Some(tip));
    let b = &result.branches[0];
    assert!(b.pushed);
    assert_eq!(b.pr_url.as_deref(), Some("https://github.com/o/r/pull/1"));
    assert!(!b.auto_merge);
    assert!(!result.ready);
    let calls = s.gh_calls();
    assert_eq!(calls[0], strings(&["--version"]));
    let view = calls_of(&calls, "view");
    assert_eq!(view.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(view[0][2], "dots/fix");
    assert_eq!(
        calls_of(&calls, "create"),
        vec![strings(&[
            "pr",
            "create",
            "--head",
            "dots/fix",
            "--base",
            "main",
            "--title",
            "Fix the bug",
            "--body",
            "The summary.",
        ])]
    );
    assert!(calls_of(&calls, "merge").is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_long_title_is_cut_to_256_characters() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let title = "é".repeat(300);
    {
        let _held = GH.lock().await;
        std::env::set_var("FAKE_GH_LOG", s.log());
        std::env::set_var("FAKE_GH_MODE", "ok");
        fake_gh()
            .deploy(
                &s.repo,
                "origin",
                "main",
                &DeployMode::Pr,
                &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)],
                &title,
                "",
            )
            .await
            .unwrap();
    }
    let create = calls_of(&s.gh_calls(), "create");
    assert_eq!(create[0][7], "é".repeat(256));
    assert_eq!(create[0][9], "");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_open_pull_request_for_the_branch_is_reused() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let result = s
        .deploy("existing", &DeployMode::Pr, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        result.branches[0].pr_url.as_deref(),
        Some("https://github.com/o/r/pull/7")
    );
    assert!(calls_of(&s.gh_calls(), "create").is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn pr_merge_sets_auto_merge_with_the_merge_method() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let squash = DeployMode::PrMerge {
        merge_method: MergeMethod::default(),
    };
    let result = s
        .deploy("ok", &squash, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert!(result.branches[0].auto_merge);
    assert_eq!(
        calls_of(&s.gh_calls(), "merge"),
        vec![strings(&[
            "pr",
            "merge",
            "https://github.com/o/r/pull/1",
            "--auto",
            "--squash",
        ])]
    );

    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let rebase = DeployMode::PrMerge {
        merge_method: MergeMethod::Rebase,
    };
    s.deploy("ok", &rebase, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let merge = calls_of(&s.gh_calls(), "merge");
    assert_eq!(merge[0][3..], strings(&["--auto", "--rebase"]));
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_failed_pr_create_fails_with_ghs_message_and_keeps_the_push() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let err = s
        .deploy(
            "fail-create",
            &DeployMode::Pr,
            &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)],
        )
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert!(
        err.starts_with("gh pr create failed: pull request create failed: GraphQL:"),
        "{err}"
    );
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), Some(tip), "nothing is undone");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_failed_auto_merge_fails_with_ghs_message_and_keeps_the_push() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let mode = DeployMode::PrMerge {
        merge_method: MergeMethod::Squash,
    };
    let err = s
        .deploy("fail-merge", &mode, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(
        err,
        "gh pr merge failed: GraphQL: Pull request Auto merge is not allowed for this \
         repository (enablePullRequestAutoMerge)"
    );
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), Some(tip), "nothing is undone");
    assert_eq!(calls_of(&s.gh_calls(), "create").len(), 1);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_missing_gh_fails_before_anything_is_pushed() {
    let s = scratch();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let deployer = Deployer {
        gh: s.dir.path().join("no-such-gh.exe"),
    };
    let err = deployer
        .deploy(
            &s.repo,
            "origin",
            "main",
            &DeployMode::Pr,
            &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)],
            "Fix the bug",
            "The summary.",
        )
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(err, GH_MISSING);
    assert_eq!(err, "gh is not installed or not on the PATH");
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), None);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_branch_moved_after_approval_is_refused_and_nothing_is_pushed() {
    let s = scratch();
    let first = s.commit("dots/a", &[("a.txt", Some(b"a\n"))]);
    let approved_tip = s.commit("dots/b", &[("b.txt", Some(b"b\n"))]);
    s.commit("dots/b", &[("b.txt", Some(b"b, later\n"))]);
    let branches = [
        approved("dots/a", &first),
        approved("dots/b", &approved_tip),
    ];
    let err = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, &branches)
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(err, BRANCH_CHANGED);
    assert_eq!(err, "The branch changed after it was approved.");
    assert_eq!(
        s.remote_tip("dots/a"),
        None,
        "the unchanged branch waits too"
    );
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/b"), None);

    let gone = approved("dots/gone", &first);
    let err = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Branch, &[gone])
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(err, BRANCH_CHANGED);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn files_that_look_like_secrets_or_are_too_big_refuse_the_deploy() {
    let s = scratch();
    let big = vec![b'x'; 10 * MIB + 1];
    let exact = vec![b'x'; 10 * MIB];
    let tip = s.commit(
        "dots/fix",
        &[
            (".env", Some(b"TOKEN=1\n")),
            (".env.example", Some(b"TOKEN=\n")),
            ("config/id_rsa", Some(b"key\n")),
            ("x.PEM", Some(b"pem\n")),
            ("big.bin", Some(&big)),
            ("exact.bin", Some(&exact)),
            ("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n")),
        ],
    );
    let err = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Pr, &[approved("dots/fix", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(
        err,
        "Not pushed: dots/fix holds files that look like secrets or are over 10 MB: .env, \
         big.bin, config/id_rsa, x.PEM. Remove them from the branch, then retry the step."
    );
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), None);
    assert!(s.gh_calls().is_empty(), "refused before gh runs");

    let allowed = s.commit(
        "dots/ok",
        &[
            (".env.example", Some(b"TOKEN=\n")),
            ("docs/.ENV.sample", Some(b"TOKEN=\n")),
            ("exact.bin", Some(&exact)),
        ],
    );
    s.deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, &[approved("dots/ok", &allowed)])
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/ok"), Some(allowed));
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_refusal_names_ten_files_and_counts_the_rest() {
    let s = scratch();
    let names: Vec<String> = (0..12).map(|i| format!("k{i:02}.key")).collect();
    let files: Vec<(&str, Option<&[u8]>)> = names
        .iter()
        .map(|n| (n.as_str(), Some(b"k\n".as_slice())))
        .collect();
    let tip = s.commit("dots/keys", &files);
    let err = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, &[approved("dots/keys", &tip)])
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    let listed = names[..10].join(", ");
    assert_eq!(
        err,
        format!(
            "Not pushed: dots/keys holds files that look like secrets or are over 10 MB: \
             {listed} and 2 more. Remove them from the branch, then retry the step."
        )
    );
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_secret_added_and_then_deleted_on_the_branch_is_not_refused() {
    let s = scratch();
    let base = git_out(&s.repo, &["rev-parse", "main"]);
    s.commit("dots/fix", &[(".env", Some(b"TOKEN=1\n"))]);
    let tip = s.commit(
        "dots/fix",
        &[(".env", None), ("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))],
    );
    let mut branch = approved("dots/fix", &tip);
    s.deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, std::slice::from_ref(&branch))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), Some(tip.clone()));

    // An explicit base is scanned from, in place of the merge-base.
    branch.base = Some(base);
    s.deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, &[branch]).await.unwrap();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn the_scan_starts_from_the_given_base() {
    let s = scratch();
    let with_secret = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("id_ed25519.pub", Some(b"ssh-ed25519\n"))]);
    let tip = s.commit("dots/fix", &[("src/fix.rs", Some(b"fn fix() {}\n"))]);
    let mut branch = approved("dots/fix", &tip);
    let err = s
        .deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, std::slice::from_ref(&branch))
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert!(err.contains(": id_ed25519.pub. Remove"), "{err}");
    // From the commit that added it, only `src/fix.rs` changed.
    branch.base = Some(with_secret);
    s.deploy("ok", &DeployMode::Push, &[branch]).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(s.remote_tip("dots/fix"), Some(tip));
}
```

**Create `crates/dots-core/src/bin/fake-gh.rs`:**

```rust
//! Test stand-in for the GitHub CLI `gh`, for the deploy tests: it never reaches the network.
//!
//! Each call appends its arguments, as one JSON array of strings, to the file named in
//! `FAKE_GH_LOG` (when set). `FAKE_GH_MODE` selects the answers (default `ok`):
//! - `ok`: `pr view` prints nothing and exits 1 (the branch has no pull request), `pr create`
//!   prints `https://github.com/o/r/pull/1`, `pr merge` succeeds.
//! - `existing`: as `ok`, but `pr view` prints `https://github.com/o/r/pull/7`.
//! - `fail-create`: `pr create` fails as gh does, with a message on stderr and exit code 1.
//! - `fail-merge`: `pr merge` fails as gh does for a repository without auto-merge.
//!
//! `--version` always succeeds.

use std::io::Write as _;

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = std::env::args().skip(1).collect();
    if let Some(log) = std::env::var_os("FAKE_GH_LOG") {
        let mut file = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
            .create(true)
            .append(true)
            .open(log)
            .unwrap();
        writeln!(file, "{}", serde_json::to_string(&args).unwrap()).unwrap();
    }
    let mode = std::env::var("FAKE_GH_MODE").unwrap_or_else(|_| "ok".to_string());
    let words: Vec<&str> = args.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();
    match (words.as_slice(), mode.as_str()) {
        (["--version", ..], _) => println!("gh version 2.62.0 (fake)"),
        (["pr", "view", ..], "existing") => println!("https://github.com/o/r/pull/7"),
        (["pr", "view", branch, ..], _) => {
            eprintln!("no pull requests found for branch \"{branch}\"");
            std::process::exit(1);
        }
        (["pr", "create", ..], "fail-create") => {
            eprintln!("pull request create failed: GraphQL: Head sha can't be blank, Base sha can't be blank, No commits between main and dots/fix (createPullRequest)");
            std::process::exit(1);
        }
        (["pr", "create", ..], _) => println!("https://github.com/o/r/pull/1"),
        (["pr", "merge", ..], "fail-merge") => {
            eprintln!("GraphQL: Pull request Auto merge is not allowed for this repository (enablePullRequestAutoMerge)");
            std::process::exit(1);
        }
        (["pr", "merge", ..], _) => {}
        _ => {
            eprintln!("unknown command {words:?}");
            std::process::exit(1);
        }
    }
}
```

**Create `crates/dots-core/src/deploy.rs`:**

```rust
//! Deploys (spec 5.5): Dots, not an agent, pushes the branches a check approved, opens a pull
//! request for each and sets it to merge on its own, so what is pushed is exactly what the
//! person approved. `git` and `gh` run as the person, with their own credentials, which Dots
//! never reads. Nothing already pushed is undone when a later part fails: the step fails with
//! the tool's message and the person retries it.

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::{Output, Stdio};
use std::time::Duration;

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use tokio::io::AsyncWriteExt;
use tokio::process::Command;

use crate::workflow::{DeployMode, MergeMethod};

/// The longest one `git` or `gh` call of a deploy may take (a slow remote, a credential helper
/// waiting): past it the call is stopped (`kill_on_drop`) and the deploy fails.
pub const TOOL_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5 * 60);

/// The largest file a deploy pushes: a bigger one is refused as the secrets are (10 MiB).
pub const MAX_FILE_BYTES: u64 = 10 * 1024 * 1024;

/// The file names a deploy refuses to push (the person's decision of 2026-11-02), matched
/// against the last part of each path, ignoring case. An entry is a whole name (`.netrc`), a
/// name start ending in `*` (`id_rsa*`) or a name end starting with `*` (`*.pem`). An entry that
/// starts with `!` is an exception: a name it matches is allowed whatever else matches it.
pub const SECRET_NAMES: &[&str] = &[
    ".env",
    ".env.*",
    "!.env.example",
    "!.env.sample",
    "!.env.template",
    "*.pem",
    "*.key",
    "*.p12",
    "*.pfx",
    "id_rsa*",
    "id_dsa*",
    "id_ecdsa*",
    "id_ed25519*",
    ".netrc",
    ".npmrc",
    ".pypirc",
    "credentials.json",
];

/// The error of a deploy whose branch no longer points at the commit its check approved.
pub const BRANCH_CHANGED: &str = "The branch changed after it was approved.";

/// The error of a `pr` or `pr_merge` deploy when `gh` cannot be started.
pub const GH_MISSING: &str = "gh is not installed or not on the PATH";

/// Files a refused deploy names; the rest are counted.
const LISTED_FILES: usize = 10;
/// The longest pull request title, in characters.
const TITLE_CHARS: usize = 256;
/// The lines of a tool's error output a failed deploy quotes.
const ERROR_LINES: usize = 3;
/// The longest quote of a tool's error output, in characters.
const ERROR_CHARS: usize = 600;

/// Runs deploys. `gh` is the GitHub CLI to run: `"gh"` (found on the PATH) unless a test sets
/// another program.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Deployer {
    pub gh: PathBuf,
}

impl Default for Deployer {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Deployer {
            gh: PathBuf::from("gh"),
        }
    }
}

/// A branch a check approved, at the commit it showed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct ApprovedBranch {
    pub branch: String,
    pub commit: String,
    /// The commit the branch's work started from, for the scan of the files it changed. `None`:
    /// the merge-base of the branch with the project's default branch.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub base: Option<String>,
}

/// What a deploy did to one branch.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct DeployedBranch {
    pub branch: String,
    pub commit: String,
    pub pushed: bool,
    pub pr_url: Option<String>,
    pub auto_merge: bool,
}

/// What a deploy did, kept as the step run's `deploy_result`. `ready` is set when the branches
/// were kept as they are and listed as ready (mode `branch`) rather than pushed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct DeployResult {
    pub branches: Vec<DeployedBranch>,
    pub ready: bool,
}

impl Deployer {
    /// Deploys `branches` of the repository at `repo` as `mode` says: `branch` (and `member`,
    /// which the workflow turns into an agent step before it gets here) pushes nothing; `push`
    /// pushes each branch to `remote`; `pr` also opens a pull request against `base` (or reuses
    /// the open one, so a retry does not fail) titled with the first line of `title` and with
    /// `body` as its text; `pr_merge` also sets it to merge once GitHub allows. Before anything
    /// is pushed, every branch must still be at its approved commit and must hold no file that
    /// looks like a secret or is too big. `Err` is the step's error.
    #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
    pub async fn deploy(
        &self,
        repo: &Path,
        remote: &str,
        base: &str,
        mode: &DeployMode,
        branches: &[ApprovedBranch],
        title: &str,
        body: &str,
    ) -> Result<DeployResult, String> {
        for b in branches {
            check_tip(repo, b).await?;
        }
        let mut result = DeployResult {
            branches: branches
                .iter()
                .map(|b| DeployedBranch {
                    branch: b.branch.clone(),
                    commit: b.commit.clone(),
                    pushed: false,
                    pr_url: None,
                    auto_merge: false,
                })
                .collect(),
            ready: false,
        };
        let (pr, merge) = match mode {
            DeployMode::Branch | DeployMode::Member { .. } => {
                result.ready = true;
                return Ok(result);
            }
            DeployMode::Push => (false, None),
            DeployMode::Pr => (true, None),
            DeployMode::PrMerge { merge_method } => (true, Some(*merge_method)),
        };
        for b in branches {
            guard(repo, remote, base, b).await?;
        }
        // Found missing before the first push, so a deploy that cannot open its pull requests
        // pushes nothing.
        if pr {
            self.gh(repo, "gh", &["--version"]).await?;
        }
        for (b, done) in branches.iter().zip(&mut result.branches) {
            push(repo, remote, b).await?;
            done.pushed = true;
            if !pr {
                continue;
            }
            let url = match self.open_pr(repo, &b.branch).await? {
                Some(url) => url,
                None => self.create_pr(repo, base, &b.branch, title, body).await?,
            };
            done.pr_url = Some(url.clone());
            if let Some(method) = merge {
                let method = match method {
                    MergeMethod::Merge => "--merge",
                    MergeMethod::Squash => "--squash",
                    MergeMethod::Rebase => "--rebase",
                };
                let args = ["pr", "merge", url.as_str(), "--auto", method];
                let out = self.gh(repo, "gh pr merge", &args).await?;
                succeeded("gh pr merge", &out)?;
                done.auto_merge = true;
            }
        }
        Ok(result)
    }

    /// The URL of the open pull request for `branch`, if there is one. A failed lookup counts
    /// as none: creating one then fails with gh's own message.
    async fn open_pr(&self, repo: &Path, branch: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, String> {
        // `gh pr view <branch>` also finds a closed or merged pull request: only an open one is
        // reused, so a branch pushed again after a merge gets a new one.
        let args = [
            "pr",
            "view",
            branch,
            "--json",
            "url,state",
            "--jq",
            "select(.state == \"OPEN\") | .url",
        ];
        let out = self.gh(repo, "gh pr view", &args).await?;
        let url = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string();
        Ok((out.status.success() && !url.is_empty()).then_some(url))
    }

    /// Opens a pull request of `branch` into `base` and returns its URL, which `gh` prints as
    /// its last line.
    async fn create_pr(
        &self,
        repo: &Path,
        base: &str,
        branch: &str,
        title: &str,
        body: &str,
    ) -> Result<String, String> {
        let title = pr_title(title, branch);
        let args = [
            "pr", "create", "--head", branch, "--base", base, "--title", &title, "--body", body,
        ];
        let out = self.gh(repo, "gh pr create", &args).await?;
        let stdout = succeeded("gh pr create", &out)?;
        stdout
            .lines()
            .map(str::trim)
            .rfind(|l| !l.is_empty())
            .map(str::to_string)
            .ok_or_else(|| "gh pr create printed no pull request URL.".to_string())
    }

    /// Runs `gh` in `repo`, without prompts. Exiting with an error is not an `Err` here: the
    /// caller decides what it means.
    async fn gh(&self, repo: &Path, tool: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<Output, String> {
        let mut cmd = Command::new(&self.gh);
        cmd.args(args)
            .current_dir(repo)
            .env("GH_PROMPT_DISABLED", "1");
        run(tool, &mut cmd, None).await
    }
}

/// Refuses `b` unless its branch still points at the approved commit.
async fn check_tip(repo: &Path, b: &ApprovedBranch) -> Result<(), String> {
    let commit = format!("refs/heads/{}^{{commit}}", b.branch);
    let args = [
        "rev-parse",
        "--verify",
        "--quiet",
        "--end-of-options",
        &commit,
    ];
    let out = run("git rev-parse", &mut git(repo, &args), None).await?;
    let tip = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string();
    if !out.status.success() || !tip.eq_ignore_ascii_case(b.commit.trim()) {
        return Err(BRANCH_CHANGED.to_string());
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// The secret and size check of one branch: the files changed between its base and the
/// approved commit that are still there at the commit. A file added and then deleted on the
/// branch is not pushed as a file of the tip, so it is not looked at.
async fn guard(repo: &Path, remote: &str, base: &str, b: &ApprovedBranch) -> Result<(), String> {
    let from = match &b.base {
        Some(from) => from.clone(),
        None => {
            let default = crate::workspace::default_branch_ref(repo, base, remote).await;
            let args = ["merge-base", "--end-of-options", &default, &b.commit];
            let out = run("git merge-base", &mut git(repo, &args), None).await?;
            succeeded("git merge-base", &out)?.trim().to_string()
        }
    };
    // Plumbing, so no diff setting of the repository changes the output; `d` leaves deleted
    // files out, and with renames off a renamed file shows as added under its new name.
    let args = [
        "diff-tree",
        "-r",
        "-z",
        "--no-renames",
        "--diff-filter=d",
        "--end-of-options",
        &from,
        &b.commit,
    ];
    let out = run("git diff-tree", &mut git(repo, &args), None).await?;
    let raw = succeeded("git diff-tree", &out)?;
    let mut files: Vec<(String, Option<String>)> = Vec::new();
    let mut fields = raw.split('\0');
    while let Some(meta) = fields.next() {
        let Some(path) = fields.next() else { break };
        let meta: Vec<&str> = meta.trim_start_matches(':').split(' ').collect();
        // `:<old mode> <new mode> <old id> <new id> <status>`; a submodule (mode 160000) is a
        // commit of another repository, not a file with a size.
        let blob = match (meta.get(1), meta.get(3)) {
            (Some(&mode), Some(&id)) if mode != "160000" => Some(id.to_string()),
            _ => None,
        };
        files.push((path.to_string(), blob));
    }
    let ids: String = files
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|(_, id)| id.as_deref())
        .map(|id| format!("{id}\n"))
        .collect();
    let mut sizes = std::collections::HashMap::new();
    if !ids.is_empty() {
        let args = ["cat-file", "--batch-check=%(objectname) %(objectsize)"];
        let out = run(
            "git cat-file",
            &mut git(repo, &args),
            Some(ids.into_bytes()),
        )
        .await?;
        for line in succeeded("git cat-file", &out)?.lines() {
            if let Some((id, size)) = line.split_once(' ') {
                if let Ok(size) = size.trim().parse::<u64>() {
                    sizes.insert(id.to_string(), size);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    let refused: Vec<&str> = files
        .iter()
        .filter(|(path, id)| {
            looks_secret(path)
                || id
                    .as_ref()
                    .and_then(|id| sizes.get(id))
                    .is_some_and(|&size| size > MAX_FILE_BYTES)
        })
        .map(|(path, _)| path.as_str())
        .collect();
    if refused.is_empty() {
        return Ok(());
    }
    let mut list = refused[..refused.len().min(LISTED_FILES)].join(", ");
    if refused.len() > LISTED_FILES {
        list.push_str(&format!(" and {} more", refused.len() - LISTED_FILES));
    }
    Err(format!(
        "Not pushed: {} holds files that look like secrets or are over 10 MB: {list}. Remove \
         them from the branch, then retry the step.",
        b.branch
    ))
}

/// Whether the file name of `path` matches [`SECRET_NAMES`].
fn looks_secret(path: &str) -> bool {
    let name = path.rsplit('/').next().unwrap_or(path).to_lowercase();
    let matches = |pattern: &str| {
        if let Some(end) = pattern.strip_prefix('*') {
            name.ends_with(end)
        } else if let Some(start) = pattern.strip_suffix('*') {
            name.starts_with(start)
        } else {
            name == pattern
        }
    };
    let mut secret = false;
    for pattern in SECRET_NAMES {
        match pattern.strip_prefix('!') {
            Some(allowed) if matches(allowed) => return false,
            Some(_) => {}
            None => secret |= matches(pattern),
        }
    }
    secret
}

/// Pushes the approved commit as the branch, without prompts. The commit, not the branch, is
/// the source: a commit made after the tip check is never pushed.
async fn push(repo: &Path, remote: &str, b: &ApprovedBranch) -> Result<(), String> {
    let refspec = format!("{}:refs/heads/{}", b.commit, b.branch);
    let args = ["push", "--end-of-options", remote, &refspec];
    let mut cmd = git(repo, &args);
    cmd.env("GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT", "0");
    let out = run("git push", &mut cmd, None).await?;
    succeeded("git push", &out).map(drop)
}

/// `git -C <repo> <args>`.
fn git(repo: &Path, args: &[&str]) -> Command {
    let mut cmd = Command::new("git");
    cmd.arg("-C").arg(repo).args(args);
    cmd
}

/// The pull request title: the first line of `title`, at most [`TITLE_CHARS`] characters; the
/// branch name when that is blank (gh refuses an empty title).
fn pr_title(title: &str, branch: &str) -> String {
    let line = title.lines().next().unwrap_or("").trim();
    if line.is_empty() {
        return branch.to_string();
    }
    line.chars().take(TITLE_CHARS).collect()
}

/// Runs `cmd` to the end within [`TOOL_TIMEOUT`], feeding it `input`, and returns what it
/// printed. `tool` names it in the errors.
async fn run(tool: &str, cmd: &mut Command, input: Option<Vec<u8>>) -> Result<Output, String> {
    cmd.stdin(if input.is_some() {
        Stdio::piped()
    } else {
        Stdio::null()
    })
    .stdout(Stdio::piped())
    .stderr(Stdio::piped())
    .kill_on_drop(true);
    crate::proc::hide_window(cmd);
    let mut child = cmd.spawn().map_err(|e| {
        if tool.starts_with("gh") && e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound {
            GH_MISSING.to_string()
        } else {
            format!("{tool} could not start: {e}")
        }
    })?;
    // Written from a task of its own, so a tool that answers while it reads never blocks on a
    // full output pipe.
    if let (Some(input), Some(mut stdin)) = (input, child.stdin.take()) {
        tokio::spawn(async move {
            let _ = stdin.write_all(&input).await;
        });
    }
    tokio::time::timeout(TOOL_TIMEOUT, child.wait_with_output())
        .await
        .map_err(|_| format!("{tool} did not finish within 5 minutes."))?
        .map_err(|e| format!("{tool} failed: {e}"))
}

/// The stdout of a tool that succeeded, else its error: the tool and the first lines of what it
/// wrote to stderr, with secret values scrubbed out.
fn succeeded(tool: &str, out: &Output) -> Result<String, String> {
    if out.status.success() {
        return Ok(String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).into_owned());
    }
    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr);
    let stderr = crate::vault::redact::global().scrub(&stderr);
    let lines: Vec<&str> = stderr
        .lines()
        .map(str::trim)
        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
        .take(ERROR_LINES)
        .collect();
    let message = if lines.is_empty() {
        format!("exit code {}", out.status.code().unwrap_or(-1))
    } else {
        lines.join(" ")
    };
    let message: String = message.chars().take(ERROR_CHARS).collect();
    Err(format!("{tool} failed: {message}"))
}
```

---

### Task 8: Deploy steps act on the branches the check approved

A check's approval now records the tips it showed (`{"branch", "commit"}`). A deploy step acts on the newest check before it on its path: a job (outside the lock) runs `Deployer::deploy` with the project's remote and default branch, the task as title and the check's hand-off summary as body, and stores `deploy_result`. A test run deploys as `branch`. A `member` deploy runs as an agent step. A deploy found running with no job after a restart fails `interrupted`. A failed deploy fails the step, and Retry runs it again.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/dots-core/src/workflows/deploy_step.rs`
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/migrations/0027_teams_b.sql`, `crates/dots-core/src/{deploy.rs,model.rs,workflows.rs,workflows/jobs.rs,store/workflow_runs.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/workflows_test.rs`; brought up to date: `crates/dots-core/tests/deploy_test.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 7's `Deployer`, `ApprovedBranch`, `DeployResult`; Task 3's `start_job`, `still_open`, `has_job`, `through_gate`; Task 6's `move_on`, `start_agent`; Task 2's `ran_before`, `handoffs_before`, `start_branch`.
- Produces: `StepRun.deploy_result: Option<DeployResult>`, `StepRunChange.deploy_result: Option<Option<DeployResult>>`; `Deployer.env: Vec<(String, String)>` (set on each gh call; changes Task 7's type); `Workflows::set_deployer(&self, deployer: Deployer)` (once; unset, `Deployer::default()`); `workflows::{INTERRUPTED, NO_APPROVAL, NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH}`; `NOT_BUILT` removed; the approval decision `{"decision": "approved", "note": …, "branches": [{"branch", "commit"}]}`; crate-private in `workflows/deploy_step.rs`: `worker(wr, step) -> Option<(&str, &str)>` (member and job), `tips(repo: &Path, shown: &Handoff) -> Vec<Value>`, `approved(wr, deploy_id) -> Result<(Vec<ApprovedBranch>, String), String>`, `base_branch(repo, &ProjectSpec) -> Option<String>`, `Workflows::{begin_deploy(&self, wr, step, attempt: u32) -> Result<()>, watch_deploy(&self, wr, step, step_run) -> Result<()>}`; `through_gate` becomes `pub(super)`.

```rust
/// A deploy step `ship` named "Ship".
fn ship(mode: DeployMode) -> Step {
    Step {
        id: "ship".into(),
        name: "Ship".into(),
        kind: StepKind::Deploy { mode },
    }
}

impl World {
    /// Gives the project a bare repository as its `origin` (with `main` pushed) and the
    /// workflows the fake `gh` answering as `gh_mode`, which logs its calls to `gh.log`. Its
    /// settings go to its own calls only, so tests running at once do not share them.
    fn shipping_to_origin(&self, gh_mode: &str) {
        git_out(self.dir.path(), &["init", "-q", "--bare", "remote.git"]);
        let remote = self.dir.path().join("remote.git");
        git_out(
            &self.repo,
            &["remote", "add", "origin", remote.to_str().unwrap()],
        );
        git_out(&self.repo, &["push", "-q", "origin", "main"]);
        let log = self.dir.path().join("gh.log");
        self.workflows.set_deployer(Deployer {
            gh: PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_fake-gh")),
            env: vec![
                ("FAKE_GH_LOG".into(), log.to_string_lossy().to_string()),
                ("FAKE_GH_MODE".into(), gh_mode.into()),
            ],
        });
    }

    /// The commit `branch` points at on `origin`, if it was pushed there.
    fn remote_tip(&self, branch: &str) -> Option<String> {
        let out = std::process::Command::new("git")
            .arg("-C")
            .arg(self.dir.path().join("remote.git"))
            .args(["rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet"])
            .arg(format!("refs/heads/{branch}"))
            .output()
            .unwrap();
        out.status
            .success()
            .then(|| String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).trim().to_string())
    }

    /// The fake `gh`'s calls, one argument list each.
    fn gh_calls(&self) -> Vec<Vec<String>> {
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(self.dir.path().join("gh.log")).unwrap_or_default();
        text.lines()
            .map(|l| serde_json::from_str(l).unwrap())
            .collect()
    }

    /// The flow "Ship": the dev fixes the bug (writing `fix.txt`), the person checks, then the
    /// step "Ship" deploys as `mode`.
    async fn ship_flow(&self, mode: DeployMode) -> String {
        self.engine.set(
            &self.dev.spec.name,
            vec![Act::Write("fix.txt", "Fixed the icon.")],
        );
        self.workflow(
            "Ship",
            vec![
                agent("dev", "Fix it", &self.dev, "Fix the bug."),
                check("check"),
                ship(mode),
            ],
        )
        .await
    }

    /// Starts a run of `flow` (a test run with `test`) and waits for its check. Gives the
    /// workflow run, the dev's branch and its tip.
    async fn at_the_check(&self, flow: &str, test: bool) -> (WorkflowRun, String, String) {
        let wr = self
            .workflows
            .start(StartRequest {
                workflow_id: flow.into(),
                project_id: self.project.clone(),
                task: "The tray icon is invisible in dark mode.".into(),
                trigger: WorkflowTrigger::Manual,
                origin_run_id: None,
                test,
            })
            .await
            .unwrap();
        let wr = self.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::Check)).await;
        let branch = newest(&wr, "dev").handoff.as_ref().unwrap().branches[0]
            .branch
            .clone();
        let tip = git_out(&self.repo, &["rev-parse", &format!("refs/heads/{branch}")]);
        (wr, branch, tip)
    }

    /// Holds every job at a gate, approves the check the workflow run `id` waits on, and
    /// waits until the deploy's job is held. Gives the deploy's step run id and the gate.
    async fn approved_and_held(&self, id: &str) -> (String, Arc<tokio::sync::Semaphore>) {
        let gate = Arc::new(tokio::sync::Semaphore::new(0));
        self.workflows.hold_jobs(Some(gate.clone()));
        let wr = self.workflows.approve_check(id, None).await.unwrap();
        let step_run = newest(&wr, "ship").clone();
        assert_eq!(step_run.status, StepRunStatus::Running);
        assert!(self.workflows.has_job(&step_run.id));
        (step_run.id, gate)
    }

    /// Moves `branch` on by one empty commit, made outside any worktree, as someone working
    /// on it after the approval would. Gives the new tip.
    fn commit_after(&self, branch: &str) -> String {
        let tip = git_out(&self.repo, &["rev-parse", &format!("refs/heads/{branch}")]);
        let tree = format!("{tip}^{{tree}}");
        let later = git_out(
            &self.repo,
            &["commit-tree", &tree, "-p", &tip, "-m", "Later."],
        );
        git_out(
            &self.repo,
            &["update-ref", &format!("refs/heads/{branch}"), &later],
        );
        later
    }
}

/// What a push deploy did to `branch` at `commit`.
fn pushed(branch: &str, commit: &str) -> DeployedBranch {
    DeployedBranch {
        branch: branch.into(),
        commit: commit.into(),
        pushed: true,
        pr_url: None,
        auto_merge: false,
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_push_deploy_lands_the_commit_the_check_approved_on_the_remote() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    let flow = w.ship_flow(DeployMode::Push).await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), None);
    w.workflows
        .approve_check(&wr.id, Some("Ship it."))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    // The approval recorded the tip it showed, and the deploy pushed exactly that.
    assert_eq!(
        newest(&wr, "check").decision,
        Some(json!({
            "decision": "approved",
            "note": "Ship it.",
            "branches": [{"branch": branch, "commit": tip}],
        }))
    );
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), Some(tip.clone()));
    let ship = newest(&wr, "ship");
    assert_eq!(ship.status, StepRunStatus::Succeeded);
    assert_eq!(
        ship.deploy_result,
        Some(DeployResult {
            branches: vec![pushed(&branch, &tip)],
            ready: false,
        })
    );
    assert!(w.gh_calls().is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_pr_merge_deploy_opens_a_pull_request_and_sets_it_to_merge() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    let flow = w
        .ship_flow(DeployMode::PrMerge {
            merge_method: MergeMethod::default(),
        })
        .await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), Some(tip.clone()));
    let url = "https://github.com/o/r/pull/1";
    // Against the default branch, titled with the task, with the approved summary as the body,
    // then auto-merge by squashing.
    let calls: Vec<Vec<&str>> = vec![
        vec!["--version"],
        vec![
            "pr",
            "view",
            &branch,
            "--json",
            "url,state",
            "--jq",
            "select(.state == \"OPEN\") | .url",
        ],
        vec![
            "pr",
            "create",
            "--head",
            &branch,
            "--base",
            "main",
            "--title",
            "The tray icon is invisible in dark mode.",
            "--body",
            "Fixed the icon.",
        ],
        vec!["pr", "merge", url, "--auto", "--squash"],
    ];
    assert_eq!(w.gh_calls(), calls);
    assert_eq!(
        newest(&wr, "ship").deploy_result,
        Some(DeployResult {
            branches: vec![DeployedBranch {
                pr_url: Some(url.into()),
                auto_merge: true,
                ..pushed(&branch, &tip)
            }],
            ready: false,
        })
    );
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_test_run_pushes_nothing_and_lists_the_branch_as_ready() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    let flow = w
        .ship_flow(DeployMode::PrMerge {
            merge_method: MergeMethod::Rebase,
        })
        .await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, true).await;
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(
        newest(&wr, "ship").deploy_result,
        Some(DeployResult {
            branches: vec![DeployedBranch {
                pushed: false,
                ..pushed(&branch, &tip)
            }],
            ready: true,
        })
    );
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), None);
    assert!(w.gh_calls().is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_branch_changed_after_the_approval_fails_the_deploy_until_it_is_put_back() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    let flow = w.ship_flow(DeployMode::Push).await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    let (_, gate) = w.approved_and_held(&wr.id).await;
    w.commit_after(&branch);
    w.workflows.hold_jobs(None);
    gate.add_permits(1);
    let failed = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::StepFailed)).await;
    assert_eq!(
        failed.error.as_deref(),
        Some("Ship: The branch changed after it was approved.")
    );
    assert_eq!(newest(&failed, "ship").deploy_result, None);
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), None);
    // Put back where it was approved, a retry deploys it.
    git_out(
        &w.repo,
        &["update-ref", &format!("refs/heads/{branch}"), &tip],
    );
    w.workflows.retry_step(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), Some(tip));
    assert_eq!(
        attempts(&wr),
        [
            ("dev", 1, StepRunStatus::Succeeded),
            ("check", 1, StepRunStatus::Succeeded),
            ("ship", 1, StepRunStatus::Failed),
            ("ship", 2, StepRunStatus::Succeeded),
        ]
    );
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_deploy_refuses_a_branch_that_holds_a_secret() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    w.engine
        .set(&w.dev.spec.name, vec![Act::Write(".env", "TOKEN=abc")]);
    let flow = w
        .workflow(
            "Ship",
            vec![
                agent("dev", "Fix it", &w.dev, "Fix the bug."),
                check("check"),
                ship(DeployMode::Pr),
            ],
        )
        .await;
    let (wr, branch, _) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::StepFailed)).await;
    assert_eq!(
        wr.error,
        Some(format!(
            "Ship: Not pushed: {branch} holds files that look like secrets or are over 10 MB: \
             .env. Remove them from the branch, then retry the step."
        ))
    );
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), None);
    assert!(w.gh_calls().is_empty());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_failed_gh_fails_the_deploy_with_its_message_and_keeps_the_push() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("fail-merge");
    let flow = w
        .ship_flow(DeployMode::PrMerge {
            merge_method: MergeMethod::Merge,
        })
        .await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::StepFailed)).await;
    assert_eq!(
        wr.error.as_deref(),
        Some(
            "Ship: gh pr merge failed: GraphQL: Pull request Auto merge is not allowed for this \
             repository (enablePullRequestAutoMerge)"
        )
    );
    assert_eq!(newest(&wr, "ship").status, StepRunStatus::Failed);
    // Nothing already done is undone.
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), Some(tip));
    assert_eq!(w.gh_calls().last().unwrap()[1], "merge");
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_deploy_cut_off_by_a_restart_fails_interrupted_and_is_not_pushed_until_retried() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    let flow = w.ship_flow(DeployMode::Push).await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    let (step_run, gate) = w.approved_and_held(&wr.id).await;
    // Dots stops while the deploy waits: its loop ends and its job is dropped unwritten.
    w.stop_loop.cancel();
    gate.close();
    w.job_ended(&step_run).await;
    let fresh = Workflows::new(
        w.store.clone(),
        new_bus(),
        w.runner.clone(),
        Arc::new(SystemClock),
    );
    fresh.pass().await.unwrap();
    let failed = w.store.get_workflow_run(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(failed.waiting_on, Some(WaitingOn::StepFailed));
    assert_eq!(failed.error.as_deref(), Some("Ship: interrupted"));
    // Further passes leave it waiting: nothing is pushed without the person.
    fresh.pass().await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), None);
    fresh.retry_step(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), Some(tip));
    assert_eq!(newest(&wr, "ship").attempt, 2);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_stop_during_a_held_deploy_wins_and_nothing_is_pushed() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    let flow = w.ship_flow(DeployMode::Push).await;
    let (wr, branch, _) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    let (step_run, gate) = w.approved_and_held(&wr.id).await;
    w.workflows.stop(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    gate.add_permits(1);
    w.job_ended(&step_run).await;
    w.workflows.pass().await.unwrap();
    let wr = w.store.get_workflow_run(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(wr.status, WorkflowRunStatus::Cancelled);
    let ship = newest(&wr, "ship");
    assert_eq!(ship.status, StepRunStatus::Cancelled);
    assert_eq!(ship.deploy_result, None);
    assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(&branch), None);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_member_deploy_runs_the_members_step_and_a_test_run_lists_the_branch_instead() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.engine.set(
        &w.helper.spec.name,
        vec![Act::Write("release.txt", "Tagged the release.")],
    );
    let flow = w
        .ship_flow(DeployMode::Member {
            member: w.helper.id.clone(),
            job: "Tag a release.".into(),
        })
        .await;
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, false).await;
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    // The member's run is the step's: its job, its place, and the approved work to start from.
    let prompts = w.engine.prompts_of("helper");
    assert_eq!(prompts.len(), 1);
    for part in [
        "- Step: Ship (3 of 3)",
        "### Your job in this step\n\nTag a release.",
        "#### Fix it\n\nFixed the icon.",
    ] {
        assert!(
            prompts[0].contains(part),
            "missing {part:?} in:\n{}",
            prompts[0]
        );
    }
    let ship = newest(&wr, "ship");
    let runs = w.runs_of(&ship.id).await;
    assert_eq!(runs.len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(runs[0].dot_id, w.helper.id);
    assert_eq!(ship.deploy_result, None);
    let handoff = ship.handoff.as_ref().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(handoff.summary, "Tagged the release.");
    let release = &handoff.branches[0].branch;
    assert_eq!(
        git_out(&w.repo, &["show", &format!("{release}:fix.txt")]),
        "Fixed the icon."
    );
    assert_eq!(
        git_out(&w.repo, &["merge-base", "--is-ancestor", &tip, release]),
        ""
    );
    assert_ne!(release, &branch);

    // A test run starts no one: the deploy lists the branch as ready.
    let (wr, branch, tip) = w.at_the_check(&flow, true).await;
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(w.engine.prompts_of("helper").len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(
        newest(&wr, "ship").deploy_result,
        Some(DeployResult {
            branches: vec![DeployedBranch {
                pushed: false,
                ..pushed(&branch, &tip)
            }],
            ready: true,
        })
    );
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_deploy_after_a_delegate_step_pushes_the_members_branches() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.shipping_to_origin("ok");
    w.engine.set(
        &w.helper.spec.name,
        vec![
            Act::Start(
                vec![("dev", "Part one."), ("dev", "Part two.")],
                Box::new(Act::Finish("Started two.")),
            ),
            Act::Finish("Both are in."),
        ],
    );
    w.engine.set(
        &w.dev.spec.name,
        vec![
            Act::Write("one.txt", "Part one done."),
            Act::Write("two.txt", "Part two done."),
        ],
    );
    let flow = w
        .workflow(
            "Split",
            vec![
                delegating("split", "Split it", &w.helper, &[&w.dev], 2, 2),
                check("check"),
                ship(DeployMode::Push),
            ],
        )
        .await;
    let wr = w.start(&flow).await;
    let wr = w.until(&wr.id, waits_on(WaitingOn::Check)).await;
    // The lead merged nothing, so the check shows each member's branch (section 6.2).
    let shown: Vec<String> = newest(&wr, "split")
        .handoff
        .as_ref()
        .unwrap()
        .branches
        .iter()
        .map(|b| b.branch.clone())
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(shown.len(), 2);
    w.workflows.approve_check(&wr.id, None).await.unwrap();
    let wr = w
        .until(&wr.id, |wr| wr.status == WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    let result = newest(&wr, "ship").deploy_result.clone().unwrap();
    let tips: Vec<String> = shown
        .iter()
        .map(|b| git_out(&w.repo, &["rev-parse", &format!("refs/heads/{b}")]))
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(
        result.branches,
        [pushed(&shown[0], &tips[0]), pushed(&shown[1], &tips[1])]
    );
    for (b, tip) in shown.iter().zip(&tips) {
        assert_eq!(w.remote_tip(b).as_ref(), Some(tip));
    }
}
```
