**Create `crates/dots-core/src/workflows/deploy_step.rs`:**

```rust
//! Deploy steps (teams spec sections 5.5 and 6.2): Dots ships exactly the branches the check
//! before the step approved, at the commits the person saw. A check's approval records each
//! branch it showed with its tip (`tips`); the deploy step reads them back (`approved`) and
//! deploys them as a job outside the loop's lock (`crate::deploy::Deployer`), since a push or
//! gh may take minutes. A test run, and mode `branch`, push nothing and list the branches as
//! ready. Mode `member` (in a real run) checks the tips the same way and then runs as an agent
//! step of that member with the step's job: its hand-off is the step's. A deploy is never run
//! twice without the person: one Dots stopped during fails `interrupted`, and Retry runs it
//! again.

use std::path::Path;

use serde_json::{json, Value};

use super::{ran_before, succeeded, Workflows};
use crate::deploy::{ApprovedBranch, DeployResult, Deployer, BRANCH_CHANGED};
use crate::model::{
    Handoff, ProjectSpec, StepRun, StepRunChange, StepRunStatus, WorkflowRun, WorkflowRunChange,
};
use crate::workflow::{DeployMode, Step, StepKind};
use crate::workspace::git;
use crate::{Error, Result};

/// Why a deploy step fails when the check before it recorded no branches it approved: a check
/// approved before Dots recorded them, or one that sent the work back.
pub const NO_APPROVAL: &str = "the check before it recorded no approved branches";
/// Why a `push`, `pr` or `pr_merge` deploy fails when the project's default branch cannot be
/// found: the project names none, the remote's `HEAD` names none, and the repo is on no branch.
pub const NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH: &str =
    "the project's default branch could not be found; set it on the project";
/// Why a deploy step Dots stopped during fails, as plan A words an interrupted run.
pub const INTERRUPTED: &str = "interrupted";

/// The member and job of a step a member works on: an agent step, or in a real run a deploy of
/// mode `member` (a test run's deploys push nothing and start no one: they act as `branch`).
pub(super) fn worker<'a>(wr: &WorkflowRun, step: &'a Step) -> Option<(&'a str, &'a str)> {
    match &step.kind {
        StepKind::Agent { member, job, .. } => Some((member, job)),
        StepKind::Deploy {
            mode: DeployMode::Member { member, job },
        } if !wr.test => Some((member, job)),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// The branches a check's approval records (section 6.2): each branch of the hand-off it
/// showed, with the commit at its tip as the person approved, `null` for one already gone.
pub(super) async fn tips(repo: &Path, shown: &Handoff) -> Vec<Value> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for b in &shown.branches {
        let commit = format!("refs/heads/{}^{{commit}}", b.branch);
        let args = [
            "rev-parse",
            "--verify",
            "--quiet",
            "--end-of-options",
            &commit,
        ];
        let tip = git(repo, &args).await.ok();
        out.push(json!({"branch": b.branch, "commit": tip}));
    }
    out
}

/// What the deploy step `id` acts on: the branches the newest check that ran before it on its
/// path approved, at the commits it recorded, each with the commit its work started from (the
/// hand-off's `base_commit`, for the secret scan); and the summary that check showed, for a
/// pull request's body.
fn approved(
    wr: &WorkflowRun,
    id: &str,
) -> std::result::Result<(Vec<ApprovedBranch>, String), String> {
    let check = ran_before(wr, id)
        .into_iter()
        .rev()
        .filter(|s| matches!(s.kind, StepKind::Check { .. }))
        .find_map(|s| succeeded(wr, &s.id))
        .ok_or_else(|| NO_APPROVAL.to_string())?;
    let shown = check.handoff.clone().unwrap_or_default();
    let recorded = check
        .decision
        .as_ref()
        .filter(|d| d.get("decision").and_then(Value::as_str) == Some("approved"))
        .and_then(|d| d.get("branches"))
        .and_then(Value::as_array)
        .ok_or_else(|| NO_APPROVAL.to_string())?;
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    for entry in recorded {
        let branch = entry
            .get("branch")
            .and_then(Value::as_str)
            .ok_or_else(|| NO_APPROVAL.to_string())?;
        // A branch that was gone when the person approved has no commit they saw.
        let commit = entry
            .get("commit")
            .and_then(Value::as_str)
            .ok_or_else(|| BRANCH_CHANGED.to_string())?;
        let base = shown
            .branches
            .iter()
            .find(|b| b.branch == branch)
            .and_then(|b| b.base_commit.clone());
        out.push(ApprovedBranch {
            branch: branch.to_string(),
            commit: commit.to_string(),
            base,
        });
    }
    Ok((out, shown.summary))
}

/// The project's default branch by name, for a pull request's base and the scan of a branch
/// with no base commit: its `default_branch`, else as worktrees find it: the branch the remote's
/// `HEAD` names, else the branch the repo is on. `None` when none of them names one.
async fn base_branch(repo: &Path, project: &ProjectSpec) -> Option<String> {
    let set = project.default_branch.as_deref().map(str::trim);
    if let Some(branch) = set.filter(|b| !b.is_empty()) {
        return Some(branch.to_string());
    }
    let remote = project.remote.trim();
    let head = format!("refs/remotes/{remote}/HEAD");
    let named = git(repo, &["symbolic-ref", "--quiet", "--short", &head]).await;
    if let Some(branch) = named
        .ok()
        .and_then(|r| r.strip_prefix(&format!("{remote}/")).map(str::to_string))
        .filter(|b| !b.is_empty())
    {
        return Some(branch);
    }
    git(repo, &["symbolic-ref", "--quiet", "--short", "HEAD"])
        .await
        .ok()
        .filter(|b| !b.is_empty())
}

impl Workflows {
    /// Sets the deployer deploy steps use, once (by default `gh` from the PATH); a test gives a
    /// fake `gh` this way.
    pub fn set_deployer(&self, deployer: Deployer) {
        let _ = self.deployer.set(deployer);
    }

    fn deployer(&self) -> Deployer {
        self.deployer.get().cloned().unwrap_or_default()
    }

    /// Begins the deploy step `step` as its attempt `attempt`: a step run that stays `Running`
    /// while a job deploys. In a real run, a deploy of mode `member` checks that the approved
    /// branches are unchanged and then runs as an agent step of that member (`start_agent`).
    pub(super) async fn begin_deploy(
        &self,
        wr: &WorkflowRun,
        step: &Step,
        attempt: u32,
    ) -> Result<()> {
        let step_run = self
            .store
            .create_step_run(&wr.id, &step.id, attempt, StepRunStatus::Running)
            .await?;
        let wr = self
            .store
            .set_workflow_run(
                &wr.id,
                &WorkflowRunChange {
                    current_step: Some(Some(step.id.clone())),
                    ..Default::default()
                },
            )
            .await?;
        if worker(&wr, step).is_none() {
            // In the locked section that made the step run: no pass finds it without its job.
            self.start_deploy(&wr, &step_run);
            self.updated(&wr);
            return Ok(());
        }
        // Only `git rev-parse` per branch for this mode, so under the lock as an approval is.
        let checked = match approved(&wr, &step.id) {
            Ok((branches, _)) => {
                let repo = Path::new(&wr.project.repo_path);
                let StepKind::Deploy { mode } = &step.kind else {
                    return Err(Error::Other(format!("step {} is not a deploy", step.id)));
                };
                self.deployer()
                    .deploy(repo, &wr.project.remote, "", mode, &branches, "", "")
                    .await
                    .map(drop)
            }
            Err(e) => Err(e),
        };
        if let Err(error) = checked {
            return self.fail_step(&wr, step, &step_run, &error).await;
        }
        self.start_agent(&wr, step, &step_run).await?;
        self.updated(&self.store.get_workflow_run(&wr.id).await?);
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Watches a deploy step run that runs with no member: its job is in flight, or Dots
    /// stopped during it, which fails it `interrupted`. A push may have gone through, so it
    /// is not run again without the person: Retry runs it again.
    pub(super) async fn watch_deploy(
        &self,
        wr: &WorkflowRun,
        step: &Step,
        step_run: &StepRun,
    ) -> Result<()> {
        if self.has_job(&step_run.id) {
            return Ok(());
        }
        self.fail_step(wr, step, step_run, INTERRUPTED).await
    }

    /// Starts the job that deploys the step run `step_run` (`deploy`).
    fn start_deploy(&self, wr: &WorkflowRun, step_run: &StepRun) {
        let id = wr.id.clone();
        let step_run_id = step_run.id.clone();
        self.start_job(&step_run.id, move |this| async move {
            this.deploy(id, step_run_id).await
        });
    }

    /// The deploy job (`start_deploy`): reads what to deploy under the lock (a Stop while the
    /// job waited wins, and nothing is pushed), deploys unlocked, then, if the step run is still
    /// open, succeeds it with what was done and moves on, or fails it with the deploy's error:
    /// the run waits for the person to retry it or stop.
    async fn deploy(&self, id: String, step_run_id: String) -> Result<()> {
        if !self.through_gate().await {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let wr = {
            let _one = self.lock.lock().await;
            match self.still_open(&id, &step_run_id).await? {
                Some((wr, _)) => wr,
                None => return Ok(()),
            }
        };
        let step_id = wr
            .steps
            .iter()
            .find(|s| s.id == step_run_id)
            .map(|s| s.step_id.clone())
            .unwrap_or_default();
        let done = self.deploy_now(&wr, &step_id).await;
        let _one = self.lock.lock().await;
        let Some((wr, step_run)) = self.still_open(&id, &step_run_id).await? else {
            return Ok(());
        };
        let Some(step) = wr.definition.step(&step_run.step_id) else {
            return Err(Error::Other(format!(
                "step {} is not in the workflow",
                step_run.step_id
            )));
        };
        match done {
            Ok(result) => {
                self.store
                    .set_step_run(
                        &step_run.id,
                        &StepRunChange {
                            status: Some(StepRunStatus::Succeeded),
                            deploy_result: Some(Some(result)),
                            ..Default::default()
                        },
                    )
                    .await?;
                self.step_ended(&wr, &step.id);
                let wr = self.store.get_workflow_run(&wr.id).await?;
                self.move_on(&wr, step).await
            }
            Err(error) => self.fail_step(&wr, step, &step_run, &error).await,
        }
    }

    /// Deploys the step `step_id` of `wr` as its mode says (`branch` in a test run), on the
    /// project's repo and remote, titled with the task and with the approved summary as the
    /// body. Slow: run without the lock.
    async fn deploy_now(
        &self,
        wr: &WorkflowRun,
        step_id: &str,
    ) -> std::result::Result<DeployResult, String> {
        let mode = match wr.definition.step(step_id).map(|s| &s.kind) {
            Some(StepKind::Deploy { .. }) if wr.test => DeployMode::Branch,
            Some(StepKind::Deploy { mode }) => mode.clone(),
            _ => return Err(format!("step {step_id} is not a deploy")),
        };
        let (branches, summary) = approved(wr, step_id)?;
        let repo = Path::new(&wr.project.repo_path);
        let base = match mode {
            DeployMode::Branch | DeployMode::Member { .. } => String::new(),
            _ => base_branch(repo, &wr.project)
                .await
                .ok_or_else(|| NO_DEFAULT_BRANCH.to_string())?,
        };
        self.deployer()
            .deploy(
                repo,
                &wr.project.remote,
                &base,
                &mode,
                &branches,
                &wr.task,
                &summary,
            )
            .await
    }
}
```

### Task 9: Workflows start on a schedule

The `Scheduler` pass also fires the scheduled workflows that are turned on and have no problem, with their own rows in `workflow_schedule_state`. A due slot is taken once, then skipped when it is 60 s or more late or when a run of the workflow is in flight, else starts the workflow on the trigger's task and project. Nothing is caught up or retried. Saving a workflow prepares its schedule as a dot's is. The runtime hands the scheduler its `Workflows`.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/dots-core/src/scheduler/workflow_slots.rs`, `crates/dots-core/src/store/workflow_schedules.rs`
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/migrations/0027_teams_b.sql`, `crates/dots-core/src/{model.rs,runtime.rs,scheduler.rs,store/mod.rs,store/teams.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/workflow_schedule_test.rs` (new)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `Trigger::Schedule`; `schedule::{due_slot, next_slot, Schedule::prepare_save}`; plan A's `Workflows::start(StartRequest { workflow_id, project_id, task, trigger, origin_run_id, test })`.
- Produces: table `workflow_schedule_state (workflow_id, rule_key, status, last_slot_at, fired_count, updated_at)`; `model::WorkflowScheduleState { workflow_id, rule_key, status: ScheduleStatus, last_slot_at, fired_count: u32, updated_at }`; `Store::{get_workflow_schedule_state(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<Option<WorkflowScheduleState>>, list_workflow_schedule_states(&self) -> Result<Vec<WorkflowScheduleState>>, sync_workflow_schedule_state(&self, workflow_id: &str, schedule: &Schedule, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<WorkflowScheduleState>, delete_workflow_schedule_state(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<bool>, record_workflow_slot(&self, workflow_id: &str, at: DateTime<Utc>, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<bool>, count_workflow_slot(&self, workflow_id: &str, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<()>, set_workflow_schedule_status(&self, workflow_id: &str, status: ScheduleStatus, last_slot_at: &str, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<bool>, resume_workflow_schedules(&self, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<Vec<String>>, workflow_has_runs_in_flight(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<bool>}`; `Scheduler::set_workflows(&self, workflows: Arc<Workflows>)`; crate-private `Scheduler::workflow_pass(&self, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<Option<DateTime<Utc>>>` and the module's `start(workflows, &Scheduled)`.

```rust
//! Scheduled workflows (teams spec section 5.1): the scheduler's pass starts a workflow at the
//! slots of its schedule trigger, never catching up and never retrying, on a fake clock.

mod common;

use std::collections::HashMap;
use std::sync::Arc;
use std::time::Duration;

use chrono::{DateTime, Local, TimeDelta, Utc};
use common::clock::FakeClock;
use common::{init_repo, spec, temp_store};
use dots_core::engine::Engine;
use dots_core::events::{new_bus, Bus};
use dots_core::model::{
    Dot, EngineKind, MemberSpec, ProjectSpec, ScheduleStatus, TeamSpec, WaitingOn, WorkflowRun,
    WorkflowRunChange, WorkflowRunStatus, WorkflowScheduleState, WorkflowSpec, WorkflowTrigger,
};
use dots_core::runner::{Runner, RunnerConfig};
use dots_core::schedule::{Rule, Schedule};
use dots_core::scheduler::Scheduler;
use dots_core::store::Store;
use dots_core::teams::Teams;
use dots_core::util::timestamp;
use dots_core::workflow::{Budget, Definition, Step, StepKind, Trigger};
use dots_core::workflows::Workflows;
use dots_core::Error;
use serde_json::{json, Value};
use tempfile::TempDir;
use tokio_util::sync::CancellationToken;

/// The fake clock starts here, years ahead, as in `scheduler_test`.
const T0: &str = "2030-01-07T09:00:00.000Z";

/// The task every scheduled workflow here is started with.
const TASK: &str = "Look for new crash reports and fix the worst one.";

fn t0() -> DateTime<Utc> {
    DateTime::parse_from_rfc3339(T0)
        .unwrap()
        .with_timezone(&Utc)
}

/// `secs` seconds after `T0`.
fn after(secs: i64) -> DateTime<Utc> {
    t0() + TimeDelta::seconds(secs)
}

/// `after(secs)` as the store writes it.
fn ts(secs: i64) -> String {
    timestamp(after(secs))
}

fn schedule(v: Value) -> Schedule {
    serde_json::from_value(v).unwrap()
}

/// Every `minutes`, with slots at `after(offset)` and every `minutes` from there, at most
/// `count` of them.
fn every(minutes: u32, offset: i64, count: Option<u32>) -> Schedule {
    schedule(json!({
        "rule": {"kind": "every", "minutes": minutes, "anchor": ts(offset)},
        "until": count.map(|n| json!({"count": n})),
    }))
}

/// Every day at the local time of `after(secs)`.
fn daily(secs: i64) -> Schedule {
    let time = after(secs)
        .with_timezone(&Local)
        .format("%H:%M")
        .to_string();
    schedule(json!({"rule": {"kind": "daily", "time": time}}))
}

/// Runner without a dispatcher: the scheduler only starts workflow runs, and no workflow loop
/// moves them, so a run stays in flight until the test ends it.
fn idle_runner(store: &Store, dir: &TempDir, bus: Bus) -> Arc<Runner> {
    Runner::new(
        store.clone(),
        bus,
        HashMap::<EngineKind, Arc<dyn Engine>>::new(),
        RunnerConfig {
            max_concurrent: 1,
            mcp_url: String::new(),
            worktrees_dir: dir.path().join("wt"),
            cancel_grace: Duration::from_millis(100),
            plugins_dir: dir.path().join("plugins"),
            quiet_unit: Duration::from_secs(1),
        },
    )
}

/// A scheduler with workflows, on a fake clock at `T0`, not started yet; a team with the
/// member `dev`, the member `other` and the project "Dots".
struct Harness {
    _dir: TempDir,
    store: Store,
    bus: Bus,
    clock: Arc<FakeClock>,
    runner: Arc<Runner>,
    workflows: Arc<Workflows>,
    sched: Arc<Scheduler>,
    shutdown: CancellationToken,
    sleeps: u64,
    team: String,
    project: String,
    dev: Dot,
    other: Dot,
}

impl Harness {
    async fn new() -> Harness {
        let (dir, store) = temp_store().await;
        let bus = new_bus();
        let clock = FakeClock::new(t0());
        let runner = idle_runner(&store, &dir, bus.clone());
        let workflows = Workflows::new(store.clone(), bus.clone(), runner.clone(), clock.clone());
        let sched = Scheduler::new(store.clone(), runner.clone(), bus.clone(), clock.clone());
        sched.set_workflows(workflows.clone());
        let dev = store
            .create_dot(&spec("dev"), None, "default")
            .await
            .unwrap();
        let other = store
            .create_dot(&spec("other"), None, "default")
            .await
            .unwrap();
        let team = store
            .create_team(&TeamSpec {
                org_id: None,
                workspace_id: "default".into(),
                name: "Dots management".into(),
                description: String::new(),
                members: vec![member(&dev, "dev agent"), member(&other, "reviewer")],
            })
            .await
            .unwrap()
            .id;
        let repo = dir.path().join("project");
        init_repo(&repo);
        let project = store
            .add_project(
                &team,
                &ProjectSpec {
                    name: "Dots".into(),
                    repo_path: repo.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
                    default_branch: None,
                    remote: "origin".into(),
                },
            )
            .await
            .unwrap()
            .id;
        Harness {
            _dir: dir,
            store,
            bus,
            clock,
            runner,
            workflows,
            sched,
            shutdown: CancellationToken::new(),
            sleeps: 0,
            team,
            project,
            dev,
            other,
        }
    }

    /// A workflow started by `schedule` on the project named `project`, whose one step is
    /// `member`'s.
    fn spec(&self, name: &str, schedule: Schedule, project: &str, member: &Dot) -> WorkflowSpec {
        WorkflowSpec {
            name: name.into(),
            definition: Definition {
                trigger: Trigger::Schedule {
                    schedule,
                    task: TASK.into(),
                    project: project.into(),
                },
                steps: vec![Step {
                    id: "fix".into(),
                    name: "Fix it".into(),
                    kind: StepKind::Agent {
                        member: member.id.clone(),
                        job: "Fix the bug.".into(),
                        can_send_back_to: vec![],
                        outcomes: vec![],
                        delegate: None,
                    },
                }],
                budget: Budget::default(),
            },
            enabled: true,
        }
    }

    /// Saves a scheduled workflow of the dev on "Dots" and returns its id.
    async fn workflow(&self, name: &str, schedule: Schedule) -> String {
        let spec = self.spec(name, schedule, "Dots", &self.dev);
        self.store
            .create_workflow(&self.team, &spec)
            .await
            .unwrap()
            .id
    }

    fn start(&self) {
        self.sched.spawn(self.shutdown.clone());
    }

    /// Stops the scheduler (Dots is closed).
    fn stop(&mut self) {
        self.shutdown.cancel();
        self.shutdown = CancellationToken::new();
    }

    /// Starts a new scheduler on the same database and clock, as Dots starting again does.
    fn restart(&mut self) {
        self.stop();
        self.sched = Scheduler::new(
            self.store.clone(),
            self.runner.clone(),
            self.bus.clone(),
            self.clock.clone(),
        );
        self.sched.set_workflows(self.workflows.clone());
        self.start();
    }

    /// Waits until the loop sleeps again after its pass, and returns when it means to wake, in
    /// seconds after `T0`.
    async fn settle(&mut self) -> i64 {
        self.sleeps += 1;
        (self.clock.nth_sleep(self.sleeps).await - t0()).num_seconds()
    }

    /// Moves the clock to `after(secs)`, which must reach the loop's wake time, and waits for
    /// the pass that follows.
    async fn at(&mut self, secs: i64) -> i64 {
        self.clock.set(after(secs));
        self.settle().await
    }

    /// Wakes the loop, as a save does, and waits for the pass that follows.
    async fn reload(&mut self) -> i64 {
        self.sched.reload();
        self.settle().await
    }

    async fn row(&self, workflow: &str) -> Option<WorkflowScheduleState> {
        self.store
            .get_workflow_schedule_state(workflow)
            .await
            .unwrap()
    }

    /// The workflow's runs, newest first.
    async fn runs(&self, workflow: &str) -> Vec<WorkflowRun> {
        self.store
            .list_workflow_runs(Some(&self.team), 50)
            .await
            .unwrap()
            .into_iter()
            .filter(|wr| wr.workflow_id.as_deref() == Some(workflow))
            .collect()
    }

    /// Moves the workflow run to `status`, as its loop would.
    async fn set_status(&self, run: &str, status: WorkflowRunStatus) {
        let waiting_on = (status == WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting).then_some(WaitingOn::Check);
        self.store
            .set_workflow_run(
                run,
                &WorkflowRunChange {
                    status: Some(status),
                    waiting_on: Some(waiting_on),
                    ..Default::default()
                },
            )
            .await
            .unwrap();
    }
}

impl Drop for Harness {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.shutdown.cancel();
    }
}

fn member(dot: &Dot, role: &str) -> MemberSpec {
    MemberSpec {
        dot_id: dot.id.clone(),
        role: role.into(),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_daily_workflow_starts_at_its_slot_with_the_trigger_s_task_and_project() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    // The trigger names the project in other case: projects are found without case.
    let spec = h.spec("Crash reports", daily(600), "dots", &h.dev);
    let id = h.store.create_workflow(&h.team, &spec).await.unwrap().id;
    h.start();
    assert_eq!(h.settle().await, 60, "the slot is 10 minutes away");
    let row = h.row(&id).await.expect("the pass made the row");
    assert_eq!(row.status, ScheduleStatus::Active);
    assert_eq!(row.last_slot_at, T0, "made at the pass's time");
    assert_eq!(row.fired_count, 0);
    assert!(h.runs(&id).await.is_empty());

    h.at(300).await;
    assert!(h.runs(&id).await.is_empty(), "not before its slot");
    h.at(600).await;
    let runs = h.runs(&id).await;
    assert_eq!(runs.len(), 1);
    let wr = &runs[0];
    assert_eq!(wr.trigger, WorkflowTrigger::Schedule);
    assert_eq!(wr.task, TASK);
    assert_eq!(wr.project_id.as_deref(), Some(h.project.as_str()));
    assert_eq!(wr.project.name, "Dots");
    assert_eq!(wr.origin_run_id, None);
    assert!(!wr.test);
    assert_eq!(wr.status, WorkflowRunStatus::Running);
    let row = h.row(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(row.last_slot_at, ts(600));
    assert_eq!(row.fired_count, 1);

    // Its run ends; nothing more starts until the next day's slot.
    h.set_status(&wr.id, WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded).await;
    h.at(660).await;
    h.at(86_400).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 1);
    h.at(86_400 + 600).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 2);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_slot_while_a_run_is_in_flight_is_skipped_and_the_next_one_starts_after_it_ended() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let id = h.workflow("Every 10", every(10, 0, None)).await;
    h.start();
    h.settle().await;
    h.at(600).await;
    let first = h.runs(&id).await;
    assert_eq!(first.len(), 1);

    // Running at the next slot: skipped, not counted, and not started later.
    h.at(1200).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 1, "no second run while one runs");
    let row = h.row(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(row.last_slot_at, ts(1200), "the slot is used up");
    assert_eq!(row.fired_count, 1);
    h.at(1260).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 1);

    // Waiting on the person's check is in flight too.
    h.set_status(&first[0].id, WorkflowRunStatus::Waiting).await;
    h.at(1800).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 1, "no second run while one waits");

    // Once it ended, the next slot starts a run.
    h.set_status(&first[0].id, WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    h.at(2400).await;
    let runs = h.runs(&id).await;
    assert_eq!(runs.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(runs[0].status, WorkflowRunStatus::Running);
    assert_eq!(h.row(&id).await.unwrap().fired_count, 2);
}

/// "Never catch up": after a gap, the first pass marks the newest missed slot handled and
/// starts nothing. A slot less than `ON_TIME` (60 s) old at that pass is on time, not missed.
#[tokio::test]
async fn slots_missed_while_dots_was_closed_never_start_late() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let id = h.workflow("Every 10", every(10, 0, None)).await;
    h.start();
    h.settle().await;

    // Closed over the slots at 600, 1200 and 1800; back at 2000.
    h.stop();
    h.clock.set(after(2000));
    h.restart();
    assert_eq!(h.settle().await, 2060);
    assert!(h.runs(&id).await.is_empty(), "no missed slot runs");
    let row = h.row(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        row.last_slot_at,
        ts(1800),
        "the newest missed slot is handled"
    );
    assert_eq!(row.fired_count, 0);
    h.at(2060).await;
    assert!(h.runs(&id).await.is_empty(), "nor later");

    // The next slot starts on time.
    h.at(2400).await;
    let runs = h.runs(&id).await;
    assert_eq!(runs.len(), 1);
    h.set_status(&runs[0].id, WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;

    // Back 30 s after a slot: that slot is on time, and starts.
    h.stop();
    h.clock.set(after(3030));
    h.restart();
    h.settle().await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(h.row(&id).await.unwrap().last_slot_at, ts(3000));
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_turned_off_workflow_and_one_with_a_problem_do_not_start() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let on = h.workflow("On", every(10, 0, None)).await;
    let mut off = h.spec("Off", every(10, 0, None), "Dots", &h.dev);
    off.enabled = false;
    let off = h.store.create_workflow(&h.team, &off).await.unwrap();
    let broken = h.spec("Broken", every(10, 0, None), "Dots", &h.other);
    let broken = h.store.create_workflow(&h.team, &broken).await.unwrap().id;
    // `other` leaves the team: the workflow naming it has a problem.
    h.store
        .update_team(
            &h.team,
            &TeamSpec {
                org_id: None,
                workspace_id: "default".into(),
                name: "Dots management".into(),
                description: String::new(),
                members: vec![member(&h.dev, "dev agent")],
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert!(h
        .store
        .get_workflow(&broken)
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .problem
        .is_some());

    h.start();
    h.settle().await;
    h.at(600).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&on).await.len(), 1, "the pass ran");
    assert!(h.runs(&off.id).await.is_empty());
    assert!(h.runs(&broken).await.is_empty());
    assert!(h.row(&off.id).await.is_none());
    assert!(h.row(&broken).await.is_none());

    // Turned on through `Teams`: its change wakes the loop (before its wake time, 660), and
    // the pass makes the row at that time.
    h.clock.set(after(630));
    let teams = Teams::new(h.store.clone(), h.bus.clone());
    let mut spec = off.spec.clone();
    spec.enabled = true;
    teams.update_workflow(&off.id, &spec).await.unwrap();
    h.settle().await;
    assert_eq!(h.row(&off.id).await.unwrap().last_slot_at, ts(630));
    h.at(1200).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&off.id).await.len(), 1);

    // Turned off again: its row goes, and a later turn on starts afresh.
    spec.enabled = false;
    h.store.update_workflow(&off.id, &spec).await.unwrap();
    h.reload().await;
    assert!(h.row(&off.id).await.is_none());
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn pausing_schedules_pauses_scheduled_workflows() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let id = h.workflow("Every 10", every(10, 0, None)).await;
    h.start();
    h.settle().await;

    h.sched.set_paused(true);
    // The slot at 600 comes and is 30 s old at the resume.
    h.clock.set(after(630));
    tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(200)).await;
    assert_eq!(h.clock.sleeps(), h.sleeps, "no pass while paused");
    h.sched.set_paused(false);
    h.settle().await;
    assert!(
        h.runs(&id).await.is_empty(),
        "the pause's slots do not start"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        h.row(&id).await.unwrap().last_slot_at,
        ts(630),
        "the resume time"
    );

    h.at(1200).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 1);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_changed_rule_resets_the_row() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let id = h.workflow("Twice", every(10, 0, Some(2))).await;
    h.start();
    h.settle().await;
    h.at(600).await;
    let runs = h.runs(&id).await;
    assert_eq!(runs.len(), 1);
    h.set_status(&runs[0].id, WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
        .await;
    let before = h.row(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(before.fired_count, 1);

    // Every 15 minutes from 300 s: slots at 1200, 2100 and on.
    let spec = h.spec("Twice", every(15, 300, Some(2)), "Dots", &h.dev);
    h.store.update_workflow(&id, &spec).await.unwrap();
    // Before the loop's wake time (660), so only the reload brings a pass.
    h.clock.set(after(650));
    h.reload().await;
    let row = h.row(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert_ne!(row.rule_key, before.rule_key);
    assert_eq!(row.last_slot_at, ts(650), "reset at the pass's time");
    assert_eq!(row.fired_count, 0, "the count starts again");

    h.at(1200).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 2);

    // Saved again unchanged: the row stays.
    h.store.update_workflow(&id, &spec).await.unwrap();
    h.reload().await;
    assert_eq!(h.row(&id).await.unwrap().fired_count, 1);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn until_count_2_stops_after_two_runs() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let id = h.workflow("Twice", every(10, 0, Some(2))).await;
    h.start();
    h.settle().await;
    for slot in [600, 1200] {
        h.at(slot).await;
        let runs = h.runs(&id).await;
        h.set_status(&runs[0].id, WorkflowRunStatus::Succeeded)
            .await;
    }
    let row = h.row(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(row.fired_count, 2);
    assert_eq!(row.status, ScheduleStatus::Completed);

    h.at(1800).await;
    h.at(2400).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 2);
    assert_eq!(h.row(&id).await.unwrap().last_slot_at, ts(1200));
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_start_that_is_refused_uses_up_its_slot() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let id = h.workflow("Every 10", every(10, 0, None)).await;
    h.store.delete_project(&h.project).await.unwrap();
    h.start();
    h.settle().await;
    h.at(600).await;
    assert!(h.runs(&id).await.is_empty());
    let row = h.row(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(row.last_slot_at, ts(600), "the slot is used up");
    assert_eq!(row.fired_count, 0);

    // Not retried when the project is back; the next slot starts.
    assert!(h.store.get_team(&h.team).await.unwrap().projects.is_empty());
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    init_repo(dir.path());
    h.store
        .add_project(
            &h.team,
            &ProjectSpec {
                name: "Dots".into(),
                repo_path: dir.path().to_string_lossy().to_string(),
                default_branch: None,
                remote: "origin".into(),
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    h.at(660).await;
    assert!(h.runs(&id).await.is_empty());
    h.at(1200).await;
    assert_eq!(h.runs(&id).await.len(), 1);
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_every_schedule_saved_gets_its_anchor_and_keeps_it() {
    let h = Harness::new().await;
    let teams = Teams::new(h.store.clone(), h.bus.clone());
    let anchor = |w: &dots_core::model::Workflow| match &w.spec.definition.trigger {
        Trigger::Schedule {
            schedule:
                Schedule {
                    rule: Rule::Every { anchor, .. },
                    ..
                },
            ..
        } => *anchor,
        other => panic!("not an every schedule: {other:?}"),
    };
    let unanchored =
        |minutes: u32| schedule(json!({"rule": {"kind": "every", "minutes": minutes}}));

    let before = Utc::now() - TimeDelta::seconds(1);
    let spec = h.spec("Every 30", unanchored(30), "Dots", &h.dev);
    let made = teams.create_workflow(&h.team, &spec).await.unwrap();
    let first = anchor(&made).expect("saving set the anchor");
    assert!(first >= before && first <= Utc::now());
    assert_eq!(
        anchor(&h.store.get_workflow(&made.id).await.unwrap()),
        Some(first)
    );

    // Saved again without one (the UI never sends it), with the same minutes: kept.
    let kept = teams.update_workflow(&made.id, &spec).await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(anchor(&kept), Some(first));

    // New minutes: it starts again.
    tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(5)).await;
    let spec = h.spec("Every 30", unanchored(45), "Dots", &h.dev);
    let moved = teams.update_workflow(&made.id, &spec).await.unwrap();
    assert!(anchor(&moved).unwrap() > first);

    // A schedule that would never run is refused, as a dot's is.
    let once = schedule(json!({"rule": {"kind": "once", "at": "2020-01-01T09:00"}}));
    let spec = h.spec("Long ago", once, "Dots", &h.dev);
    match teams.create_workflow(&h.team, &spec).await {
        Err(Error::Invalid(m)) => {
            assert_eq!(m, "the trigger's schedule: this schedule would never run")
        }
        other => panic!("expected a refusal, got {other:?}"),
    }
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn deleting_a_workflow_deletes_its_schedule_row() {
    let mut h = Harness::new().await;
    let id = h.workflow("Every 10", every(10, 0, None)).await;
    h.start();
    h.settle().await;
    assert!(h.row(&id).await.is_some());
    h.stop();
    h.store.delete_workflow(&id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(h.row(&id).await.is_none());
    assert!(h
        .store
        .list_workflow_schedule_states()
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .is_empty());
}
```

```rust
}
```

**Create `crates/dots-core/src/scheduler/workflow_slots.rs`:**

```rust
//! The scheduled workflows (teams spec section 5.1). Each pass of the scheduler also fires
//! every workflow that is turned on, has no `problem` and whose trigger is a schedule. Its slots
//! are a dot's (`schedule::due_slot`, `Until`), kept in its own row (`workflow_schedule_state`),
//! but a workflow schedule never catches up and never retries:
//!
//! - A due slot handled `ON_TIME` or later after its time is skipped. So after a gap (Dots was
//!   closed, or the PC asleep), the first pass marks the newest missed slot handled and starts
//!   nothing; none of the missed slots runs, and the next slot starts on time.
//! - A slot that comes due while a run of the workflow is in flight (running or waiting, a check
//!   included) is skipped, with a log line.
//! - A start that is refused (the project or its repo is gone, the workflow was turned off, the
//!   role may not run dots) is logged, and the slot is used up.
//!
//! The slot is taken before its run is started, so a slot starts at most one run. Only a
//! started run counts towards `Until::Count`. A row is made at the pass's time, and reset when
//! its rule key is not the schedule's, so a new or changed rule starts no slot from before. A
//! workflow that no longer fires on a schedule (turned off, a problem, another trigger) loses
//! its row, so turning it back on starts afresh, as enabling a dot does.

use std::collections::HashMap;

use chrono::{DateTime, Local, Utc};

use super::{parse_time, Scheduler, ON_TIME};
use crate::model::{
    ScheduleStatus, Team, Workflow, WorkflowRun, WorkflowScheduleState, WorkflowTrigger,
};
use crate::schedule::{due_slot, next_slot, Schedule, Until};
use crate::util::timestamp;
use crate::workflow::Trigger;
use crate::workflows::{StartRequest, Workflows};
use crate::{Error, Result};

/// What a workflow's schedule trigger asks for: its schedule, its task and the name of its
/// project.
struct Scheduled<'a> {
    team: &'a Team,
    workflow: &'a Workflow,
    schedule: &'a Schedule,
    task: &'a str,
    project: &'a str,
}

impl Scheduler {
    /// The scheduled workflows' part of a pass. Returns the earliest next slot among them;
    /// `None` when none has one, or when no workflows are set. A workflow whose slot could not
    /// be handled is logged and tried again on the next pass.
    pub(super) async fn workflow_pass(&self, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<Option<DateTime<Utc>>> {
        let Some(workflows) = self.workflows.get() else {
            return Ok(None);
        };
        let mut rows: HashMap<String, WorkflowScheduleState> = self
            .store
            .list_workflow_schedule_states()
            .await?
            .into_iter()
            .map(|row| (row.workflow_id.clone(), row))
            .collect();
        let mut wake: Option<DateTime<Utc>> = None;
        for team in self.store.list_teams().await? {
            for workflow in self.store.list_workflows(&team.id).await? {
                let Trigger::Schedule {
                    schedule,
                    task,
                    project,
                } = &workflow.spec.definition.trigger
                else {
                    continue;
                };
                if !workflow.spec.enabled || workflow.problem.is_some() {
                    continue;
                }
                let row = rows.remove(&workflow.id);
                let scheduled = Scheduled {
                    team: &team,
                    workflow: &workflow,
                    schedule,
                    task,
                    project,
                };
                match self.handle_workflow(workflows, &scheduled, row, now).await {
                    Ok(next) => wake = wake.into_iter().chain(next).min(),
                    Err(e) => tracing::warn!(
                        workflow = %workflow.spec.name,
                        "the workflow's schedule could not be handled: {e}"
                    ),
                }
            }
        }
        // The rows left belong to workflows that no longer fire on a schedule.
        for workflow_id in rows.into_keys() {
            self.store
                .delete_workflow_schedule_state(&workflow_id)
                .await?;
        }
        Ok(wake)
    }

    /// One scheduled workflow's part of a pass (module docs): its row made or reset, the due
    /// slot started or skipped, the status updated. Returns its next slot, `None` when it has
    /// none.
    async fn handle_workflow(
        &self,
        workflows: &Workflows,
        s: &Scheduled<'_>,
        row: Option<WorkflowScheduleState>,
        now: DateTime<Utc>,
    ) -> Result<Option<DateTime<Utc>>> {
        let id = &s.workflow.id;
        let name = &s.workflow.spec.name;
        let row = match row {
            Some(row) if row.rule_key == s.schedule.rule_key() => row,
            _ => {
                self.store
                    .sync_workflow_schedule_state(id, s.schedule, now)
                    .await?
            }
        };
        if row.status != ScheduleStatus::Active {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let mut last = parse_time(&row.last_slot_at)?;
        let mut row_slot = row.last_slot_at.clone();
        let mut fired = row.fired_count;
        let limit = match s.schedule.until {
            Some(Until::Count(n)) => Some(n),
            _ => None,
        };
        let due = match due_slot(s.schedule, &last, &now, &Local) {
            Ok(due) => due.map(|t| t.with_timezone(&Utc)),
            Err(e) => return self.expire_workflow(s.workflow, &e, &row_slot, now).await,
        };
        if let Some(due) = due {
            if !self.store.record_workflow_slot(id, due, now).await? {
                // Taken since the row was read: nothing to do.
                return Ok(None);
            }
            last = due;
            row_slot = timestamp(due);
            if limit.is_some_and(|n| fired >= n) {
                // The count was reached: the status below completes the row.
            } else if now - due >= ON_TIME {
                tracing::info!(
                    workflow = %name, slot = %due,
                    "missed slot skipped: a workflow schedule never catches up"
                );
            } else if self.store.workflow_has_runs_in_flight(id).await? {
                tracing::info!(
                    workflow = %name, slot = %due,
                    "slot skipped: a run of the workflow is still in flight"
                );
            } else {
                match start(workflows, s).await {
                    Ok(wr) => {
                        self.store.count_workflow_slot(id, now).await?;
                        fired += 1;
                        tracing::info!(
                            workflow = %name, workflow_run = %wr.id, slot = %due,
                            "scheduled workflow started"
                        );
                    }
                    Err(e) => tracing::warn!(
                        workflow = %name, slot = %due,
                        "scheduled workflow not started, the slot is used up: {e}"
                    ),
                }
            }
        }
        let next = if limit.is_some_and(|n| fired >= n) {
            None
        } else {
            match next_slot(s.schedule, &last, &Local) {
                Ok(next) => next.map(|t| t.with_timezone(&Utc)),
                Err(e) => return self.expire_workflow(s.workflow, &e, &row_slot, now).await,
            }
        };
        if next.is_none() {
            // No slot is left: the count is reached, a `Once` is handled, or the stop date has
            // passed.
            if self
                .store
                .set_workflow_schedule_status(id, ScheduleStatus::Completed, &row_slot, now)
                .await?
            {
                tracing::info!(workflow = %name, "workflow schedule completed");
            }
        }
        Ok(next)
    }

    /// The schedule no longer gives slots (an `Every` without an anchor, a cron expression
    /// that no longer parses): the row is `expired` and fires nothing until its rule changes.
    async fn expire_workflow(
        &self,
        workflow: &Workflow,
        e: &Error,
        last_slot_at: &str,
        now: DateTime<Utc>,
    ) -> Result<Option<DateTime<Utc>>> {
        if self
            .store
            .set_workflow_schedule_status(&workflow.id, ScheduleStatus::Expired, last_slot_at, now)
            .await?
        {
            tracing::warn!(workflow = %workflow.spec.name, "workflow schedule expired: {e}");
        }
        Ok(None)
    }
}

/// Starts the scheduled workflow on the project its trigger names, found by name without case
/// among its team's projects, with the trigger's task. `Workflows::start` refuses what it
/// refuses for any start.
async fn start(workflows: &Workflows, s: &Scheduled<'_>) -> Result<WorkflowRun> {
    let wanted = s.project.trim();
    let Some(project) = s
        .team
        .projects
        .iter()
        .find(|p| p.spec.name.trim().eq_ignore_ascii_case(wanted))
    else {
        return Err(Error::NotFound(format!(
            "the team has no project named \"{wanted}\""
        )));
    };
    workflows
        .start(StartRequest {
            workflow_id: s.workflow.id.clone(),
            project_id: project.id.clone(),
            task: s.task.to_string(),
            trigger: WorkflowTrigger::Schedule,
            origin_run_id: None,
            test: false,
        })
        .await
}
```

```rust
/// The schedule of a schedule trigger.
fn trigger_schedule(spec: &WorkflowSpec) -> Option<&Schedule> {
    match &spec.definition.trigger {
        Trigger::Schedule { schedule, .. } => Some(schedule),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// The spec as it is stored: a schedule trigger's schedule prepared against `previous`, the
/// stored one, as a dot's is (`Schedule::prepare_save`: the `Every` anchor, and the refusal of
/// a new or changed schedule that would never run).
fn prepared(spec: &WorkflowSpec, previous: Option<&Schedule>) -> Result<WorkflowSpec> {
    let mut spec = spec.clone();
    if let Trigger::Schedule { schedule, .. } = &mut spec.definition.trigger {
        // Whole milliseconds, as `util::now()`: the stored anchor reads back the same.
        schedule
            .prepare_save(previous, Utc::now().trunc_subsecs(3), &Local)
            .map_err(|e| match e {
                Error::Invalid(m) => Error::Invalid(format!("the trigger's schedule: {m}")),
                e => e,
            })?;
    }
    Ok(spec)
}
```

```rust
    /// Replaces a workflow's name, definition and switch, validated against its team's members,
    /// its schedule prepared against the stored one (`prepared`).
    pub async fn update_workflow(&self, id: &str, spec: &WorkflowSpec) -> Result<Workflow> {
        let stored = self.get_workflow(id).await?;
        let team_id = stored.team_id.clone();
        spec.validate(&self.team_member_ids(&team_id).await?)?;
        let spec = &prepared(spec, trigger_schedule(&stored.spec))?;
```

**Create `crates/dots-core/src/store/workflow_schedules.rs`:**

```rust
//! The `workflow_schedule_state` rows: where each scheduled workflow's schedule stands (teams
//! spec section 5.1). Kept as a dot's `schedule_state` is (`store/schedules.rs`), without a
//! watched run, a retry or attention: a workflow schedule never catches up and never retries.
//! The scheduler's pass makes, resets and deletes the rows and handles their slots. Callers
//! pass `now`, so the scheduler's clock decides every time written here.

use chrono::{DateTime, Utc};
use sqlx::sqlite::SqliteRow;
use sqlx::Row;

use super::Store;
use crate::model::{ScheduleStatus, WorkflowScheduleState};
use crate::schedule::Schedule;
use crate::util::timestamp;
use crate::Result;

const STATE_COLUMNS: &str = "workflow_id, rule_key, status, last_slot_at, fired_count, updated_at";

fn state_from_row(row: &SqliteRow) -> Result<WorkflowScheduleState> {
    Ok(WorkflowScheduleState {
        workflow_id: row.try_get("workflow_id")?,
        rule_key: row.try_get("rule_key")?,
        status: ScheduleStatus::parse(&row.try_get::<String, _>("status")?)?,
        last_slot_at: row.try_get("last_slot_at")?,
        fired_count: row.try_get::<i64, _>("fired_count")? as u32,
        updated_at: row.try_get("updated_at")?,
    })
}

impl Store {
    /// The workflow's schedule row, if it has one.
    pub async fn get_workflow_schedule_state(
        &self,
        workflow_id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<WorkflowScheduleState>> {
        let row = sqlx::query(&format!(
            "SELECT {STATE_COLUMNS} FROM workflow_schedule_state WHERE workflow_id = ?"
        ))
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .fetch_optional(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        row.as_ref().map(state_from_row).transpose()
    }

    /// Every workflow's schedule row, by workflow id.
    pub async fn list_workflow_schedule_states(&self) -> Result<Vec<WorkflowScheduleState>> {
        let rows = sqlx::query(&format!(
            "SELECT {STATE_COLUMNS} FROM workflow_schedule_state ORDER BY workflow_id"
        ))
        .fetch_all(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        rows.iter().map(state_from_row).collect()
    }

    /// Makes the workflow's row for `schedule`, or resets it when its `rule_key` is not the
    /// schedule's, as `sync_schedule_state` does a dot's: the row is `active`, with
    /// `last_slot_at = now` and no count, so no slot from before is ever started. A row whose
    /// key is the schedule's is left as it is. Returns the row.
    pub async fn sync_workflow_schedule_state(
        &self,
        workflow_id: &str,
        schedule: &Schedule,
        now: DateTime<Utc>,
    ) -> Result<WorkflowScheduleState> {
        let ts = timestamp(now);
        sqlx::query(
            "INSERT INTO workflow_schedule_state (workflow_id, rule_key, status, last_slot_at, \
             fired_count, updated_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, 0, ?) \
             ON CONFLICT (workflow_id) DO UPDATE SET rule_key = excluded.rule_key, \
             status = excluded.status, last_slot_at = excluded.last_slot_at, fired_count = 0, \
             updated_at = excluded.updated_at \
             WHERE workflow_schedule_state.rule_key <> excluded.rule_key",
        )
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .bind(schedule.rule_key())
        .bind(ScheduleStatus::Active.as_str())
        .bind(&ts)
        .bind(&ts)
        .execute(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        let row = sqlx::query(&format!(
            "SELECT {STATE_COLUMNS} FROM workflow_schedule_state WHERE workflow_id = ?"
        ))
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .fetch_one(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        state_from_row(&row)
    }

    /// Deletes the workflow's row. True when there was one.
    pub async fn delete_workflow_schedule_state(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<bool> {
        let res = sqlx::query("DELETE FROM workflow_schedule_state WHERE workflow_id = ?")
            .bind(workflow_id)
            .execute(&self.pool)
            .await?;
        Ok(res.rows_affected() > 0)
    }

    /// Takes the due slot `at`: `last_slot_at = at`. Written only while the row's
    /// `last_slot_at` is before `at`, so a slot is taken once, before its run is started; false
    /// when nothing was written (no row, or the slot was taken already).
    pub async fn record_workflow_slot(
        &self,
        workflow_id: &str,
        at: DateTime<Utc>,
        now: DateTime<Utc>,
    ) -> Result<bool> {
        let slot = timestamp(at);
        let res = sqlx::query(
            "UPDATE workflow_schedule_state SET last_slot_at = ?, updated_at = ? \
             WHERE workflow_id = ? AND last_slot_at < ?",
        )
        .bind(&slot)
        .bind(timestamp(now))
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .bind(&slot)
        .execute(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        Ok(res.rows_affected() > 0)
    }

    /// Counts a slot that started a workflow run.
    pub async fn count_workflow_slot(&self, workflow_id: &str, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<()> {
        sqlx::query(
            "UPDATE workflow_schedule_state SET fired_count = fired_count + 1, updated_at = ? \
             WHERE workflow_id = ?",
        )
        .bind(timestamp(now))
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .execute(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Writes `status` while the row's `last_slot_at` is still `last_slot_at`, as the pass left
    /// it. True when written.
    pub async fn set_workflow_schedule_status(
        &self,
        workflow_id: &str,
        status: ScheduleStatus,
        last_slot_at: &str,
        now: DateTime<Utc>,
    ) -> Result<bool> {
        let res = sqlx::query(
            "UPDATE workflow_schedule_state SET status = ?, updated_at = ? \
             WHERE workflow_id = ? AND last_slot_at = ?",
        )
        .bind(status.as_str())
        .bind(timestamp(now))
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .bind(last_slot_at)
        .execute(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        Ok(res.rows_affected() > 0)
    }

    /// A resume after a pause, as `resume_schedules` does for dots: every active row's
    /// `last_slot_at` before `now` becomes `now`, so no slot of the pause is started. Returns
    /// the workflows whose row moved.
    pub async fn resume_workflow_schedules(&self, now: DateTime<Utc>) -> Result<Vec<String>> {
        let ts = timestamp(now);
        let ids = sqlx::query_scalar(
            "UPDATE workflow_schedule_state SET last_slot_at = ?, updated_at = ? \
             WHERE status = ? AND last_slot_at < ? RETURNING workflow_id",
        )
        .bind(&ts)
        .bind(&ts)
        .bind(ScheduleStatus::Active.as_str())
        .bind(&ts)
        .fetch_all(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        Ok(ids)
    }

    /// Whether the workflow has a run that has not ended (running or waiting).
    pub async fn workflow_has_runs_in_flight(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> Result<bool> {
        let n: i64 = sqlx::query_scalar(
            "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM workflow_runs WHERE workflow_id = ? \
             AND status IN ('running', 'waiting')",
        )
        .bind(workflow_id)
        .fetch_one(&self.pool)
        .await?;
        Ok(n > 0)
    }
}
```

### Task 10: Workflows start from a webhook

`POST /workflows/{id}/trigger` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` starts a workflow with a webhook trigger, on the body's `task` and `project` (by name), each falling back to the trigger's. The token is hashed in `workflows.webhook_token` as a dot's is, and `Teams::regenerate_workflow_token` hands out a new one. `teams::project_named` finds a project by name for both the schedule and the webhook.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/dots-core/src/server/workflow_webhook.rs`
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/migrations/0027_teams_b.sql`, `crates/dots-core/src/{model.rs,teams.rs,scheduler/workflow_slots.rs,server/mod.rs,store/teams.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/workflow_webhook_test.rs` (new), `crates/dots-core/tests/runtime_roles_test.rs` (the 403 test, beside the dots' webhook role test)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's `Trigger::Webhook`; Task 9's `scheduler/workflow_slots.rs::start`; `server::webhook::{bearer, payload, error}`; `util::{token_hash, ct_eq, new_token}`; `Workflows::start`, reached as `runner.checkins()?.workflows()`.
- Produces: `workflows.webhook_token TEXT` (nullable); `Workflow.has_webhook_token: bool`; `Store::{check_workflow_token(&self, id: &str, token: &str) -> Result<bool>, regenerate_workflow_token(&self, id: &str) -> Result<String>}`; `Teams::regenerate_workflow_token(&self, id: &str) -> Result<String>` (EditDots; sends `WorkflowUpdated`); `teams::project_named<'a>(team: &'a Team, name: &str) -> Option<&'a Project>`; `server::workflow_webhook::trigger` (axum handler). Answers: 202 `{"workflow_run_id"}`; 404 `unknown workflow`; 401 `invalid token`; 409 `the workflow does not start from a webhook`, `workflow <name> is turned off`, `workflow <name> cannot start: <problem>`; 400 `body must be JSON: <error>`, `body must be a JSON object whose "task" and "project", when given, are strings`, `no task: give "task" in the body, or a task in the workflow's webhook trigger`, `no project: give "project" in the body, or a project in the workflow's webhook trigger`, `the team has no project named "<name>"`; 403 the role's refusal; 503 `workflows are not running`.

**with:**

```rust

/// A workflow's webhook (teams spec section 5.1) in an account that does not let this PC run
/// dots is refused with 403 and the refusal's words, and starts nothing; a Viewer may not make
/// it a new token either.
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_workflow_webhook_is_refused_when_the_role_cannot_run_dots() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    t.join_as("member").await;
    let dot =
        t.rt.create_dot(t.triage(), Some(ACME.into()), None)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    let store = t.rt.store();
    let team =
        t.rt.teams()
            .create(&TeamSpec {
                name: "crew".into(),
                description: String::new(),
                members: vec![MemberSpec {
                    dot_id: dot.id.clone(),
                    role: String::new(),
                }],
                org_id: Some(ACME.into()),
                workspace_id: "default".into(),
            })
            .await
            .unwrap();
    // Added in the store: the folder is not a git repo, which the store does not check, and
    // the role is refused before the repo is looked at.
    store
        .add_project(
            &team.id,
            &ProjectSpec {
                name: "repo".into(),
                repo_path: t.dir.path().to_string_lossy().to_string(),
                default_branch: None,
                remote: "origin".into(),
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let mut spec = flow(&dot.id);
    spec.definition.trigger = Trigger::Webhook {
        task: Some("Fix it.".into()),
        project: Some("repo".into()),
    };
    let workflow = t.rt.teams().create_workflow(&team.id, &spec).await.unwrap();
    let token =
        t.rt.teams()
            .regenerate_workflow_token(&workflow.id)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    let url = format!(
        "http://127.0.0.1:{}/workflows/{}/trigger",
        t.rt.port(),
        workflow.id
    );
    let call = || reqwest::Client::new().post(&url).bearer_auth(&token).send();

    t.become_(2, Some("viewer")).await;
    let resp = call().await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(resp.status(), 403);
    let body: Value = resp.json().await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(body["error"], viewer_cant("run dots"));
    assert_eq!(
        refused(t.rt.teams().regenerate_workflow_token(&workflow.id).await),
        viewer_cant("create or change dots")
    );

    t.become_(3, None).await;
    let resp = call().await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(resp.status(), 403);
    let body: Value = resp.json().await.unwrap();
    assert_eq!(body["error"], "Acme Engineering: you were removed from it.");
    assert!(store
        .list_workflow_runs(Some(&team.id), 100)
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .is_empty());
    t.rt.shutdown();
}
```

**Create `crates/dots-core/tests/workflow_webhook_test.rs`:**

```rust
//! A workflow's webhook (teams spec section 5.1): `POST /workflows/{id}/trigger` with the
//! workflow's token starts a run on the body's task and project, each falling back to the
//! webhook trigger's own, through Dots' own server. The workflow's one step is the person's
//! check, so a started run waits there and no engine runs. A role that may not run dots is
//! refused in `runtime_roles_test` (it needs an account's member list).

mod common;

use std::path::PathBuf;

use common::{init_repo, spec};
use dots_core::model::{
    Dot, MemberSpec, ProjectSpec, TeamSpec, Workflow, WorkflowRun, WorkflowSpec, WorkflowTrigger,
};
use dots_core::workflow::{Budget, Definition, Step, StepKind, Trigger};
use dots_core::{Config, Runtime};
use serde_json::{json, Value};
use tempfile::TempDir;

/// The task the trigger falls back to in these tests.
const TRIGGER_TASK: &str = "Triage the crash reports that came in.";

struct Rt {
    _dir: TempDir,
    rt: Runtime,
    team: String,
    dev: Dot,
    other: Dot,
    http: reqwest::Client,
}

impl Rt {
    /// A runtime on Dots' own server (a free port), with a team of `dev` and `other` and two
    /// projects, "Dots" and "Site".
    async fn start() -> Rt {
        let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
        let mut cfg = Config::new(dir.path().join("data"));
        cfg.port = 0;
        // Never the real CLI: no run here reaches an engine.
        cfg.claude_path = Some(PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_fake-claude")));
        let rt = Runtime::start(cfg).await.unwrap();
        let dev = rt
            .store()
            .create_dot(&spec("dev"), None, "default")
            .await
            .unwrap();
        let other = rt
            .store()
            .create_dot(&spec("other"), None, "default")
            .await
            .unwrap();
        let team = rt
            .teams()
            .create(&TeamSpec {
                org_id: None,
                workspace_id: "default".into(),
                name: "Dots management".into(),
                description: String::new(),
                members: vec![member(&dev), member(&other)],
            })
            .await
            .unwrap()
            .id;
        for name in ["Dots", "Site"] {
            let repo = dir.path().join(name.to_lowercase());
            init_repo(&repo);
            rt.teams()
                .add_project(
                    &team,
                    &ProjectSpec {
                        name: name.into(),
                        repo_path: repo.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
                        default_branch: None,
                        remote: "origin".into(),
                    },
                )
                .await
                .unwrap();
        }
        Rt {
            _dir: dir,
            rt,
            team,
            dev,
            other,
            http: reqwest::Client::new(),
        }
    }

    /// The id of the team's project named `name`.
    async fn project(&self, name: &str) -> String {
        let team = self.rt.teams().get(&self.team).await.unwrap();
        let project = team.projects.iter().find(|p| p.spec.name == name).unwrap();
        project.id.clone()
    }

    /// A workflow "Fix" with `trigger` and the person's check as its one step.
    async fn workflow(&self, trigger: Trigger) -> Workflow {
        self.rt
            .teams()
            .create_workflow(&self.team, &flow(trigger, check()))
            .await
            .unwrap()
    }

    /// A webhook workflow whose trigger falls back to `task` and `project`, and its token.
    async fn hooked(&self, task: Option<&str>, project: Option<&str>) -> (Workflow, String) {
        let workflow = self.workflow(webhook(task, project)).await;
        let token = self
            .rt
            .teams()
            .regenerate_workflow_token(&workflow.id)
            .await
            .unwrap();
        (workflow, token)
    }

    fn url(&self, workflow_id: &str) -> String {
        format!(
            "http://127.0.0.1:{}/workflows/{workflow_id}/trigger",
            self.rt.port()
        )
    }

    /// Posts `body` (raw, sent as JSON) to the workflow's webhook with `token` as the bearer;
    /// the status and the JSON answer.
    async fn post(
        &self,
        workflow_id: &str,
        token: Option<&str>,
        body: Option<&str>,
    ) -> (u16, Value) {
        let mut req = self.http.post(self.url(workflow_id));
        if let Some(token) = token {
            req = req.bearer_auth(token);
        }
        if let Some(body) = body {
            req = req
                .header("content-type", "application/json")
                .body(body.to_string());
        }
        let resp = req.send().await.unwrap();
        let status = resp.status().as_u16();
        (status, resp.json().await.unwrap())
    }

    /// The workflow run a 202 answer named.
    async fn started(&self, answer: (u16, Value)) -> WorkflowRun {
        let (status, body) = answer;
        assert_eq!(status, 202, "{body}");
        let id = body["workflow_run_id"].as_str().unwrap();
        self.rt.store().get_workflow_run(id).await.unwrap()
    }

    async fn runs(&self) -> usize {
        self.rt
            .store()
            .list_workflow_runs(Some(&self.team), 100)
            .await
            .unwrap()
            .len()
    }
}

fn member(dot: &Dot) -> MemberSpec {
    MemberSpec {
        dot_id: dot.id.clone(),
        role: "dev agent".into(),
    }
}

fn check() -> Step {
    Step {
        id: "check".into(),
        name: "Your check".into(),
        kind: StepKind::Check { send_back_to: None },
    }
}

fn webhook(task: Option<&str>, project: Option<&str>) -> Trigger {
    Trigger::Webhook {
        task: task.map(str::to_string),
        project: project.map(str::to_string),
    }
}

fn flow(trigger: Trigger, step: Step) -> WorkflowSpec {
    WorkflowSpec {
        name: "Fix".into(),
        definition: Definition {
            trigger,
            steps: vec![step],
            budget: Budget::default(),
        },
        enabled: true,
    }
}

fn error(answer: &(u16, Value)) -> &str {
    answer.1["error"].as_str().unwrap()
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_call_starts_a_run_on_the_body_s_task_and_project_found_without_case() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")).await;
    let body = json!({"task": "  Fix the login crash.  ", "project": " site "}).to_string();
    let wr = t
        .started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(&body)).await)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(wr.workflow_id.as_deref(), Some(workflow.id.as_str()));
    assert_eq!(wr.task, "Fix the login crash.");
    assert_eq!(wr.project_id, Some(t.project("Site").await));
    assert_eq!(wr.project.name, "Site");
    assert_eq!(wr.trigger, WorkflowTrigger::Webhook);
    assert_eq!(wr.origin_run_id, None);
    assert!(!wr.test);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn the_body_falls_back_to_the_trigger_s_task_and_project() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("dots")).await;
    let dots = t.project("Dots").await;
    // No body.
    let wr = t
        .started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(
        (wr.task.as_str(), wr.project_id.clone()),
        (TRIGGER_TASK, Some(dots.clone()))
    );
    // A blank body, an empty object, blank values and nulls.
    for body in [
        "  ",
        "{}",
        r#"{"task": "  ", "project": ""}"#,
        r#"{"task": null, "project": null}"#,
    ] {
        let wr = t
            .started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(body)).await)
            .await;
        assert_eq!(
            (wr.task.as_str(), wr.project_id.clone()),
            (TRIGGER_TASK, Some(dots.clone())),
            "{body}"
        );
    }
    // Only the task, with another sender's own keys, which are ignored.
    let body = json!({"task": "Fix the build.", "action": "opened", "number": 7}).to_string();
    let wr = t
        .started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(&body)).await)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(
        (wr.task.as_str(), wr.project_id),
        ("Fix the build.", Some(dots))
    );
    // Only the project.
    let body = json!({"project": "SITE"}).to_string();
    let wr = t
        .started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(&body)).await)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(
        (wr.task.as_str(), wr.project_id),
        (TRIGGER_TASK, Some(t.project("Site").await))
    );
    assert_eq!(t.runs().await, 7);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_missing_task_or_project_or_an_unknown_project_is_400() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(None, None).await;
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await;
    assert_eq!(answer.0, 400);
    assert_eq!(
        error(&answer),
        "no task: give \"task\" in the body, or a task in the workflow's webhook trigger"
    );
    let body = json!({"task": "Fix it."}).to_string();
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(&body)).await;
    assert_eq!(answer.0, 400);
    assert_eq!(
        error(&answer),
        "no project: give \"project\" in the body, or a project in the workflow's webhook trigger"
    );
    let body = json!({"task": "Fix it.", "project": " Docs "}).to_string();
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(&body)).await;
    assert_eq!(answer.0, 400);
    assert_eq!(error(&answer), "the team has no project named \"Docs\"");
    // `Workflows::start`'s refusal of a task too long.
    let body = json!({"task": "x".repeat(8_001), "project": "Dots"}).to_string();
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(&body)).await;
    assert_eq!(answer.0, 400);
    assert_eq!(error(&answer), "the task must be 1 to 8,000 characters");
    // A trigger project the team does not have.
    let (workflow, token) = {
        let mut spec = flow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Gone")), check());
        spec.name = "Gone".into();
        let w = t.rt.teams().create_workflow(&t.team, &spec).await.unwrap();
        let token = t.rt.teams().regenerate_workflow_token(&w.id).await.unwrap();
        (w, token)
    };
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await;
    assert_eq!(answer.0, 400);
    assert_eq!(error(&answer), "the team has no project named \"Gone\"");
    assert_eq!(t.runs().await, 0);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_body_that_is_not_json_of_that_shape_is_400() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")).await;
    let shape =
        "body must be a JSON object whose \"task\" and \"project\", when given, are strings";
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some("task=fix")).await;
    assert_eq!(answer.0, 400);
    assert!(
        error(&answer).starts_with("body must be JSON: "),
        "{}",
        error(&answer)
    );
    for body in [
        r#"["Fix it.", "Dots"]"#,
        r#""Fix it.""#,
        "7",
        r#"{"task": 7}"#,
        r#"{"project": ["Dots"]}"#,
    ] {
        let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some(body)).await;
        assert_eq!(answer.0, 400, "{body}");
        assert_eq!(error(&answer), shape, "{body}");
    }
    assert_eq!(t.runs().await, 0);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_missing_or_wrong_token_or_a_workflow_without_one_is_401() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let workflow = t.workflow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots"))).await;
    assert!(!workflow.has_webhook_token);
    for token in [None, Some(""), Some("anything")] {
        let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, token, None).await;
        assert_eq!(
            answer,
            (401, json!({"error": "invalid token"})),
            "{token:?}"
        );
    }
    let token =
        t.rt.teams()
            .regenerate_workflow_token(&workflow.id)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, None, None).await.0, 401);
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some("wrong"), None).await.0, 401);
    // Its hash is not the token either.
    let hash = dots_core::util::token_hash(&token);
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&hash), None).await.0, 401);
    // A token in the URL is refused, as a dot's: it would end up in proxy logs.
    let resp = t
        .http
        .post(format!("{}?token={token}", t.url(&workflow.id)))
        .send()
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(resp.status(), 401);
    // Another workflow's token.
    let (_, other_token) = {
        let mut spec = flow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")), check());
        spec.name = "Other".into();
        let w = t.rt.teams().create_workflow(&t.team, &spec).await.unwrap();
        let token = t.rt.teams().regenerate_workflow_token(&w.id).await.unwrap();
        (w, token)
    };
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&other_token), None).await.0, 401);
    assert_eq!(t.runs().await, 0);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn an_unknown_workflow_is_404() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")).await;
    let answer = t.post("no-such-workflow", Some(&token), None).await;
    assert_eq!(answer, (404, json!({"error": "unknown workflow"})));
    t.rt.teams().delete_workflow(&workflow.id).await.unwrap();
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await;
    assert_eq!(answer, (404, json!({"error": "unknown workflow"})));
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_workflow_without_a_webhook_trigger_turned_off_or_with_a_problem_is_409() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let teams = t.rt.teams();
    // Another trigger: its token is kept, but it does not start from a webhook.
    let (workflow, token) = t.hooked(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")).await;
    let manual = teams
        .update_workflow(&workflow.id, &flow(Trigger::Manual, check()))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert!(manual.has_webhook_token);
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await;
    assert_eq!(
        answer,
        (
            409,
            json!({"error": "the workflow does not start from a webhook"})
        )
    );
    // Turned off.
    let mut off = flow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")), check());
    off.enabled = false;
    teams.update_workflow(&workflow.id, &off).await.unwrap();
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), None).await;
    assert_eq!(
        answer,
        (409, json!({"error": "workflow Fix is turned off"}))
    );
    // A problem: its agent step's member left the team. The body is not read.
    let agent = Step {
        id: "work".into(),
        name: "Work".into(),
        kind: StepKind::Agent {
            member: t.other.id.clone(),
            job: "Fix it.".into(),
            can_send_back_to: vec![],
            outcomes: vec![],
            delegate: None,
        },
    };
    teams
        .update_workflow(
            &workflow.id,
            &flow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots")), agent),
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    teams
        .update(
            &t.team,
            &TeamSpec {
                org_id: None,
                workspace_id: "default".into(),
                name: "Dots management".into(),
                description: String::new(),
                members: vec![member(&t.dev)],
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let problem = teams
        .get_workflow(&workflow.id)
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .problem
        .unwrap();
    let answer = t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&token), Some("not json")).await;
    assert_eq!(answer.0, 409);
    assert_eq!(
        error(&answer),
        format!("workflow Fix cannot start: {problem}")
    );
    assert_eq!(t.runs().await, 0);
    t.rt.shutdown();
}

#[tokio::test]
async fn a_new_token_replaces_the_old_one_and_shows_on_the_workflow() {
    let t = Rt::start().await;
    let workflow = t.workflow(webhook(Some(TRIGGER_TASK), Some("Dots"))).await;
    assert!(!workflow.has_webhook_token);
    let mut events = t.rt.subscribe();
    let first =
        t.rt.teams()
            .regenerate_workflow_token(&workflow.id)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    // Announced, so a page showing the workflow reads it again.
    let event = tokio::time::timeout(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5), async {
        loop {
            if let Ok(dots_core::events::RuntimeEvent::WorkflowUpdated { workflow_id, .. }) =
                events.recv().await
            {
                break workflow_id;
            }
        }
    })
    .await
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(event, workflow.id);
    let stored = t.rt.teams().get_workflow(&workflow.id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(stored.has_webhook_token);
    // Only its hash is kept.
    let json = serde_json::to_value(&stored).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(json["has_webhook_token"], json!(true));
    assert!(!json.to_string().contains(&first));
    t.started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&first), None).await)
        .await;

    let second =
        t.rt.teams()
            .regenerate_workflow_token(&workflow.id)
            .await
            .unwrap();
    assert_ne!(first, second);
    assert_eq!(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&first), None).await.0, 401);
    t.started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&second), None).await)
        .await;
    // Saving the workflow keeps its token.
    let saved =
        t.rt.teams()
            .update_workflow(
                &workflow.id,
                &flow(webhook(Some("Another task."), Some("Dots")), check()),
            )
            .await
            .unwrap();
    assert!(saved.has_webhook_token);
    let wr = t
        .started(t.post(&workflow.id, Some(&second), None).await)
        .await;
    assert_eq!(wr.task, "Another task.");
    assert!(matches!(
        t.rt.teams()
            .regenerate_workflow_token("no-such-workflow")
            .await,
        Err(dots_core::Error::NotFound(_))
    ));
    t.rt.shutdown();
}
```
