**Create `crates/dots-core/src/server/workflow_webhook.rs`:**

```rust
//! A workflow's webhook (teams spec section 5.1): `POST /workflows/{id}/trigger` with the
//! workflow's token as a bearer starts a run of it, as a dot's webhook (`webhook.rs`) queues a
//! run of the dot. The body names the task and the project; each falls back to the webhook
//! trigger's own.

use axum::body::Bytes;
use axum::extract::{Path, State};
use axum::http::{HeaderMap, StatusCode};
use axum::response::{IntoResponse, Response};
use axum::Json;
use serde::Deserialize;
use serde_json::json;

use super::webhook::{bearer, error, payload};
use super::ServerState;
use crate::model::WorkflowTrigger;
use crate::workflow::Trigger;
use crate::workflows::StartRequest;
use crate::Error;

/// What a body may ask for. Other keys are ignored, so a sender's own payload can start the
/// workflow with the trigger's task and project.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Deserialize)]
struct Asked {
    #[serde(default)]
    task: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default)]
    project: Option<String>,
}

/// The body's task and project: none asked when it is empty or blank; a 400 answer when it is
/// not JSON, not an object, or its `task` or `project` is not a string.
fn asked(body: &[u8]) -> Result<Asked, Box<Response>> {
    let Some(value) = payload(body)? else {
        return Ok(Asked::default());
    };
    // Checked first: serde would read an array as the struct's fields in order.
    if !value.is_object() {
        return Err(Box::new(error(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, SHAPE)));
    }
    serde_json::from_value(value).map_err(|_| Box::new(error(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, SHAPE)))
}

/// The 400 answer to a body of another shape.
const SHAPE: &str = "body must be a JSON object whose \"task\" and \"project\", when given, are \
                     strings";

/// The body's value when it is not blank, else the trigger's fallback when that is not blank.
fn given(asked: Option<String>, fallback: &Option<String>) -> Option<String> {
    [asked.as_deref(), fallback.as_deref()]
        .into_iter()
        .flatten()
        .map(str::trim)
        .find(|v| !v.is_empty())
        .map(str::to_string)
}

/// The answer to a start `Workflows::start` refused: a role that may not run dots is 403, a
/// bad task or a project that cannot be worked on is 400, a workflow that cannot start now is
/// 409, with the refusal's words.
fn refused(e: Error) -> Response {
    match e {
        Error::NotAllowed(text) => error(StatusCode::FORBIDDEN, &text),
        Error::Invalid(text) => error(StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST, &text),
        Error::Conflict(text) => error(StatusCode::CONFLICT, &text),
        // Deleted since it was read.
        Error::NotFound(_) => error(StatusCode::NOT_FOUND, "unknown workflow"),
        e => error(StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, &e.to_string()),
    }
}

/// Starts a run of the workflow `id` (module docs). Answers 202 `{"workflow_run_id"}`; 404
/// `unknown workflow`; 401 `invalid token` (none given, or the workflow has none); 409 for a
/// workflow without a webhook trigger, turned off or with a problem; 400 for a body of another
/// shape, no task or project, or a project the team does not have; and `Workflows::start`'s
/// refusals (`refused`).
pub async fn trigger(
    State(s): State<ServerState>,
    Path(id): Path<String>,
    headers: HeaderMap,
    body: Bytes,
) -> Response {
    let workflow = match s.store.get_workflow(&id).await {
        Ok(w) => w,
        Err(Error::NotFound(_)) => return error(StatusCode::NOT_FOUND, "unknown workflow"),
        Err(e) => return error(StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, &e.to_string()),
    };
    // A bearer header only, as a dot's: a token in the URL ends up in proxy logs.
    let valid = match bearer(&headers) {
        Some(token) => match s.store.check_workflow_token(&workflow.id, &token).await {
            Ok(valid) => valid,
            Err(e) => return error(StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, &e.to_string()),
        },
        None => false,
    };
    if !valid {
        return error(StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED, "invalid token");
    }
    let Trigger::Webhook {
        task: trigger_task,
        project: trigger_project,
    } = &workflow.spec.definition.trigger
    else {
        return error(
            StatusCode::CONFLICT,
            "the workflow does not start from a webhook",
        );
    };
    // `Workflows::start`'s words, answered before the body is read: a workflow that cannot
    // start is 409 whatever the body asks.
    let name = &workflow.spec.name;
    if !workflow.spec.enabled {
        return error(
            StatusCode::CONFLICT,
            &format!("workflow {name} is turned off"),
        );
    }
    if let Some(problem) = &workflow.problem {
        return error(
            StatusCode::CONFLICT,
            &format!("workflow {name} cannot start: {problem}"),
        );
    }
    let asked = match asked(&body) {
        Ok(a) => a,
        Err(answer) => return *answer,
    };
    let Some(task) = given(asked.task, trigger_task) else {
        return error(
            StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
            "no task: give \"task\" in the body, or a task in the workflow's webhook trigger",
        );
    };
    let Some(project) = given(asked.project, trigger_project) else {
        return error(
            StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
            "no project: give \"project\" in the body, or a project in the workflow's webhook \
             trigger",
        );
    };
    let team = match s.store.get_team(&workflow.team_id).await {
        Ok(t) => t,
        Err(e) => return error(StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR, &e.to_string()),
    };
    let Some(project) = crate::teams::project_named(&team, &project) else {
        return error(
            StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
            &format!("the team has no project named \"{project}\""),
        );
    };
    let Some(workflows) = s.runner.checkins().and_then(|c| c.workflows()) else {
        return error(StatusCode::SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, "workflows are not running");
    };
    match workflows
        .start(StartRequest {
            workflow_id: workflow.id.clone(),
            project_id: project.id.clone(),
            task,
            trigger: WorkflowTrigger::Webhook,
            origin_run_id: None,
            test: false,
        })
        .await
    {
        Ok(wr) => (
            StatusCode::ACCEPTED,
            Json(json!({ "workflow_run_id": wr.id })),
        )
            .into_response(),
        Err(e) => refused(e),
    }
}
```

**In `crates/dots-core/src/store/teams.rs`, replace:**


**with:**

```rust

    /// Whether `token` is the workflow's webhook token, compared by its hash in constant time,
    /// as a dot's (`check_webhook_token`). A workflow without one takes none.
    pub async fn check_workflow_token(&self, id: &str, token: &str) -> Result<bool> {
        let stored: Option<Option<String>> =
            sqlx::query_scalar("SELECT webhook_token FROM workflows WHERE id = ?")
                .bind(id)
                .fetch_optional(&self.pool)
                .await?;
        let stored = stored.ok_or_else(|| Error::NotFound(format!("workflow {id}")))?;
        Ok(stored.is_some_and(|stored| !stored.is_empty() && ct_eq(&token_hash(token), &stored)))
    }

    /// Makes the workflow a new webhook token and returns it: the only time it is shown. Only
    /// its hash is kept, and the old token stops working.
    pub async fn regenerate_workflow_token(&self, id: &str) -> Result<String> {
        let token = new_token();
        let res =
            sqlx::query("UPDATE workflows SET webhook_token = ?, updated_at = ? WHERE id = ?")
                .bind(token_hash(&token))
                .bind(now())
                .bind(id)
                .execute(&self.pool)
                .await?;
        if res.rows_affected() == 0 {
            return Err(Error::NotFound(format!("workflow {id}")));
        }
        Ok(token)
    }

    /// The workflows `dot_id` may start from a chat (teams spec section 7), by team and name.
```

```rust
/// The team's project named `name`, compared without case and surrounding spaces, as a chat's
/// `start_workflow` and a branch on project match it: how a schedule or webhook trigger's
/// project name, or a webhook body's, is found.
pub fn project_named<'a>(team: &'a Team, name: &str) -> Option<&'a Project> {
    let wanted = name.trim();
    team.projects
        .iter()
        .find(|p| p.spec.name.trim().eq_ignore_ascii_case(wanted))
}
```

### Task 11: Workflow steps refuse late questions and follow-ups, and projects refuse bad branch names

`CheckIns::perform` refuses an `ask` or `follow_up` from a run whose step has ended (`STEP_CLOSED`), checked and written under the loop's lock (`lock_open_step`), so a Stop and a late call cannot cross. The runner ends a run whose own step was stopped before its engine started `Cancelled`, which fixes the Stop race Task 1 found. `check_git_name` refuses `..`, a trailing `/` and a part ending `.lock`; a project's default branch with a remote prefix is refused on save. `runner_workflow_test` waits up to 60 s.

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/dots-core/src/{checkins.rs,model.rs,runner.rs,teams.rs,workflows/tools.rs}`
- Test: `crates/dots-core/tests/{teams_test,workflows_test}.rs`; brought up to date (the waits only): `crates/dots-core/tests/runner_workflow_test.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 4's `STEP_CLOSED` and `Workflows::lock`; plan A's `CheckIns::perform`, the runner's `in_ended_step_thread`, `model::check_git_name`, `Teams::{add_project, update_project}`.

**with:**

```rust
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_project_refuses_names_git_refuses_and_a_default_branch_with_its_remote() {
    let (dir, store) = temp_store().await;
    let teams = Teams::new(store.clone(), new_bus());
    let t = teams.create(&team("T", &[])).await.unwrap();
    let repo = dir.path().join("repo");
    init_repo(&repo);
    let p = teams
        .add_project(&t.id, &project("Dots", &repo))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let with_branch = |branch: &str| {
        let mut spec = project("Dots", &repo);
        spec.default_branch = Some(branch.into());
        spec
    };
    // What git refuses in a ref name: `..`, a trailing `/`, a part that ends in `.lock`.
    for bad in ["fix..main", "feature/", "main.lock", "release.lock/1"] {
        assert!(
            matches!(
                teams.update_project(&p.id, &with_branch(bad)).await,
                Err(Error::Invalid(m)) if m == "the default branch must not contain '..' or end \
                    with '/', and no part of it may end with '.lock'"
            ),
            "{bad} was not refused"
        );
    }
    let mut remote = project("Dots", &repo);
    remote.remote = "up..stream".into();
    assert!(matches!(
        teams.update_project(&p.id, &remote).await,
        Err(Error::Invalid(m)) if m == "the remote must not contain '..' or end with '/', and no \
            part of it may end with '.lock'"
    ));
    for good in ["release/1.0", "feature/lock", "main.locked"] {
        let saved = teams
            .update_project(&p.id, &with_branch(good))
            .await
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(saved.spec.default_branch.as_deref(), Some(good));
    }
    // The project's own remote in front, though the repo has no remote yet.
    assert!(matches!(
        teams.update_project(&p.id, &with_branch("origin/main")).await,
        Err(Error::Invalid(m)) if m == "give the default branch without the remote: \"main\", \
            not \"origin/main\""
    ));
    // Another of the repo's remotes in front: a branch name until the repo has that remote.
    let saved = teams
        .update_project(&p.id, &with_branch("upstream/main"))
        .await
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(saved.spec.default_branch.as_deref(), Some("upstream/main"));
    let upstream = dir.path().join("upstream");
    init_repo(&upstream);
    common::git_out(
        &repo,
        &["remote", "add", "upstream", &upstream.to_string_lossy()],
    );
    assert!(matches!(
        teams.update_project(&p.id, &with_branch("upstream/main")).await,
        Err(Error::Invalid(m)) if m == "give the default branch without the remote: \"main\", \
            not \"upstream/main\""
    ));
    let mut added = with_branch("upstream/main");
    added.name = "Other".into();
    assert!(matches!(
        teams.add_project(&t.id, &added).await,
        Err(Error::Invalid(m)) if m.starts_with("give the default branch without the remote")
    ));
}

#[tokio::test]
```
```rust
async fn a_step_s_run_asks_and_schedules_follow_ups_only_while_its_step_is_open() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.engine.set(&w.dev.spec.name, vec![Act::Hold]);
    let flow = w
        .workflow("Fix", vec![agent("dev", "Fix it", &w.dev, "Fix.")])
        .await;
    let wr = w.start(&flow).await;
    let step = w.until(&wr.id, |wr| !wr.steps.is_empty()).await.steps[0]
        .id
        .clone();
    let run = loop {
        let runs = w.store.runs_of_step_run(&step).await.unwrap();
        if let Some(r) = runs.first().filter(|r| r.status == RunStatus::Running) {
            break r.clone();
        }
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(30)).await;
    };
    let follow_ups = FollowUps::new(w.store.clone(), w.bus.clone(), Arc::new(SystemClock));
    let checkins = CheckIns::new(w.store.clone(), w.bus.clone(), follow_ups.clone());
    checkins.set_workflows(&w.workflows);
    let ask = json!({"question": "Which icon set?"});
    let later = json!({"note": "Look again.", "in": "1h"});
    // While the step is open, its run schedules a follow-up as any run does.
    let scheduled = checkins.call(&run.id, CheckIn::FollowUp, &later).await;
    assert!(scheduled.unwrap().starts_with("Follow-up "));
    w.workflows.stop(&wr.id).await.unwrap();
    assert!(w
        .store
        .list_pending_follow_ups(Some(&w.dev.id))
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .is_empty());
    // A call that comes after the Stop closed the step, while the engine has not yet ended the
    // run, is refused and records nothing; with and without the workflow runs.
    let bare = CheckIns::new(w.store.clone(), w.bus.clone(), follow_ups);
    for checkins in [&checkins, &bare] {
        for (tool, input) in [(CheckIn::Ask, &ask), (CheckIn::FollowUp, &later)] {
            assert_eq!(
                checkins.call(&run.id, tool, input).await,
                Err(STEP_CLOSED.to_string())
            );
        }
    }
    let messages = w.store.list_messages(Some(&w.dev.id)).await.unwrap();
    assert!(messages
        .iter()
        .all(|m| m.run_id.as_deref() != Some(run.id.as_str())));
    assert!(w
        .store
        .list_pending_follow_ups(Some(&w.dev.id))
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .is_empty());
}

/// The window a Stop can hit: the step's run has turned `Running`, and the step is closed
/// before the runner reads it and before the Stop's cancel reaches the run. The queue is held
/// so the run is claimed only once its step is closed as a Stop closes it; nothing cancels the
/// run itself.
#[tokio::test]
async fn a_run_whose_step_was_stopped_before_its_engine_started_ends_cancelled() {
    let w = world().await;
    w.engine.set(&w.dev.spec.name, vec![Act::Hold]);
    let flow = w
        .workflow("Fix", vec![agent("dev", "Fix it", &w.dev, "Fix.")])
        .await;
    w.runner.set_held(true).await;
    let wr = w.start(&flow).await;
    let step = w.until(&wr.id, |wr| !wr.steps.is_empty()).await.steps[0]
        .id
        .clone();
    let run = loop {
        let runs = w.store.runs_of_step_run(&step).await.unwrap();
        if let Some(r) = runs.first() {
            break r.clone();
        }
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(30)).await;
    };
    assert_eq!(run.status, RunStatus::Queued);
    w.store
        .set_step_run(
            &step,
            &StepRunChange {
                status: Some(StepRunStatus::Cancelled),
                ..Default::default()
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    w.store
        .set_workflow_run(
            &wr.id,
            &WorkflowRunChange {
                status: Some(WorkflowRunStatus::Cancelled),
                waiting_on: Some(None),
                ..Default::default()
            },
        )
        .await
        .unwrap();
    w.runner.set_held(false).await;
    let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(60);
    let ended = loop {
        let r = w.store.get_run(&run.id).await.unwrap();
        if r.status.is_terminal() {
            break r;
        }
        assert!(std::time::Instant::now() < deadline, "the run never ended");
        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20)).await;
    };
    assert_eq!(ended.status, RunStatus::Cancelled);
    assert_eq!(ended.error, None);
    // The engine never started.
    assert!(w.engine.prompts_of(&w.dev.spec.name).is_empty());
}
```

```rust
                ))
```

```rust
    /// The workflow loop's lock while the step run `step_run_id` is open
    /// (`Workflows::lock_open_step`); without the workflow runs, only the step's status is read.
    /// `Invalid` with `STEP_CLOSED` once the step has ended.
    async fn hold_open_step<'a>(
        &self,
        workflows: Option<&'a crate::workflows::Workflows>,
        step_run_id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<tokio::sync::MutexGuard<'a, ()>>> {
        let closed = || Error::Invalid(crate::workflows::STEP_CLOSED.to_string());
        match workflows {
            Some(workflows) => match workflows.lock_open_step(step_run_id).await? {
                Some(one) => Ok(Some(one)),
                None => Err(closed()),
            },
            None => match self.store.get_step_run(step_run_id).await?.status {
                StepRunStatus::Running | StepRunStatus::Waiting => Ok(None),
                _ => Err(closed()),
            },
        }
    }
```

```rust
/// Refuses a default branch given with a remote in front, such as `origin/main`: worktrees and
/// deploys look the name up as a branch and add the remote themselves, so it would name a ref
/// that does not exist. The remotes are the repo's (`git remote`) and the project's own, which
/// counts even before the repo has it. Called after `check_repo`.
async fn check_default_branch(spec: &ProjectSpec) -> Result<()> {
    let Some(branch) = spec.default_branch.as_deref().filter(|b| b.contains('/')) else {
        return Ok(());
    };
    let listed = git(Path::new(spec.repo_path.trim()), &["remote"])
        .await
        .unwrap_or_default();
    let remotes = listed.lines().map(str::trim).chain([spec.remote.as_str()]);
    for remote in remotes.filter(|r| !r.is_empty()) {
        if let Some(name) = branch.strip_prefix(&format!("{remote}/")) {
            return Err(Error::Invalid(format!(
                "give the default branch without the remote: \"{name}\", not \"{branch}\""
            )));
        }
    }
```

```rust
        let project = self.store.add_project(team_id, spec).await?;
```

**with:**

```rust
        check_default_branch(spec).await?;
        let project = self.store.add_project(team_id, spec).await?;
```


**with:**

```rust
impl Workflows {
    /// The loop's lock, held while the step run `step_run_id` is open (running or waiting), for
    /// a question or a follow-up one of its runs asks for (`CheckIns`): a Stop or the step's end
    /// closes the step under the same lock, so the call cannot cross it and leave a question
    /// open or a follow-up pending in a step that has ended. `None` once the step has ended.
    pub async fn lock_open_step(
        &self,
        step_run_id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<tokio::sync::MutexGuard<'_, ()>>> {
        let one = self.lock.lock().await;
        let status = self.store.get_step_run(step_run_id).await?.status;
        Ok(matches!(status, StepRunStatus::Running | StepRunStatus::Waiting).then_some(one))
    }
```
